# Ecency native (4.0.0 spike)

Native rewrite spike for the Ecency mobile app: SwiftUI on iOS, Jetpack Compose on Android, over a shared Kotlin Multiplatform core. Tracking: #3577.

The React Native app at the repo root is unaffected. Nothing in `native/` is bundled by Metro or built by the RN CI workflows.

## Layout

```
native/
  shared/       Kotlin Multiplatform core (Android + iOS), package com.ecency.core
  androidApp/   Jetpack Compose app
  iosApp/       SwiftUI app; project.yml is the source, the .xcodeproj is generated
```

## Requirements

- JDK 17+, Android SDK 36 (`native/local.properties` with `sdk.dir=...`, not committed)
- Xcode 26+ and [XcodeGen](https://github.com/yonaskolb/XcodeGen) (`brew install xcodegen`)

## Build and test

```sh
cd native

# Everything on the Kotlin side: shared tests (Android host + iOS simulator), Android unit/UI tests, lint
./gradlew check

# Android app
./gradlew :androidApp:assembleDebug

# The shared framework for arm64 Apple targets (devices, Apple silicon simulators, the App Store archive). Run it
# after shared changes: Kotlin/Native 2.3.10 crashes there on a 5-flow combine (a suspend lambda of 5 parameters)
# while the JVM and iosX64 builds pass; NativeCompilerGuardTest keeps those out
./gradlew :shared:linkDebugFrameworkIosSimulatorArm64 :shared:linkReleaseFrameworkIosArm64

# iOS app and its tests (unit + UI)
cd iosApp && xcodegen generate
xcodebuild test -project EcencySpike.xcodeproj -scheme EcencyApp \
  -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=<your simulator>'
```

## Run on a simulator or emulator

```sh
native/scripts/ios-sim.sh                  # build, install and launch on the "Ecency Spike" iOS simulator (created on first run)
native/scripts/ios-sim.sh --test           # unit + UI tests on that simulator
native/scripts/ios-sim.sh --shot out.png   # launch, then screenshot (for UI comparisons)
native/scripts/android-emu.sh              # build, install, launch, print launch time
native/scripts/android-emu.sh --shot out.png
```

`android-emu.sh` uses a connected device, or boots the AVD named by `ECENCY_AVD` (default `Ecency_Spike_API_34`).

## Versioning

The app version lives in `gradle/libs.versions.toml` (`appVersion`, currently 4.0.0) and in `iosApp/project.yml` (`MARKETING_VERSION`). Tests fail if the apps and the core disagree. Android versionCode follows RN: `major * 10000 + minor * 100 + patch` locally (40000; RN 3.5.11 is 30511), and in store builds the build number, which CI sets to the Unix time exactly as the RN workflows did (about 1.79 billion in 2026), so every upload is above every RN store build. iOS CFBundleVersion is the build number (CI: the Unix time, as RN; 1 locally). `VersionTest` checks the Android rule. Details: [docs/release-builds.md](docs/release-builds.md).

## In-place upgrade from RN 3.x

Native 4.x ships as an update of the RN store app: same ids, same store records, same signing, so users and testers update in place and keep their accounts, PIN and settings.

| | Android | iOS |
|---|---|---|
| App id | `app.esteem.mobile.android` (Kotlin namespace and packages stay `com.ecency.*`) | `app.esteem.mobile.ios` |
| Name | Ecency | Ecency |
| Extensions | | share `app.esteem.mobile.ios.eshare`, notification service `app.esteem.mobile.ios.ImageNotifi`, App Group `group.com.ecency.eshare` (RN `ios/*/*.entitlements`) |

Both extensions use RN's ids and profiles: `EcencyShare` is `app.esteem.mobile.ios.eshare` (App Group `group.com.ecency.eshare`, also on the app; `PROVISIONING_PROFILE_SPECIFIER: $(ECENCY_SHARE_PROFILE)`), `EcencyNotificationService` is `app.esteem.mobile.ios.ImageNotifi` (`$(ECENCY_NSE_PROFILE)`, built with `ECENCY_PUSH_EXTENSION`); the release workflow installs all three RN profiles.

Links and sharing after the merges: gaps3 added RN's remaining App Link paths (`/trending`, `/hot`, `/waves`, `/waves/*`) and `/purchase`, spike/platform the Android share targets (`SEND`, `SEND_MULTIPLE`); they sit in the same activity as the identity changes (Custom Tabs and browser `<queries>`, the browser fallback for pages no screen claims).

What the update carries over, checked against RN 3.5.11 (@react-native-async-storage/async-storage 2.2.0, RN 0.79.5):
- RN data, read by `AsyncStorageReader` and imported once (see "Importing accounts from the RN app"). Android: the SQLite database `databases/RKStorage`, table `catalystLocalStorage (key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, value TEXT NOT NULL)`, the async-storage default (RN does not set `AsyncStorage_useNextStorage`, so the newer Room database is not used); RN opens it with write-ahead logging, which the reader handles while RN's rows may still sit in `RKStorage-wal`. iOS: `Library/Application Support/app.esteem.mobile.ios/RCTAsyncLocalStorage_V1/manifest.json`, values over 1024 characters as null there and in a file named md5(key); checked on a simulator holding a real RN 3.5.11 install: `persist:root`, `user` and `REACT_QUERY_OFFLINE_CACHE` are files `817e01df...`, `ee11cbb1...`, `b6fc4091...`, the other keys inline. An RN install never opened since async-storage moved its folder out of `Documents` is read from `Documents/RCTAsyncLocalStorage_V1` (or the older `RNCAsyncLocalStorage_V1`, `RCTAsyncLocalStorage`) instead. RN copies `Documents` into Application Support without deleting the old copy, so those folders can still hold old RN secrets: every RN folder other than the live one is deleted whole together with the secrets (`LegacyCleanup.scrub`: after the import mark, when the import is given up, and in the PIN wipe; `AsyncStorageReader.forApp`, `LegacyStorage.hasStaleCopies`). Tests with that layout: `AsyncStorageReaderTest` (Android, shared iOS).
- Push: the same Firebase apps (RN's `google-services.json` and `GoogleService-Info.plist`; CI decodes the existing `GOOGLE_JSON` and `GOOGLE_PLIST` secrets), so Firebase's installation id and the FCM token in the app's data stay valid; the app registers the token with Ecency again at start, as RN did. The notification permission belongs to the app id: Android's `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` grant (RN declared it through notifee and Firebase) and iOS's authorization stay. Pushes keep landing in Firebase's `fcm_fallback_notification_channel`, the channel RN's pushes used, so a user's sound and importance settings for it survive (no `default_notification_channel_id` on purpose, see the manifest). RN's media channel (`RNVIDEO_SESSION_NOTIFICATION`) was deleted by RN whenever playback stopped, so there is nothing to keep there.
- Links: the same app id, so ecency.com's `assetlinks.json` (RN's signing certificate) already verifies the Android App Links and the `apple-app-site-association` already lists the iOS app; `ecency://` and `hive://` stay registered on both platforms. RN's `esteem://` scheme is intentionally not carried over (the brand was retired years ago).
- Backups: `allowBackup=false` as RN; the RN AsyncStorage folder on iOS is excluded from backups by RN.
- Keychain (iOS): RN kept no secrets in the Keychain (no keychain library); native uses the service `app.esteem.mobile.ios.secure` in the app's default access group, which no RN item uses (Firebase's own items stay untouched). `wipeIfFreshInstall` only ever clears that service, and an update from RN has nothing there.
- Debug builds use the release id too, with no `applicationIdSuffix`: a suffix would give the app another data folder (Android) or bundle id folder (iOS), so it could never exercise the real RN import, which is the path that matters now. A debug build installs over an RN build only when both are signed with the same key (on Android RN's debug keystore differs from Gradle's default, so a debug build does not replace an RN store or dev build there).
- Devices: the shared simulator and emulator have RN 3.5.11 installed under these ids. Installing native on the simulator replaces the RN app in place (its data stays) and a normal launch imports and then scrubs the RN secrets there; UI tests run with `-fakeData`, and the unit test host never builds the environment, so neither touches RN data. On the emulator the RN build is signed with another key, so the debug install fails (`scripts/android-emu.sh` says why) unless the RN app is removed, which deletes its data. The old preview app `com.ecency.mobile` can be deleted freely.

Rollback (installing an RN 3.x build over native; owner decision 10-21: not supported, the RN secrets are scrubbed right after the import with no grace period, a user who goes back signs in again): Play never offers a lower versionCode and the App Store has no downgrade, so this only happens through an RN build uploaded later with a higher number, a sideloaded APK or an older TestFlight build. RN then finds its storage without `user`, `sc_accounts`, `auth`, the PINs and the accounts' `local` keys: no usable accounts and no PIN, but its settings in `persist:root` (theme, language, currency, servers and the rest), drafts and caches are still there. Native's data stays beside it unread: on Android the vault (`ecency.secure` preferences under a Keystore key) and the `ecency.prefs`, `ecency.ui`, `ecency.push` preferences; on iOS the Keychain service `app.esteem.mobile.ios.secure` and the UserDefaults (including the install mark). Updating to native again finds that vault as it was and its "imported" mark, so nothing is imported again: accounts signed in under RN in between are scrubbed from RN's storage without being imported (sign in again), and the native PIN is the one from before the rollback.

## Release pipeline

The RN app's GitHub Actions now build the native apps, with the RN secrets, signing and store targets
([docs/release-builds.md](docs/release-builds.md) has the secrets table and the details):

- Pull request: `test.yml` ("Lint & Unit Tests") runs `:shared:testAndroidHostTest :androidApp:testDebugUnitTest :androidApp:lintDebug` on Linux when the pull request touches `native/`, and passes without running otherwise. Its "RN Lint & Unit Tests" job runs `yarn lint`, `yarn typecheck` and `yarn test:ci` when the pull request changes anything outside `native/`. No app builds on pull requests.
- Push to `development` touching `native/`: `build-android.yml` (unit tests, secrets into `local.properties`, RN's `google-services.json`, R8 bundle signed with the RN upload key, the release launch check on an emulator, Play alpha track, mapping to Play and, in a job of its own, to Sentry) and `build-ios.yml` (secrets, RN's `GoogleService-Info.plist`, RN certificate and profiles, archive, export, the release launch check on a simulator for manual runs, upload with RN's Apple ID and app-specific password, dSYMs to Sentry in a job of its own). Sentry uploads need `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` and are skipped without it. Build numbers are the Unix time, as RN.
- By hand: both build workflows (track, notes, upload choice, build number) and `e2e-android.yml` (the Android device tests on an emulator; also on the `e2e-test` label).

Owner checklist: add `NATIVE_PIN_KEY_SHA256` and `NATIVE_DEFAULT_PIN_SHA256` (and optionally `NATIVE_SENTRY_DSN`); confirm the existing RN secrets are current (upload keystore and alias, `GOOGLE_JSON`, `GOOGLE_PLIST`, the three iOS profiles); upload no RN build after the first native one. Not needed: new store records, App IDs, profiles, IAP products, ePoints package verification or Firebase apps.

## Tests are required

Every change lands with tests:

- Shared logic: `shared/src/commonTest`, runs on Android host and iOS. Hive serialization and signing must have golden vectors generated from `@ecency/sdk`.
- Android UI: Compose tests under `androidApp/src/test` (Robolectric, no emulator needed).
- Android device: `androidApp/src/androidTest` for what Robolectric cannot do (Android Keystore), `./gradlew :androidApp:connectedDebugAndroidTest`.
- iOS: `EcencyAppTests` (XCTest) and `EcencyAppUITests` (XCUITest).
- Test data uses throwaway keys and dummy values only.

## Hive core and golden vectors

`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/hive` serializes and signs Hive transactions (vote, comment, comment_options, custom_json, transfer, delete_comment and metadata-only account_update2 so far). `GoldenVectors.kt` pins it to the existing stack:

- serialized bytes come from hived itself (`condenser_api.get_transaction_hex`) and must hash to the `@ecency/sdk` transaction id;
- signatures must equal the SDK's deterministic (RFC 6979) signature byte for byte, and the SDK's own randomised signatures must recover to the signer;
- master-password key derivation must match the SDK for every role.

Regenerate after adding an operation (run from the RN app root, which has `node_modules`):

```sh
node native/tools/golden/generate.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/hive/GoldenVectors.kt
```

All keys in the vectors derive from public throwaway seeds and control no funds.

## Importing accounts from the RN app (3.x to 4.0.0)

`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/legacy` reads what the RN app stored so users stay signed in:

- `AsyncStorageReader` (Android: SQLite `RKStorage`; iOS: `RCTAsyncLocalStorage_V1` manifest plus md5-named files)
- `LegacyImporter` decrypts the PIN from `persist:root` with the build's `PIN_KEY`, then each account's secrets from `user` (crypto-js passphrase AES with the app's stamp) with the app PIN, `DEFAULT_PIN` or the old unlock PIN, and validates every key

Fixtures come from the app's own `src/utils/crypto.ts` with dummy secrets:

```sh
node --experimental-strip-types --no-warnings native/tools/legacy/generate-fixtures.mjs \
  native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/legacy/LegacyFixtures.kt
```

The real `PIN_KEY` and `DEFAULT_PIN` are build secrets. They must come from local, untracked config or CI secrets, never from this repo (see Secure storage).

## Secure storage

`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/secure` keeps account secrets once they leave the RN app's storage.

- `SecureStore`: put, get, delete, list and wipe small secrets by entry name, plus `withLock` for read-modify-write. Every failure is a `SecureStoreException` (`KeyInvalidated`, `Corrupted`, `Unavailable`, `InvalidArgument`), including caller mistakes, because Swift only sees declared exceptions and any other Kotlin exception terminates the app. Calls block on platform I/O; UI code uses `KeyVaultAsync` (suspend functions on `Dispatchers.IO`, async in Swift).
  - Android, `AndroidKeystoreSecureStore`: AES-256-GCM under a non-exportable Android Keystore key, ciphertext in a private SharedPreferences file, entry name bound as GCM associated data. One process-wide lock per store name covers the alias check, the "never mint a key next to existing ciphertext" check, key creation and every read and write, so separate instances cannot race. If the key is gone while ciphertext remains (restore onto a new device, Keystore reset) calls throw `KeyInvalidated`; `UnrecoverableKeyException` is `Unavailable` because it is not always permanent. Nothing wipes automatically. `KeyVault` reports the lost key from whichever call met it first (`WatchedSecureStore`, `KeyVault.onKeyInvalidated`) and `StorageResetModel` (`env.storageReset`) opens a blocking prompt, "Secure storage was reset on this device. Sign in again.", in a window above the lock cover, the PIN pad and every sheet (no back, no tap outside; no root prompt opens under it). Only its confirmation wipes: the same path as "forgot PIN" (`PinLock.wipeAll`, `resetAfterWipe`), then `AppEvent.SignInAgain` opens the login screen. A wipe the store refused keeps the prompt with an error for another try. `Unavailable` and `Corrupted` never open it. `StorageResetModelTest`, `LockFlowsTest` (removing the hook, reporting every storage failure or dropping the confirmation guard fails them).
  - iOS, `KeychainSecureStore`: one `kSecClassGenericPassword` item per entry, service `<bundle id>.secure`, `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`, never synchronizable, one process-wide lock per service. Keychain items survive an uninstall, so the app must call `wipeIfFreshInstall()` at launch before reading the vault (a UserDefaults mark, which an uninstall removes and an update from the RN app keeps). iOS calls it at launch (`AppContainer.make`). The Keychain has no separate device key to lose: this store reports every failure as `Unavailable` and never `KeyInvalidated`, so iOS has no "sign in again" prompt (it would be dead UI) and never wipes on a storage error.
- `KeyVault`: accounts on top of a `SecureStore`, one entry per username with auth type, posting, active and memo keys (never the owner key, never the master password: see "What the app stores" below), access token and HiveAuth key, token and expiry, plus the current account, PIN lock flag and migration mark. `VaultAccount.toString()` is redacted. Invalid usernames read as absent and are rejected on save with `InvalidArgument`; an unreadable account is skipped by `accounts()` and listed by `unreadableUsernames()`; unreadable meta reads as defaults; `remove()` clears the current account before deleting.
- `LegacyMigration.run(vault, storage, pinKey, defaultPin)`: RN accounts into the vault, once, then the RN secrets out of the RN storage. Both apps pass a `LegacyMigrationSource` (RN storage, `PIN_KEY`, `DEFAULT_PIN`) to `AppEnvironment.create`, and `AppLockModel.start()` runs the migration to completion before it reads the lock state, so the first launch after the update never opens unlocked. RN storage that cannot be read now is retried on the next launch.
  - Every field is decrypted with the app PIN, then DEFAULT_PIN, then the user's old PIN from `application.encUnlockPin` (the RN app leaves secrets under all three).
  - Accounts are keyed by username and an account already in the vault is never overwritten. Every migrated account is read back before the "imported" mark is written, and the mark is the last vault write: a run killed at any write completes on the next launch, a finished run never repeats.
  - Only provably absent RN storage counts as done. Storage that exists but cannot be read (`StorageUnreadable`), a wrong `PIN_KEY` (`PinUnavailable`), malformed data or a failed read-back changes nothing and is retried later.
  - The editor's unsaved RN drafts come along once, with the settings import (`LegacyEditorDrafts`, `SettingsModel.importLegacy`): RN autosaves into the persisted redux `cache.draftsCollection` in `persist:root`, the new post under `DEFAULT_USER_DRAFT_ID_<username>` and an opened server draft under its id, kept 7 days. Unexpired ones with text become the account's `LocalDrafts.NEW_POST` or `draft(id)` slot (title, body, comma tags, updated, description, thumbnail, reward) unless that slot already holds something. Dropped: the poll and beneficiaries in the draft's meta, reply autosaves (`replyCache`), ids with a `/` (wave and reply autosaves of RN builds that never ran its migration 14, such as `<author>/ecency.waves`), and RN's old `draft` key (storage.ts `setDraftPost`), which nothing in RN 3.x writes any more. `LegacyEditorDraftsTest`, `SettingsModelTest.rnEditorDraftsComeWithTheSettingsImport`.
  - A failure a retry cannot fix (`PinUnavailable`: the build's `PIN_KEY` does not open this data; `Malformed`) is not silent: `AppLockModel` counts it in the prefs (`legacy.importFailures`, `legacy.importFailureKind`), writes a warning line (`AppLog`: logcat tag `Ecency`, iOS NSLog; kind and count only), leaves a crash reporter breadcrumb (`legacy`, "RN import failed", kind, attempts, abandoned), emits `AppEvent.LegacyImportFailed(kind, attempts, abandoned)`, and tells the user once "We could not bring over your accounts from the previous version of the app. Please log in again." After 3 such launches (`IMPORT_ATTEMPTS`) it gives up (`LegacyMigration.abandon`): the import is marked done and the RN secrets are deleted, since nothing can use them and going back to RN is not supported. Unreadable storage and failed read-backs stay silent retries. `AppLockModelTest.anRnImportTheBuildCannotOpenIsToldOnceThenGivenUp`.
  - After the mark, `LegacyCleanup.scrub` deletes `user`, `sc_accounts`, `auth` (unsalted PIN hash) and, inside `persist:root`, the encrypted PINs and each account's `local` copy of its keys. Settings and drafts stay. A failed cleanup is retried on the next run. Fields that none of the three PINs decrypt are reported and then deleted with the rest; they were unrecoverable anyway.

### Keys are not PIN-encrypted again inside the vault

The RN app encrypted keys with the PIN because AsyncStorage had no other protection. In the native app the device key (Keystore, Keychain) is the at-rest protection, and the PIN stays an app lock. We recommend not adding a PIN layer on top:

- A PIN is 4 to 6 digits. Anyone who already got past the Keystore or Keychain can try all PINs offline in seconds, and a WIF checksum tells them when they hit, so the extra layer adds almost no strength.
- It costs real UX and failure modes: every PIN change re-encrypts every key, a forgotten PIN loses every key, and the RN build secrets would stay permanent dependencies instead of one-time migration inputs.
- Stronger options exist when wanted: biometric or device-credential bound keys (`setUserAuthenticationRequired`, `kSecAccessControlUserPresence`) for the active key, enforced by the hardware rather than by our code.

Decided: the PIN is an app lock (see App lock below); active/owner signing behind biometrics stays optional.

### App lock (PIN)

`secure/PinLock` is the RN app's PIN lock without the RN weaknesses; `app/AppLockModel` drives both UIs.

```kotlin
val lock = env.pinLock                   // blocking; the model calls it on AppDispatchers.io
lock.setPin("1234")                      // first PIN only, turns the lock on (4 digits, else InvalidArgument)
lock.verify(pin)                         // PinCheck.Success | Wrong(failures, remaining) | Wiped | NoPin | BiometricChanged
lock.changePin(old, new); lock.disable(pin); lock.setBiometricEnabled(pin, on) { binder.bind() }   // each checks the PIN like verify
lock.unlockWithBiometric(binding)        // after the platform prompt succeeded
lock.wipeAll()                           // "forgot PIN"
env.appLock.biometricBinder = ...        // set by each app before start()
env.appLock.start(); onBackground(); onForeground(); enterDigit(d); deleteDigit(); forgotPin()
env.appLock.biometricSucceeded(binding); biometricInvalidated(); consumeAutoBiometric()
env.appLock.startEnable() / startDisable() / startChange() / startBiometric(on) / cancel()
```

- Verifier: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 through the platform (CommonCrypto `CCKeyDerivationPBKDF` on iOS, `SecretKeyFactory` `PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256` on Android 8+; common Kotlin over kotlincrypto SHA-256 on Android 7 and in JVM host tests; RFC 7914 and hashlib vectors pass on every implementation and a cross-check compares them), 16-byte random salt, 100,000 rounds stored with the hash, constant-time compare, entry `pin.verifier`. The PIN itself is never stored or logged. With only 10,000 possible PINs the hash cannot stop an offline search of a stolen verifier; the device key of the store and the counter are the protection.
- Counter (`pin.failures`): written before the PIN is checked and reset only by a correct PIN or a biometric unlock, so killing the app mid-check or restarting it never gives attempts back. A missing or unreadable counter next to a verifier counts as tampering and leaves one attempt. `MAX_ATTEMPTS` = 3, as RN: the third wrong PIN in a row (in any flow: unlock, change, turn off, biometric toggle) wipes the vault, marks the RN migration done so no RN copy comes back, deletes the RN copies of the secrets (`LegacyCleanup.scrub`; the next start retries one that failed), clears session, votes and cached posts, and emits `AppEvent.DataWiped` then `SessionChanged(null)`; the UIs return to the signed-out app with a notice. A wipe that failed (store unavailable) is finished before any later check.
- On or off: the stored verifier alone decides. Unreadable vault meta never turns the lock off (fails closed). A verifier entry that exists but cannot be read or decoded keeps the lock on too: no PIN matches it, wrong PINs count as usual, biometrics do not unlock (biometric unlock goes off), and "forgot PIN" (a wipe) is the way out, the same stance as an unreadable counter. A PIN change verifies the current PIN once and keeps only a "verified" flag (never the PIN) through the new-PIN steps; a relock ends it.
- `AppLockModel.start()` runs once per process (the environment outlives activities and scenes): Android rotation or any activity recreation calls it again and nothing changes, an unlocked app stays unlocked and no biometric prompt fires. Only a start that failed (lock cover with Retry) runs again.
- Lock: at start when the lock is on and any account is stored on the device (signed in or not), and when the app was in the background for 60 s or more (RN `_startPinCodeTimer`), timed on a monotonic clock that counts sleep (Android `elapsedRealtime`, Darwin `CLOCK_MONOTONIC_RAW`); a clock that went backwards locks. The relock prompt is set synchronously from memory (content is hidden in the same frame) and the failure count is refreshed afterwards. A settings flow's result that lands after a relock never replaces the lock prompt, and no second PIN check starts while one is running. Nothing private is composed until the lock state is read or while locked.
- Screenshots, recording and the app switcher while the lock is on:
  - Android 13+: the app is left out of the recents thumbnail (`setRecentsScreenshotEnabled(false)`); screenshots and screen recording stay allowed, as in the RN app.
  - Android 7 to 12: there is no recents-only switch, so `FLAG_SECURE` is set while the lock is on (cleared when it goes off). It also blocks screenshots and screen recording of the app.
  - iOS: the lock screen, the settings PIN flows, lock notices and the app switcher cover live in their own `UIWindow` at `.alert + 1`, above every sheet, Safari view and alert of the app window. The cover goes up synchronously on `UIScene.willDeactivateNotification` (before the snapshot) and comes down on activation only after the model decided, so a relock goes straight from cover to PIN pad. Screenshots and screen recording are not blocked (iOS has no supported switch for it).
- Biometrics (Android `BiometricPrompt` with `BIOMETRIC_STRONG`, iOS `LAContext` `.deviceOwnerAuthenticationWithBiometrics`, no passcode fallback): on the lock screen and on the PIN confirmation before something sensitive (`verifyPin`: the keys screen, a key or sign-in proof for another app; RN's PINCODE screen runs its biometric prompt for both), never in a settings flow (turn off, change, biometric toggle), only when turned on with the PIN. Success unlocks and resets the counter like a correct PIN; failure or cancel leaves the PIN pad and the counter as they were; an exhausted counter wipes instead of unlocking. The prompt starts by itself once per lock (cold start, relock), never again after a wrong PIN or a cancel.
- Enrollment changes: turning biometric unlock on binds it (`BiometricBinder`, called only after the PIN was right). Android creates a Keystore key with `setUserAuthenticationRequired(true)` and `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(true)` and runs the prompt with a `CryptoObject` over it; iOS stores `evaluatedPolicyDomainState` and compares it after each prompt. An invalidated key or a different domain state turns biometric unlock off (`PinCheck.BiometricChanged`), shows a notice and asks for the PIN; the user can turn it on again. A pending wipe still wins over any biometric result.
- Signing out the last account turns the lock off, as RN's logout does.
- Migration: the RN unlock PIN (`application.encUnlockPin`, else `application.pin`, decrypted with `PIN_KEY` exactly as `_verifyPinCode` does) becomes the verifier when RN had `isPinCodeOpen` on, so users keep their PIN. Without a usable 4-digit PIN the lock stays off, `LegacyMigrationReport.pinLockDropped` is true and the app shows a one-time notice asking to set a PIN again. RN's `application.isBiometricEnabled` carries over unbound: biometrics are not offered until the first correct PIN binds them to the biometrics enrolled then.
- Wipe: the "migration done" mark is written even when the wipe failed part way (Android: ciphertext cleared, Keystore key delete failed; the key delete is best effort), so RN data, including the RN PIN, is never imported again.
- Debug only: Android `adb shell am start -n app.esteem.mobile.android/com.ecency.app.MainActivity --ez lockDemo true`; iOS `-fakeData YES -lockDemo YES` (`-keepFakeStore YES` keeps that store across launches, `-lockTimeoutSeconds 0` relocks on every return). Both seed a throwaway account with PIN 1234. The Android `demo` extra (renderer demo, which skips the app and its lock) is honoured in debug builds only.
- Errors: a storage failure during a PIN or biometric check shows on the PIN pad itself (`AppLockState.promptError`), since no alert can show while locked; the next check clears it. Outside a PIN pad it is an alert as before.
- Android biometric key: only a `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException` or a missing key when preparing the prompt turns biometric unlock off (with a notice, PIN asked). Other Keystore failures (`UnrecoverableKeyException`, other `InvalidKeyException`s) may be transient: the PIN is asked this time and the setting stays on. Whether biometrics are available is read again on every resume.
- iOS overlay: while a PIN pad shows, the overlay's view is `accessibilityViewIsModal`, and when it hides the key window goes back to the window that had it.
- Accessibility: Dynamic Type on the iOS PIN screen (`scaledFont`, `@ScaledMetric` dots); errors and the digit count are announced (iOS `UIAccessibility` announcements, Android live regions).

Tests: `PinLockTest`, `Pbkdf2Test`, `AppLockModelTest`, the PIN cases of `LegacyMigrationTest` / `LegacyImporterTest` (shared), `LockFlowsTest` and `LockPlatformTest` (Robolectric: clock provider, FLAG_SECURE policy), `Pbkdf2DeviceTest` (instrumented), `PinLockKeychainTests` and `LockOverlayTests` (XCTest) and `LockUITests` (XCUITest). Removing the counter's persistence, the wipe, the counter's negative-value guard, the single-check guard or the `elapsedRealtime` clock fails them.

### Threat model

- Protected: a copied app data directory, a device backup or a restore onto another device (`allowBackup=false`, the secure store and RN storage excluded from Android 12+ device transfer, Keystore keys and `ThisDeviceOnly` items do not travel), other apps (sandbox), iCloud Keychain sync, secrets in logs, crash reports or build logs (redacted `toString`, entry names are never secrets, build secrets never become Xcode build settings).
- Not protected: a rooted or jailbroken device with the app running (it can also roll the stored attempt counter back), malware with the user's unlocked session, someone holding the unlocked phone (the app lock is the only barrier there), a compromised build.
- `PIN_KEY` and `DEFAULT_PIN` are not real secrets against anyone with the RN or native binary; both are extractable. They only have to stay out of this public repo and out of logs.

### RN build secrets in native builds

`PIN_KEY` and `DEFAULT_PIN` are the RN app's values (`.env`, repository secrets `PIN_KEY` and `DEFAULT_PIN`). Both apps read them at build time from the untracked `native/local.properties`, parsed the same way on both sides (raw text after the first `=`, trimmed, no escapes):

```properties
ecency.pinKey=...
ecency.pinKeySha256=<sha-256 hex of the value>
ecency.defaultPin=...
ecency.defaultPinSha256=<sha-256 hex of the value>
```

- Android (`BuildConfig.PIN_KEY`, `BuildConfig.DEFAULT_PIN`) also takes `ECENCY_PIN_KEY`, `ECENCY_PIN_KEY_SHA256`, `ECENCY_DEFAULT_PIN`, `ECENCY_DEFAULT_PIN_SHA256` from the environment, which wins over the file.
- iOS (`BuildSecrets.pinKey`, `.defaultPin`) reads the file only: the "Build secrets" phase (`iosApp/scripts/build-secrets.sh`) writes them as byte arrays into the gitignored `iosApp/Generated/BuildSecrets.generated.swift`. They are never build settings, so xcodebuild cannot print them; a Debug build with canary values had zero hits in its output and its `.xcactivitylog`. Do not export these names in the shell that runs xcodebuild, since Xcode imports the environment into build settings.
- CI: write those lines into `native/local.properties` from the secrets in a step of their own (`printf` with `${{ secrets.* }}`, no `set -x`), then build.
- Debug builds fall back to dummies. Every other build type or configuration (any non-debuggable Android variant, any Xcode configuration other than `Debug`) fails before packaging unless both values are set and match their SHA-256, so a missing, truncated or wrong value cannot ship.

### Device tests

The host tests use an in-memory store (`commonTest/.../secure/InMemorySecureStore.kt`) and an in-memory RN storage (`commonTest/.../legacy/MapLegacyStorage.kt`). The real Keystore and Keychain need a device:

```sh
cd native
./gradlew :androidApp:connectedDebugAndroidTest    # emulator or device attached (scripts/android-emu.sh boots one)
scripts/ios-sim.sh --test                          # XCTest in the app host, then:
xcrun xcresulttool get test-results summary --path "$(ls -td build/ios-dd/Logs/Test/*.xcresult | head -1)"
```

`ios-sim.sh` signs ad hoc ("Sign to Run Locally"): the simulator Keychain refuses unsigned apps with `-34018`.

## Authentication core

`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/auth` ports the RN app's sign-in, session and signing logic (no UI). Golden vectors come from the RN stack (`@ecency/sdk`, crypto-js, hive-uri, `operations.json`, Node's URL); regenerate from the RN app root with `node native/tools/golden/generate-auth.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/auth/AuthGoldenVectors.kt`. Nothing in the tests talks to the network.

- `KeyLogin`: username plus master password or WIF, classified against `get_accounts` key auths (`ChainAuthorities`, `RpcChainAccounts`). Auth type and stored keys follow RN `auth.ts`; stricter on memo-only keys (`MemoKeyOnly`), malformed WIFs (`InvalidKey`), and it keeps every role a key matched. Typed failures are `AuthException`. A master password is only an input: the posting, active and memo keys it derives are kept when the chain has them, the password and the owner key are not; a secret that only gives the owner key is refused (`OwnerKeyOnly`).
- `AccountManager` over `AccountStore` (`VaultAccountStore`: the `KeyVault` plus an `auth.session.<user>` entry for refresh token and expiry): key, HiveSigner code, web `/auth` code, auth-transfer and HiveAuth logins, switch, remove, logout, `hasAnyAccount()` (counts unreadable entries too; the app lock applies while it is true), RN's refresh rule (`refreshSession`) and `ensureKeySession` for key accounts without a token. Links never plant credentials: auth-transfer tokens are redeemed at the token endpoint and web codes must be signed for, and exchange to, the link's account; a stored key account keeps its keys and only takes the new token (`storedAccount` lets the UI confirm first).
- Ecency session: `HiveSignerMessages.code` signs the HiveSigner-style code locally (byte-identical JSON to RN, timestamp printed like JS), `EcencyTokenApi` exchanges codes and refresh tokens at `/auth-api/hs-token-refresh` with RN's retries, `EcencyPrivateApi` attaches `code` to private API calls.
- HiveSigner: `HiveSigner.authorizeUrl`, `parseCallback`, `hotSignUrl`, `parseSignResult` (only navigations to the redirect URI or HiveSigner's own success page count), `HiveSignerApi` (token broadcast, `me`). Decision: the native apps keep RN's app id `ecency.app` and the loopback redirect `http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth` (`HiveSignerConfig.RN`), read off the navigation in an in-app WebView, so no new HiveSigner registration is needed.
- HiveAuth: `HiveAuthClient` (auth and sign requests over `HasSocket`, Ktor websockets in production), crypto-js compatible `CryptoJsCipher`, `has://` links, relay expiry, reattach after a dropped socket.
- Links: `HiveUri` (hive-uri port), `HiveUriTx.format` (RN getFormattedTx), `AuthLinks.parse` for `ecency://auth-transfer|auth-request|login|transfer`, `hive://` and `ecency://sign/`, and ecency.com `/auth` and `/signup`; `callbackUrl` matches `URLSearchParams.set`.
- Signing: `OperationAuthority` (SDK map, RN hive-uri rule, HiveAuth key type), `TempActiveKey` (single use, 60 s, bound to the account it was entered for), `SigningRouter` (key, HiveSigner token or page, HiveAuth, auth upgrade through `SigningBackends`; one upgrade sheet per account and authority). Owner operations are always refused, from links and in the app: no owner key is stored, and `SigningRouter` ends an owner operation before any key or prompt (`SigningBackends.confirmOwnerSigning` is no longer asked). Every callback a link carries passes `AuthLinks.isAcceptableCallback` in the parser.
- Open items for later waves: import the RN app's HiveSigner refresh tokens (`sc_accounts`) into `auth.session.<user>` during the RN migration (today migrated accounts keep their token until it expires); binary serializers for the hive:// operations beyond vote, comment, comment_options, custom_json and transfer.

## Markdown (remarkable port)

`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render/markdown` is a pure Kotlin port of remarkable 2.0.1 plus the parts of Autolinker 3.16.2 its linkify plugin uses, configured exactly as `@ecency/render-helper` configures it. `Markdown.render(src)` must be byte-identical to the JS output; JS is only the offline oracle.

Two generators, both from the pinned packages in `tools/render` (`npm ci` there first):

```sh
cd native/tools/render
node generate-markdown-data.mjs       # Entities.kt + AutolinkerData.kt (entity table, TLDs, URL character classes)
node generate-markdown-fixtures.mjs   # commonTest/.../markdown/MarkdownFixtures.kt (golden cases + 200 KB document hash)
```

Rerun both after bumping remarkable, autolinker or render-helper. Fixture inputs are synthetic only. `MarkdownTest` compares every case exactly; known differences go in its `allowedDeviations` with a reason (currently none).

## Post HTML: DOM and sanitizer

`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render/dom` and `.../render/sanitize` port the HTML layer of `@ecency/render-helper` 2.5.42 to pure Kotlin: the xmldom 0.9.12 `text/html` parser and serializer (a small mutable DOM the post transforms run on), the htmlparser2 + dom-serializer repair path render-helper takes when xmldom rejects a body, and `sanitizeHtml` (xss 1.0.15 with render-helper's whitelist and hooks). `RenderBodyParser` mirrors the parse step of `markdownToHTML`, including its fallbacks.

Golden fixtures come from the JS packages themselves (synthetic inputs only, plus seeded random combinations):

```sh
cd native/tools/render && npm ci
node generate-dom-fixtures.mjs ../../shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render/dom
# entity tables used by the port (only when the oracle packages change)
node generate-entity-tables.mjs ../../shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render/dom/EntityTables.kt
```

For a local fuzzing run, raise the random case counts (keep each run under about 5,000 cases or the JVM fixture class gets too large) and regenerate the committed set afterwards:

```sh
RANDOM_COUNT=1500 SOUP_COUNT=3000 SOUP_LEN=80 EXTRA_FUZZ=2000 SEED=99 node generate-dom-fixtures.mjs ../../shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render/dom
```

### Deliberate deviations from the oracle

Everything above is compared byte for byte; the only exceptions are listed in code with a reason, and the tests fail if a listed case stops differing:

- **Iframe host allow list (security).** The sanitizer drops an iframe `src` unless the embed allow list (`EmbedSrc.isAllowedEmbedSrc`, judged on the `https:` form so protocol-relative and `http:` sources of allowed hosts keep working) accepts it, and drops a `sandbox` holding any token beyond the ones render-helper's `iframe()` transform grants (`allow-scripts`, `allow-same-origin`, `allow-popups`). render-helper only enforces the allow list inside the DOM transform, which its sanitize-only fallback never runs. Cases: `DomGoldenTest.securityDeviations` and `TransformDeviations.security` (whole-pipeline goldens); `RenderCorpusTest` counts a post as this deviation only when dropping exactly the off-list iframe sources and their "Unsupported" placeholders from the oracle output gives the Kotlin output. Every rendered iframe is also checked against `IframeInvariant`. Upstream write-up: [docs/upstream-render-helper-issues.md](docs/upstream-render-helper-issues.md).
- **Very deep nesting (robustness).** Past a few thousand nested elements dom-serializer overflows the V8 stack and render-helper falls back to sanitize-only output; the Kotlin repair is iterative and returns the repaired, fully closed body (`DomGoldenTest.deepNestingPastTheJsStackIsRepaired`).

### URLs and IDNA

`render/url/WebUrl` is the WHATWG URL parser used everywhere in the renderer, the embed allow list included. Its host step (`Idna`) ports the IDNA code of the Node that produces the goldens (Ada: UTS 46 mapping, NFC, Punycode, CheckBidi and CheckJoiners as Ada applies them) over Ada's own tables:

```sh
cd native/tools/render
node generate-idna-tables.mjs ../../shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render/url/IdnaTables.kt   # when Node's Ada version changes
node generate-url-fixtures.mjs ../../shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render/url/UrlFixtures.kt
## Data layer

`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/api` reads Hive and broadcasts votes and comments, mirroring the RPC calls `@ecency/sdk` makes for the RN app. Create one `EcencyApi` and share it:

```kotlin
val ecency = EcencyApi()                                  // HiveNodes.DEFAULT over Ktor (OkHttp / Darwin)

// Feeds: bridge.get_ranked_posts / bridge.get_account_posts, bridge order kept
val pager = ecency.feeds.pager(FeedSource.Ranked(RankedSort.TRENDING, tag = "hive-125125"), observer = user)
pager.loadMore()                                          // next page, duplicates dropped; pager.isEndReached
ecency.feeds.page(FeedSource.Account("alice", AccountPostsSort.FEED), cursor, observer)  // "friends" feed
FeedSource.myCommunities()                                // the app's communities tab (needs an observer)

// Post and comments: bridge.get_post / bridge.get_discussion, cached for 60 s
ecency.posts.getPost("alice", "hello-world", observer = user)          // null if it does not exist
val d = ecency.posts.getDiscussion("alice", "hello-world", user, CommentSort.TRENDING, botAuthors)
d.threads            // top-level comments (pinned reply first), each with replies flattened to level 3
d.threadFrom(key)    // "more replies" below a cut node

// Accounts: condenser_api.get_accounts / bridge.get_profile
ecency.accounts.getAccount("alice"); ecency.accounts.getProfile("alice")

// Broadcast (posting key): vote, reply (+ comment_options for payout type / beneficiaries)
ecency.broadcaster.vote(user, "alice", "hello-world", 10000, postingKey)
val draft = Comments.reply(user, parent.author, parent.permlink, body, parent.metadata.tags, nowMillis)
ecency.broadcaster.comment(draft, postingKey)
ecency.posts.addLocalComment(rootKey, localPost)          // on top until the bridge indexes it (5 min max)
```

Retrying a broadcast: on `BroadcastException.Network` the transaction may have landed. Retry with `e.pending.send()`, or call the same `vote(...)` / `comment(draft)` again with the same arguments or the SAME draft: the same signed transaction is re-sent (Hive dedupes it), and once it is too close to expiry it is re-signed only after the chain said it did not land (`transaction_status_api.find_transaction`; a reply is also looked up with `get_content`; an "identical vote" answer after a re-sign counts as success). When the chain cannot tell yet, nothing is re-signed and `BroadcastException.Network.undecided` asks the user to check their wallet (see Node failover). Never build a new draft to retry a reply: it gets a new permlink and can post twice. Reply permlinks follow the app's `re-<author>-<date>t<time>z` shape but are zero-padded and UTC (the app's unpadded form collides, e.g. Jan 11 and Nov 1).

From Swift, every data-layer entry point is `@Throws(EcencyException, CancellationException)`, so failures arrive as thrown errors whose `userInfo["KotlinException"]` is the Kotlin exception (`EcencyAppTests/DataLayerErrorTests.swift`). Bad input (blank reply, malformed key, out-of-range vote weight) is an `InvalidInputException` or `BroadcastException.InvalidOperation`, never a crash.

Errors: reads throw `HiveApiException` subclasses (`RpcErrorException`, `AllNodesFailedException` with one failure per attempt, ...); broadcasts throw `BroadcastException` (`DuplicateVote`, `InsufficientResourceCredits`, `MissingAuthority`, `CommentTooSoon`, `PaidOut`, `Network`, `Rejected`).

### Node failover

The same rules as @ecency/sdk 2.4.26 (hive-tx `callRPC`, `callRPCBroadcast`, `NodeHealthTracker`) with the RN app's settings (10 s read timeout, everything else the SDK default). `NodeFailoverTest`, `HiveRpcClientTest`.

- **Pool**: `HiveNodeSettings.start()` (both apps call it at launch) puts the saved server (`settings.server`, RN `settings.server`) in front of the built-in `SERVER_LIST` at once, then replaces the list with `https://ecency.com/public-nodes.json` (`hived`, else the body as a list; the built-in list when unreadable). A saved server already in the list keeps its place. `BLOCKED_SERVERS` and non-http(s) entries never enter the pool, a saved one included. A saved, picked or RN-imported server must also pass the published list's rule (`HiveNodes.userServer`: https, no user or password); an unsafe saved value reads as none (the default pool). Demo runs keep their offline node.
- **Order**: every call orders the pool by health. Unhealthy nodes go to the back (never out): rate limited (a 429's `Retry-After`, else 10 s doubling to 60 s while 429s keep coming), 3 failures in a row within 30 s, an API cooling down on the node (2 failures of that API within 30 s, or one answer the caller's validator rejected: 60 s, not lifted by a success), or a head block more than 30 behind the median seen in the last 2 minutes. Healthy nodes are ranked by an EWMA of their round trips once they have 3 fresh samples (pool order until then); one ordering a minute puts a node not sampled for a minute first, so a recovered node climbs back. "Sticky" is by measurement: the node that keeps answering fast stays first.
- **Reads**: up to 6 attempts (SDK `retry` 5), each on the healthiest node not yet tried; when all were tried a second round starts (a pool of one node is asked again). No new attempt after 2 x the timeout (20 s); 50 to 100 ms between attempts. A node measured on a method gets min(10 s, max(2 s, 4 x its EWMA for that method)); an explicit `timeoutMillis` is exact. Fail over on network errors, timeouts, HTTP 429/5xx, a proxy's non-RPC 4xx page, a body that is not a JSON-RPC reply to the request, `validate` rejecting the answer (`get_accounts` must be a list), and node-level JSON-RPC errors (-32603, -32601, -32000..-32099, sick -32602). Any other JSON-RPC error is an answer and is thrown at once, never retried.
- **Broadcasts**: `condenser_api.broadcast_transaction`, TaPoS 3 blocks behind head, 60 s expiration, 15 s per attempt, health-ordered, each node at most once (no second round), never after a chain rejection. Every node gets the SAME signed transaction, so Hive dedupes it by id; "Duplicate transaction" is success. Failures where the request never reached a node (connection refused, DNS, HTTP 429/5xx, an HTML page) fail over freely; after one where it may have (a timeout, any other network error) only until expiration minus 20 s, and that uncertain failure is what is thrown. Broadcasts give no latency samples.
- **Retrying after an uncertain send never broadcasts twice** (key accounts: the app signs, so it can re-send the same transaction). A retry re-sends the same signed transaction while it can still be accepted. After an uncertain attempt (a timeout, a broken connection), a later node's rejection other than "Duplicate transaction" (a lagging node's TaPoS assert, say) is no verdict: the send stays uncertain. A new transaction is signed only after the chain said the earlier one did not land, both when the earlier one is past its failover window and when a resend is answered "expired" or with a TaPoS error: `transaction_status_api.find_transaction` must answer `expired_irreversible` (only that is final, as in hive-tx `checkStatus`). `within_mempool` / `within_*_block`, or a reply found with `get_content`, is success with the earlier id. Anything else signs and sends nothing: the send fails with `BroadcastException.Network(undecided = true)` ("This may already have gone through. Please check your wallet or history before trying again."; a retry asks the chain again). It ends as `BroadcastException.Unconfirmed` ("Could not confirm whether this went through... start it again only if it is not there", not retryable, the action then never sends again for an hour) only once the transaction can no longer be valid by the chain's own clock: `too_old`, or the chain's head time past its expiration plus 10 minutes. The device clock never decides it. `BroadcastTest`.
- **HiveSigner and HiveAuth accounts** are signed elsewhere: a retry there is a new signed request, as in RN; the guarantee above does not reach them.
- **What counts as a retry**: `SigningModel.broadcast(user, ops, authority, actionId)` names one user action (an id made once when the user confirms, e.g. `env.idempotencyKey()`); the key is account, action id and operations, and only a call with that key resumes it, however late. A second, deliberate transfer of the same amount is its own transaction. Hive links (`hive://sign/...`) get one id per link; the debug demo transfer makes one per tap. A key account's broadcast without an action id is refused (`BroadcastException.InvalidOperation`) unless every operation is repeat-safe (`RepeatSafeOps`: vote, comment, comment_options, delete_comment, account_update(2), posting-only follow/reblog/community/notify/ecency_notify/polls custom_json). Those are keyed by their operations, and only for them does a key long past its transaction's expiration start a new transaction.
- **Persisted since the wallet wave** (see Wallet, Restart journal); the original design note: pending and undecided sends live in memory. After a restart, a retry is a new action (the screen's action id is gone), so an undecided transfer could be sent again by a user who ignores the "check your wallet" message. Closing that needs two pieces that belong with the wallet wave: (1) a small journal of undecided sends per account (tx id, expiration, action id, account; no signature, key or operation body), written when a send turns uncertain and removed when it settles, resolved with `find_transaction` at the next launch; (2) a place in the UI to show what it found ("your transfer of ... went through" / "could not confirm") and screens that keep their action id across a restart. Built without (2), the journal would decide things nobody sees, so it waits for the wallet screens.

- **Broadcast deviations from the SDK** (deliberate, all safe because the same signed transaction is re-sent): the SDK fails over only on pre-connection errors, HTTP 429/5xx, an unparseable body and (within the window) a timeout; it throws at once on a 200 with neither result nor error, a reply with the wrong id, and other network errors such as a connection broken mid-request. Native fails over on those too: the first two as answers that never reached the RPC layer, any network error inside the expiration window as an uncertain send. So native fails over more often than RN, never with a different transaction, and the window keeps a later node from answering "expired" for a transaction an earlier one took. A cancellation the HTTP engine raises by itself (not the caller's) is a network error like any other.

- **Settings** (`SettingsModel.selectServer`, see Settings): `env.hiveNodes.servers()` (the picker's list, RN useGetServersQuery), `check(server)` and `select(server, list)`: `get_dynamic_global_properties` on that node alone within 5 s, alive when its chain time is less than 15 s behind the device (`ServerCheck.OK`, `UNREACHABLE`, `STALE` for RN's three toasts); on OK it is saved and the pool becomes the server followed by the rest of the list. RN differences kept out on purpose: RN also re-runs `checkClient`, which can replace the fetched list with the built-in one, and on a stale server leaves its spinner on. RN's saved `settings.server` is imported once (see Settings).
- The published list must give https nodes without user info (any port); a 429's `Retry-After` counts for at most a day; a reply's `jsonrpc` must be the string "2.0" and its `id` the number sent. The tracker's state is behind a platform lock (ReentrantLock / NSLock).
- Not ported: hedged reads (off by default in the SDK and in RN), the server-side read-through proxy (Node only), `callREST` (native makes no REST node calls).
- Every attempt is bounded by the client's own timeout: OkHttp's read/write timeouts are disabled and NSURLSession's are set to 60 s, so they never fire first. Cancelling the calling coroutine (an outer `withTimeout` included) cancels the HTTP request and is reported as cancellation, not as a node failure, and nothing is recorded against the node.
- Response bodies over 32 MB, or nested deeper than 256 levels, are treated as a node failure; json_metadata nested that deep reads as empty. (kotlinx-serialization parses recursively, so a few thousand brackets on chain would otherwise overflow the stack.)

### Tests

All tests run against a fake `RpcTransport` (`commonTest/.../api/FakeTransport.kt`) with synthetic fixtures. Broadcast tests replay the golden-vector chain state, so the asserted request bytes carry the SDK's own signatures. One optional read-only smoke test hits real nodes (Android host only, never broadcasts):

```sh
ECENCY_LIVE_TEST=1 ./gradlew :shared:testAndroidHostTest --tests '*LiveSmokeTest*'
```
## Post HTML to blocks

`render/blocks/BlockBuilder.kt` turns the final post HTML into the native block model (mapping in `docs/post-rendering.md`). Test inputs and the demo post are real render-helper output for synthetic markdown:

```sh
cd native/tools/render && npm ci
node generate-block-inputs.mjs   # commonTest/.../blocks/BlockInputs.kt (+ expected words), SampleHtmlWords.kt and commonMain/.../blocks/SampleHtml.kt
node block-words.mjs corpus      # local only: expected words for the corpus (htmlparser2 oracle)
```

With the local corpus in `tools/render/corpus/expected.jsonl`, `./gradlew :shared:testAndroidHostTest --tests '*BlockCorpusTest'` reports crashes, text loss, unhandled tags and classes, and timing.

## App layer

`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/app` is the presentation layer both UIs sit on: state holders with immutable state, so Compose and SwiftUI only draw and forward taps. Create one `AppEnvironment` per process and get every model from it.

```kotlin
val env = AppEnvironment.create(AndroidKeystoreSecureStore(context), debugKeyImport = BuildConfig.DEBUG)
env.session.start()                                   // reads the current account from the vault
val feed = env.feedModel(FeedSource.Ranked(RankedSort.TRENDING))   // or HOT, CREATED, tag, community, FeedSource.Account(...)
feed.start(); feed.refresh(); feed.loadMore(); feed.retry()
feed.refreshAndWait()                                 // suspend: refresh, then return when it finished (pull to refresh)
val state by feed.state.collectAsState()              // FeedState(items: List<PostCard>, isLoading, isRefreshing, isLoadingMore, endReached, error, loaded, pages)
val post = env.postModel("alice", "hello-world")      // PostState(post, card, body: List<Block>?, comments: List<CommentItem>, ...)
env.votes.vote("alice", "hello-world", 10000)         // optimistic; VoteState.statuses[key] while in flight or failed
val reply = env.replyModel(rootKey, parentPost); reply.setText("..."); reply.send()   // one per parent, app-wide
when (val r = LinkRouter.route(target)) { is Route.Post -> ...; is Route.ExternalBrowser -> ...; ... }
model.close()                                         // when the screen goes away (not for env.session / env.votes / reply models)
```

Rules every model follows:
- State lives in a `StateFlow` and changes only on the main dispatcher. Actions (`start`, `refresh`, `loadMore`, `retry`, `vote`, `send`) return at once; failures land in the state as an `AppError(kind, message, retryable)`. The suspend entry points are SessionModel's logins, `switchTo`, `logout`, `logoutAll` and `signOut`, `@Throws(EcencyException, CancellationException)`; their failures are `AppException` whose `error` is the `AppError`.
- Post bodies, card summaries and card images are computed on `AppDispatchers.render`, never on the main thread (tests check every renderer call). Bodies are cached per post and `last_update` (200 entries), so reopening a post does not re-render it.
- `PostBodyRenderer` is the seam for body rendering. `InterimPostBodyRenderer` (markdown, parse, finish, blocks, image proxy, without render-helper's DOM transforms, so no chips yet) is TEMPORARY; the swap to the render-helper port is the one line in `PostBodyRenderer.default`.
- Votes: `env.localVotes` overrides what the API returned, so a vote shows on every feed card and post header at once and is rolled back everywhere on failure. A retry after a network failure (`votes.retry`) resends the same signed transaction; "identical vote" counts as success. Only the cards and comments whose vote changed are rebuilt; every other item keeps its instance, so UIs redraw one row.
- Session changes (sign-in, sign-out, wipe) clear local votes, vote statuses and reply drafts; a vote still in flight finishes on chain but its result (success or rollback) is ignored, so the previous account's vote never reappears. Feed and post models that were loaded refresh themselves in place for the new observer, so screens stay where they are.
- Paging: `FeedState.pages` counts loaded pages, including a page of nothing but duplicates; UIs key their "near the end" check on it so such a page does not stall infinite scroll. The feed ends when a node answers with the cursor it was given or after 3 pages in a row that added nothing, so a misbehaving node cannot page forever.
- Long discussions: the first 12 comments are rendered and shown before the rest (first load only; a reload keeps the old list until the new one is complete). The body cache holds 1000 entries (a post and each comment are one entry each).
- Replies: the first `send` fixes the permlink; the draft is kept until a success, so a retry resends the same transaction (and an edit after a failure keeps the permlink: it creates or edits, never posts twice). An edited resend whose earlier attempt did create the comment goes out without comment_options (write-once on chain). Reply models belong to the environment, one per parent: a send keeps running when the post screen goes away, the draft is there again when the user comes back (in memory only for now; RN persists drafts to disk), and `close()` from a screen is a no-op. A successful send drops the model from the environment, so the next composer for that parent is a fresh, empty model (UIs close the sheet on `sent`). After success the reply shows at the top of the post's comments (`CommentItem.isLocal`) until the API indexes it.
- Sign-in: see Login and accounts (the debug posting key import is gone).
- `LinkRouter.route` validates every target again with `SafeTarget`: only http(s) opens a browser, only allowlisted https players open the video sheet, `hive://` and `ecency://` are `Route.Unsupported` in the spike.
- Perf: `PerfMarks.mark(PerfEvent.POST_OPEN_TAP)` logs `EcencyPerf <event> <millis since process start>` (Android logcat tag `EcencyPerf`; iOS os_log subsystem `app.esteem.mobile.ios`, category `perf`, plus an os_signpost event). Both platforms mark the same events the same way:
  - `app_start`: the first line of the app's own entry point, before any UI (Android `Application.onCreate`, iOS the `@main` App's `init`). Its value is how long the system took from process start to the app's code.
  - `first_frame`: the first frame of the app's UI, whatever screen it is (Android: the root's first draw; iOS: the root's first appearance, marked after the run loop turn that commits it).
  - `feed_first_content` (first card drawn, `markOnce`), `post_open_tap` (card tap), `post_body_drawn` (first body block drawn); the models mark `feed_first_page` and `post_body_ready` (data, not pixels).
  - Compare platforms on these names only; `app_start` is not a frame, and launch time as the OS sees it (`am start -W` TotalTime, Instruments) is a separate number.

From Swift (no SKIE: observe with `watch`, which delivers the current state at once and then every change on the main thread; keep the returned `Closeable` and close it with the view):

```swift
import EcencyCore

let env = try AppEnvironment.companion.create(store: KeychainSecureStore(), debugKeyImport: true)
env.session.start()

@MainActor final class FeedViewModel: ObservableObject {
    @Published var state: FeedState
    private let model: FeedModel
    private var watcher: Closeable?

    init(env: AppEnvironment, sort: RankedSort) {
        model = env.feedModel(source: FeedSourceRanked(sort: sort, tag: ""))
        state = model.current
        watcher = model.watch { [weak self] s in self?.state = s }
        model.start()
    }
    func refresh() { model.refresh() }
    func onAppear(_ card: PostCard) { if card == state.items.last { model.loadMore() } }
    deinit { watcher?.close(); model.close() }
}

// Post screen
let post = env.postModel(author: "alice", permlink: "hello-world")
PerfMarks.shared.mark(event: PerfEvent.shared.POST_OPEN_TAP)
let w = post.watch { s in /* s.card, s.body (nil while rendering), s.comments, s.error, s.commentsError */ }
post.start()
post.setCommentSort(sort: .votes)

// Votes and replies
env.votes.vote(author: "alice", permlink: "hello-world", weight: 10000)
env.votes.retry(author: "alice", permlink: "hello-world")
let reply = env.replyModel(root: PostKey(author: "alice", permlink: "hello-world"), parent: postState.post!)
reply.setText(text: "Nice")
reply.send()

// Sign-in
do {
    _ = try await env.session.loginWithKey(username: name, secret: passwordOrWif)
} catch {
    let appError = ((error as NSError).userInfo["KotlinException"] as? AppException)?.error
    // appError?.kind == .auth / .notFound / .invalidInput / .network, appError?.message
}

// Links
switch LinkRouter.shared.route(target: target) {
case let r as RoutePost: open(r.author, r.permlink)
case let r as RouteProfile: openProfile(r.author)
case let r as RouteFeed: openFeed(r.source, r.title)
case let r as RouteExternalBrowser: present(SFSafariViewController(url: URL(string: r.url)!), animated: true)
case let r as RouteVideoPlayer: playVideo(r.embedUrl)
default: break // RouteUnsupported
}
```

Tests (`commonTest/.../app`, Android host and iOS; `SliceFixesTest` holds the session, reply lifetime, write-once options, partial update, paging and progressive comment cases): `TestApp` wires a fake transport, an in-memory vault, distinct test dispatchers for main and render (a tracking dispatcher proves rendering ran on it) and a fixed clock. They cover paging, refresh, errors and retry, card data, render caching per last update, optimistic votes and rollback, retry resending the same transaction, reply draft reuse and local comments on top, session import validation (wrong key, unknown account, bad input, release builds) and malicious link targets.
### iOS app (SwiftUI)

`iosApp/EcencyApp/App` wires the shared layer into SwiftUI (`AppContainer` owns the `AppEnvironment`, navigation path, sheets and alerts; `Observed<S>` mirrors any `StateModel` and closes it with the view); `iosApp/EcencyApp/Screens` holds the feed (trending / hot / new, pull to refresh, infinite scroll), post (header, body blocks and comments in one lazy stack, pinned action bar), vote weight sheet, reply composer, debug sign-in, Safari and video sheets. Lists identify rows by post key (`ForEach(..., id: \.key)`; `FeedPaging` compares keys too), never by the Kotlin data class itself: hashing whole cards and comment block trees ran on the main thread and a vote replaced the row instead of updating it. Pull to refresh awaits the shared `refreshAndWait()`. Reply drafts are the environment's (one per parent), so a send outlives the post screen. The unit test host (`XCTestConfigurationFilePath` set) shows an empty root and builds no environment, so no live feed or image fetch runs during unit tests. Tap routing goes through `NavDecision.from(LinkRouter.route(...))`; the video sheet's `WKWebView` has no script handlers, opens no windows and cancels every navigation off the embed host (`VideoEmbedPolicy`).

Debug launch arguments (ignored in Release):

```sh
xcrun simctl launch <udid> app.esteem.mobile.ios -fakeData YES            # offline synthetic fixtures (UI tests use this)
xcrun simctl launch <udid> app.esteem.mobile.ios -openPost author/permlink  # push a post at launch (screenshots, perf)
xcrun simctl launch <udid> app.esteem.mobile.ios -openPost a/p -scrollToComments YES
xcrun simctl launch <udid> app.esteem.mobile.ios -rendererDemo YES        # the renderer demo post
xcrun simctl launch <udid> app.esteem.mobile.ios -startTab WALLET -openDrawer YES   # shell screenshots
```

Perf marks from the UI: `app_start` (App init), `first_frame` (root first appearance), `feed_first_content` (first card on screen), `post_open_tap` (card tap), `post_body_drawn` (first body block on screen); see App layer for the definitions. Read them with `xcrun simctl spawn <udid> log stream --predicate 'subsystem == "app.esteem.mobile.ios" AND category == "perf"'`.

A Release build needs the build secrets (see Secure storage). For a local compile check only, append dummy values with their SHA-256 to `native/local.properties`, build `-configuration Release -sdk iphonesimulator ARCHS=x86_64`, then remove them again; never ship such a build.

## App shell

The RN shell (`src/navigation`, `components/bottomTabBar`, `components/sideMenu`, `components/header`) on both platforms, with the navigation model in the core (`shared/.../core/app/Navigation.kt`, `SideMenu.kt`, `Shell.kt`):

- `RouteName` lists every RN route (`routeNames.ts` SCREENS and MODALS, plus the QR and accounts sheets) with its feature title, whether it is built and whether it needs an account. Unbuilt routes open `AppScreen.Pending`, the one "Coming soon" screen that names the feature; a later wave adds a typed `AppScreen` and flips `built`. `pushGated` sends signed-out users to the login screen for routes that need an account (RN `showLoginAlert`).
- `NavState` is a value: selected tab, screens pushed above the tabs (RN's main stack, which covers the tab bar) and whether the side menu is open. Back closes the menu, then pops, then returns to the Feed tab (RN `backBehavior="initialRoute"`); at the Feed root the system takes it. `NavEntry` is the deep link entry (an optional tab, whose stack is cleared, then screens), built from `LinkRouter` results. `encode`/`decode` save it: Android in the `SavedStateHandle`, iOS in `@SceneStorage`. The menu is never restored open.
- Tabs as RN: Feed, Waves, Chats, Wallet, Notifications, icons only. Chats, Wallet and Notifications show RN's sign-in prompt while signed out; signed in, the Chats tab is the channel list (see Chats). The header on every tab: avatar pill (opens the side menu), name and @user (reputation) or "Login to customize your feed", QR, search and the boost button per RN `<Header>` flags. Feed has RN's create button (editor placeholder, or login). `BadgeModel` feeds the notification and chat badges; nothing sets it yet.
- Side menu as RN (`sideMenuItems.ts`): signed in Profile, Bookmarks, Drafts, Communities, Explore, then QR Scan, Refer & Earn, Docs, Settings, Logout below one divider; signed out Add Account, QR Scan, Docs, Settings. The header shows display name, @user and voting power (SDK `calculateVPMana`, fetched fresh on open). Add Account opens the accounts sheet (placeholder) when other accounts are on the device, else login. Logout asks first and calls `SessionModel.signOut`. Footer `v<version>, <build>`.
- Settings: see the Settings section (Manage Authorities and Delete Account: "Accounts and shell gaps"). The side menu header opens the account switcher; the drawer's Logout signs the current account out and switches to the next saved one (`SessionModel.signOut`); logout-all stays in the switcher.
- Links: `AuthLinkModel` takes ecency://, hive:// and ecency.com /auth and /signup first; ecency.com posts, profiles and feeds (`ContentLinks`) open through `NavState.open(entry, loggedIn)`, gated, on top of the open tab (Android intent filters with `onNewIntent`, a launch link handled once; iOS `onOpenURL`). A link that arrives while locked opens behind the lock, and a cold-start link waits until the accounts were read before the login gate applies. iOS takes links at the scene root (outside the lock gate), so links delivered while the PIN pad shows are kept. Android uses the RN app's ecency.com path patterns with `autoVerify`, verified through ecency.com/.well-known/assetlinks.json, which already lists the RN app id `app.esteem.mobile.android` and its signing certificate (README "In-place upgrade from RN 3.x"). Any account change (sign-out, switch, a logout that moved to the next account, a link login as someone else) keeps the tab and public screens and drops gated ones (`afterAccountChange`); a wipe returns to the Feed tab. Link and key notices use the shared toast channel.
- iOS saves the navigation from the container (not a view, which is gone while locked) and restores it at launch only when it is under 30 minutes old (Android: saved state after process death; RN restores nothing).
- Global UI: `UiMessages` (toasts and in-app banners from shared code, one of each at a time), `ConnectivityModel` (fed by `ConnectivityManager` and `NWPathMonitor`; RN's "No connection!" strip at the bottom). Error boundary: screen setup runs guarded (`Guarded` on Android, `ShellErrorView` on iOS) and shows RN's "Something went wrong / Try again?"; neither Compose nor SwiftUI can catch an exception thrown while rendering.
- Portrait only on both. Nothing of the shell is composed while the app is locked; on iOS the lock window stays above the side menu and every sheet.
- Deliberate differences: Docs and other web links open the system in-app browser rather than RN's WebView screen; the Feed tab keeps the guest filters (TRENDING, HOT, NEW) until the feed wave; iOS keeps login as a sheet and the segmented feed filter; tab icons are Material (Android) and SF Symbols (iOS) stand-ins for RN's icon fonts, Waves uses RN's wavy dash; there is no edge swipe to open the menu.
- Tests: `NavigationTest`, `ShellTest` (shared), `ShellFlowsTest` (Android), `ShellTests` and `ShellUITests` (iOS).

## Render pipeline

`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render` reproduces `@ecency/render-helper` 2.5.42 end to end in pure Kotlin (JS is only the offline oracle):

- `RenderHelper(proxyBase)` is the entry point: `renderPostBody(body | Entry, forApp, parentDomain, seoContext, options)`, `postBodySummary(body | Entry, length, platform)`, `catchPostImage(Entry | body, width, height, fast)`, `getEntryImageRawUrl`, `getEntryCardImageRawUrl` and `simpleMarkdownToHTML`. The app's `renderPostBody(post, true, false)` plus `setProxyBase(imageServer)` becomes `RenderHelper(imageServer).renderPostBody(post.body, forApp = true)`.
- `methods/` holds the transforms (traverse, a, img, iframe, text, linkify, clean-reply, markdown-to-html), the helpers and the regex constants. Per-element regexes go through `JsRegex`, which translates the JS source so the JVM and Kotlin/Native engines match JS (`.`, `$`, `\s`, ASCII-only `/i`); hot and whole-body patterns are hand-written scanners (`Scanners`, `Linkify`, `CatchPostImage`).
- The app should call `renderPostBodySafe` (never throws on content; a failure or a blank result for a non-blank body shows the body as escaped text). Renders are CPU-bound and pathological bodies take seconds, so always render off the main thread.
- Robustness: traversal deeper than `Traverse.MAX_DEPTH` (5,000) takes render-helper's sanitize-only fallback, as V8's stack overflow does; see `docs/upstream-render-helper-issues.md` and `PathologicalRenderTest`.
- Shared JS semantics (whitespace, trim, toLowerCase, URI coding, parseInt, number formatting, UTF-8) live in one place, `render/js/Js.kt`.
- Threading: a `RenderHelper` holds only its immutable `ImageProxy`. Each call builds its own document and state, regexes are compiled once and immutable, and there is no render cache, so one instance can serve concurrent background renders. `traverse` is iterative, so deeply nested bodies cannot overflow a thread stack.

Golden fixtures (synthetic inputs covering every provider and option combination, plus seeded random bodies stored as hashes):

```sh
cd native/tools/render
node generate-transform-fixtures.mjs ../../shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render/transform
node generate-fixtures.mjs ../../shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/render/RenderFixtures.kt
```

`TransformGoldenTest` compares every case exactly; deviations go in `TransformDeviations` with a reason (currently only the iframe security deviation above), and `securityDeviationsStillDiffer` fails if a listed case starts matching the oracle. For a local fuzzing run use `SEED=... RANDOM_COUNT=... node generate-transform-fixtures.mjs ...` and regenerate the committed set afterwards.

Corpus run (local only: the posts are other people's content and `corpus/` is gitignored):

```sh
cd native/tools/render
node corpus.mjs fetch 500   # or copy an existing posts.jsonl into corpus/
node corpus.mjs render      # expected.jsonl from the same oracle
cd ../.. && ./gradlew :shared:testAndroidHostTest --tests 'com.ecency.core.render.RenderCorpusTest'
```

`RenderCorpusTest` (JVM only, skipped without the files) prints exact-match counts for html, summary and image plus renderPostBody timings, and writes mismatch details to `shared/build/render-corpus-report.txt`.

Speak voice posts (`json_metadata.speak`): the body's audio markup is removed as in RN and a native player row (ExoPlayer / AVPlayer, waveform seek, one player at a time) is drawn under the body; see `docs/post-rendering.md`.

Updating the oracle: bump `tools/render/package.json`, `npm install`, diff the two package sources (the source maps carry them: `npm pack @ecency/render-helper@<v>`), port the change, rerun every `generate-*.mjs` that reads the package (fixtures, transform, dom, markdown, markdown-data, proxy, block-inputs, `block-words.mjs`), add golden inputs for the new rule (new ones go in their own object so the seeded random cases keep their names), then refetch and rerun the corpus. The rule inventory per version is in `docs/post-rendering.md`.

## Android screens (slice)

`androidApp/src/main/kotlin/com/ecency/app/ui` draws the app layer with Compose: home with TRENDING / HOT / NEW tabs, tag, community and profile feeds (same `FeedList`), the post screen (header, body via `postBody` and comments in one LazyColumn, action bar, vote weight sheet, reply composer), a debug-only sign-in, Custom Tabs for external links and a video sheet for allowlisted embeds. Layout and colors follow the RN app (`src/components/postCard`, `src/screens/post`, theme tokens in `ui/Theme.kt`); dark mode follows the system.

- Navigation is the shared `NavState` (see App shell) held by `ui/Navigation.kt`'s `Navigator`. Each pushed entry keeps its models and list state, so going back restores the scroll position; models close when the entry is popped. The `Navigator` lives in `NavigationViewModel`: rotation and other configuration changes keep the stack, its models and scroll positions; the models close in `onCleared` when the activity finishes; the screen list is written to the `SavedStateHandle`, so a process killed in the background reopens on the same screens (models are created again). Sign-in and sign-out keep the stack (a reader who signs in to vote stays on the post; the models refresh in place); only a wipe returns to the Feed tab.
- Reply sheet: the draft is the app-wide model of its parent, so closing the sheet or leaving the post keeps it; while a send runs the sheet cannot be swiped, tapped or backed away. Body images open a pager (`GallerySheet`), not the browser. The vote sheet starts at the last weight used (stored in preferences), as RN and iOS do.
- Rows get their vote state per card (`voteInFlight(votes, key)`), and the root's actions are remembered, so a vote recomposes only its own card (`SliceFlowsTest`). `compose-stability.conf` declares the shared state classes stable.
- The video sheet's WebView loads only allowlisted https players (`SafeTarget.embed`: every host render-helper accepts on any path needs its player path, from render-helper's embed URL shapes; a host without a rule is refused, and a test keeps every allowlisted host covered; a refused http(s) iframe shows as a link to its page instead of a hole) with `Referer: https://ecency.com/` (YouTube shows error 153 without one); every frame, main or sub, stays on the embed's own host over https, with or without `www.` (`SafeTarget.samePlayerHost`, so youtube.com/embed redirecting to www.youtube.com plays; as iOS `VideoEmbedPolicy`); popups, file and content access are off, there are no JavaScript bridges, third-party cookies are refused, and cookies, storage and cache are cleared when the sheet closes. Debug check: `adb shell am start -n app.esteem.mobile.android/com.ecency.app.MainActivity --es video https://www.youtube.com/embed/<id>`.
- `adb shell am start -n app.esteem.mobile.android/com.ecency.app.MainActivity --ez demo true` opens the renderer demo post instead.
- Perf: `adb logcat -s EcencyPerf` shows `app_start`, `first_frame`, `feed_first_page`, `feed_first_content`, `post_open_tap`, `post_body_ready`, `post_body_drawn` (definitions under App layer). Measure on a release build: debuggable builds render post bodies about 20 times slower.
- Release build check with throwaway secrets (never real values):

```sh
export ECENCY_PIN_KEY=release-check-dummy ECENCY_DEFAULT_PIN=1111
export ECENCY_PIN_KEY_SHA256=$(printf %s "$ECENCY_PIN_KEY" | shasum -a 256 | cut -d' ' -f1)
export ECENCY_DEFAULT_PIN_SHA256=$(printf %s "$ECENCY_DEFAULT_PIN" | shasum -a 256 | cut -d' ' -f1)
./gradlew :androidApp:assembleRelease    # unsigned APK; signing config is not part of the spike
```

Tests: `SliceFlowsTest` (sign-in keeps the post, a reply send outlives the post, drafts survive, gallery, last vote weight, one card recomposed per vote, saved back stack, player rules and Referer) and `RecreationTest` (activity recreation keeps the stack and its models, finishing closes them). `AppFlowsTest` (Robolectric) drives the main flows against a scripted node: feed, opening a post with comments, tabs, error and retry, vote while signed out, debug sign-in with a wrong key, a signed-in vote, and a reply that fails and is retried with the same transaction.
