Robolectric never runs draw callbacks, so the draw-time perf marks are checked on the emulator.

## Login and accounts

Sign-in, accounts, signing prompts and auth links on both apps, over the shared auth core. Shared models (`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/app`): `SessionModel` (login, accounts, Ecency session), `SigningModel` (every broadcast, through `SigningRouter`), `AuthLinkModel` (hive://, ecency://, ecency.com links), `AuthServices` (token endpoint, HiveSigner API, HiveAuth relay; `offline()` refuses everything, `DemoAuthServices` answers offline for debug fake-data runs). UI: Android `ui/LoginScreen.kt`, `ui/AuthUi.kt`, `ui/AuthWeb.kt`; iOS `Screens/LoginViews.swift`, `Screens/AccountViews.swift`, `Screens/AuthOverlay.swift`.

```kotlin
env.session.loginWithKey(username, passwordOrWif)      // suspend; AppException with RN copy ("Invalid credentials, please check and try again")
env.session.loginWithHiveSignerCode(code)              // the code HiveSigner's page hands to http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth
env.session.startHiveAuth(username); cancelHiveAuth()  // state.hiveAuth: INITIATING, WAITING (link + expiry), LOGGING_IN, SUCCESS, FAILED
env.session.switchTo(name); logout(name); logoutAll()  // state.accounts (current marked), state.switchingTo
env.signing.broadcast(username, opsJson)               // key, HiveSigner token / page, HiveAuth wallet, auth upgrade
env.authLinks.handle(url)                              // true when it was an auth link; state.prompt, confirm(), decline()
```

- Login (RN screens/login): username plus master password or any private key, RN copy and validation (both fields at least 2 characters), errors in RN's "Login Failed" alert. Memo-only keys are refused with their own message (RN failed later at the token exchange). An owner key is refused too ("This is the owner key. Ecency never stores or uses the owner key: sign in with your master password, or with your posting or active key."); RN has no string for this, since it signs in with an owner key and stores it.
- Key input (RN #3600), the same at sign-in, on the auth upgrade sheet and at the keys screen's import: every key or password goes through `KeyInput.secret` (`auth/KeyInput.kt`). Its edges lose whitespace (newlines, NBSP and the other no-break spaces) and invisible format characters (zero-width space, joiners, word joiner, byte order mark, soft hyphen, direction marks); usernames lose the same edges. Inside, characters are dropped only when the result then has a checksum: a WIF, a public key, or a generated master password ("P" + WIF). A password the user chose keeps its interior as typed, since Hive derives keys from the exact string and spaces are legal in it. A pasted public key (STM...) says "This is your public key. Paste the private key (starts with 5) or your master password." everywhere; on the upgrade sheet a private key of another role says which one it is and which is needed, and a WIF shaped key with a bad checksum is "Invalid private key or password", never a master password. An import changes only the keys it brings (a key account that imports a full master password becomes a master password account): the token, the session's refresh token and expiry, the HiveAuth key, token and expiry, and the sign-in method (HiveSigner, HiveAuth) stay (RN #3600 items 2 and 3, which RN loses). Tests: `KeyInputTest`, `AccountManagerTest`, `SigningModelTest`, `BackupKeysModelTest` (shared), `LoginFlowsTest`, `KeysFlowsTest` (Android), `LoginTests` (iOS).
- HiveSigner: the authorize page (app `ecency.app`, loopback redirect) in an in-app web view (Android WebView with cookies and storage cleared before and after, iOS `WKWebView` with a non-persistent store). `HiveSignerPage.decide` loads only https on hivesigner.com, reads the redirect URI without loading it, and cancels everything else; no script bridges, no popups. Hot signing uses the same view.
- HiveAuth (RN hiveAuthModal): the wallet request as a QR code (zxing on Android, CoreImage on iOS) plus "Open wallet app" (`has://`, `waves://`; opened by itself once, "Hive authentication app not installed" when nothing takes it), a countdown to the relay's expiry, cancel abandons the request. Deliberate difference: RN shows no QR code; the QR lets a wallet on another device approve.
- Ecency session: every login exchanges a code for tokens (`AccountManager`); at start the current account's session is refreshed (RN `_refreshAccessToken`), a key account without a token signs a fresh code, and on foreground a session expiring within a day is refreshed. A refused refresh after expiry, or an expired HiveAuth key, shows RN's "Your session has expired" alert with Verify (login prefilled) or Cancel.
- Post-login hooks: `env.postLoginHooks` (RN usePostLogin). RN's hook prefetches nothing beyond subscribed communities; the data layer has no subscriptions API yet, so the list is empty for now.
- Accounts: the switcher (RN accountsBottomSheet) lists every account with its sign-in method and a check on the current one; tap switches (votes, drafts and the held active key of the previous account are dropped, feeds reload in place via `SessionChanged`); the logout icon asks RN's "Are you sure you want to logout?"; "Log out of all accounts" (not in RN) removes everything. Logging out the current account moves to the next one, as RN.
- App lock and PIN after login: RN does not ask for a PIN after login (it stores its default PIN silently; the lock is opt-in in settings), so neither do the native apps. The lock applies while any account remains (`hasAnyAccount`) and goes off with the last account. `AppLockModel.verifyPin` (new `PinPurpose.VERIFY`) asks for the PIN before a key or a sign-in proof leaves the app and before the keys screen, like RN's PINCODE-with-callback.
- Signing: votes and replies go through `SigningRouter` (key accounts sign locally and a retry resends the same transaction via `HiveBroadcaster.broadcastIdempotent`; HiveSigner accounts post with their token and sign anything stronger on HiveSigner's page; HiveAuth accounts wait for the wallet, posting through the token when ecency.app has posting authority). A vote is signed by the account signed in when it was tapped and is dropped if the session changed before it started. Auth upgrade sheet (RN copy): active key or master password, checked against the chain ("Key does not match account's active authority"), held 60 s for one signature of that account only; or HiveSigner / HiveAuth. Owner operations are never signed in the app yet (`confirmOwnerSigning` returns false) and always refused from links. A cancelled prompt rolls a vote back without an error.
- Review hardening: key accounts are refused, before any prompt, hive:// operations the app cannot serialize yet (callback `not_supported`); links wait while the app is locked; a second link declines the open question (`user_cancelled`) and a link during a running request is answered `busy`; callback answers queue so none is lost (they open back to back: with two requesters, the second app comes up right after the first); a link still waiting for the unlock is cancelled the same way when another arrives; links never route while the lock state is unknown (also after a failed lock start); an `ecency://transfer` whose `from` is another account is refused (`account_mismatch`); sign-in proofs use the posting key only (`use_hivesigner` otherwise); the wallet opens by itself once per relay request (`openWallet` in model state), never again on a remount, rotation or relock.
- Privacy (Android): FLAG_SECURE on every Android version while the login screen, the keys screen, the auth upgrade sheet or HiveSigner's page shows (their dialog windows are `SecureOn` too), on top of the lock's recents rule; settings and PIN confirmation pads render in their own dialog window above any sheet; a copied private key is cleared after 2 minutes if the clipboard still holds a key clip of ours (only its label is checked, never its text, so no "pasted from your clipboard" notice; the delayed check holds no key) (API 28+; Android 10+ cannot read the clipboard in the background, so a key copied before leaving the app stays until replaced). The activity is portrait only, like RN and iOS. iOS: the secret field has no password content type, so iCloud Keychain does not offer to save a master password or key.
- Links: Android intent filters (`ecency`, `hive`, and `https://ecency.com/auth|signup` with `autoVerify`), iOS URL types (`ecency`, `hive`). Every link is confirmed first: sign links and `ecency://transfer` show the operation, its fields and the key it uses; auth-transfer says what happens to an account already here (a key account keeps its keys, a HiveSigner or HiveAuth one is replaced by the HiveSigner session); `ecency://login` hands over the posting key only after Approve, only for master-password or posting-key accounts, and only to a callback the parser accepted; `ecency://auth-request` answers with a sign-in proof, never a key; ecency.com `/auth` checks the code (7 days, right account) before "Easy Login"; `/signup` opens the register screen with the link's referral, username and email (see "Welcome and terms, register"). Callback answers carry RN's payload (`status`, `error`, `id`, `posting_key`, `code`, `request_id`). Verified Android links use the RN app's existing ecency.com `assetlinks.json` entry. iOS universal links: the app has RN's associated domains `applinks:ecency.com` and `applinks:alpha.ecency.com` (not `ecency.page.link`: Firebase Dynamic Links is shut down), and `onContinueUserActivity(NSUserActivityTypeBrowsingWeb)` passes an https page on those hosts (`UniversalLinks.url(of:)`) to the same `AppContainer.handle(url:)` as every other link; the paths are whatever ecency.com's `apple-app-site-association` lists for the RN app id (`UniversalLinkTests`). Both platforms are handed every listed path (the live AASA lists `/@*`, `/*/@*/*`, `/hot`, `/trending`, `/created`, `/waves` and their subpaths), so a first-party https page no screen claims (`ContentLinks.entry` is null) opens in the in-app browser (`ContentLinks.browserFallback`: SFSafariViewController sheet, Custom Tab) instead of bringing the app forward with nothing on screen; when another branch teaches `entry` more paths (bare `/hot` and the others), those simply stop falling back. `/@user/<page>` for the web's profile pages (render-helper `SECTION_LIST`, `rss`, `rss.xml`, `insights`...) opens the profile and is never looked up as a post; a second segment that is no Hive permlink falls back to the browser (`ContentLinksFallbackTest`, Android `LinkEntryTest`, iOS `ShellTests`). Android never hands such a page to the system as a bare VIEW: the app is the verified App Link handler for ecency.com, so Android would route it straight back to `handleLink`, which would fall back again, in a loop (for example `/@alice/Some.Thing`, `/@user/a/b`, `/hot/a/b`). `openInCustomTab`, for every caller, pins the tab to a Custom Tabs browser (`CustomTabsClient.getPackageName`, visible through the manifest's `CustomTabsService` query), or without one sends the page to the default browser app through a browser-only selector (`ACTION_MAIN` + `CATEGORY_APP_BROWSER`, never this app, no chooser on every link), and only when no browser app resolves opens a chooser that leaves the app out (`EXTRA_EXCLUDE_COMPONENTS`); `LinkEntryTest` checks all three intents. Device check owed: the pinned tab and the chooser on a phone with the verified App Links.
- Welcome (RN welcome): shown on the first launch and again after each update, as RN (see "Welcome and terms, register").
- Keys (RN backupKeysScreen, "Manage Authorities"): see "Backup keys" under "Accounts and shell gaps".
- Logged-out browsing stays: vote and reply open the login screen.

Hooks for the shell (side menu): Android `AccountSwitcherSheet(env, host, onAddAccount, onDismiss)`, `LogoutConfirmDialog(...)`, `BackupKeysScreen` via `Screen.BackupKeys`, and `AuthOverlays(env, host, onOpenLogin)` mounted once at the root; iOS `app.sheet = .accounts`, `.backupKeys`, `.login`, `app.logout(name)`, `app.logoutAll()`, with `AuthOverlay` (its own window at `.alert`, under the lock window) attached by `RootView`.

Debug launch options (fake data only, nothing on the network): iOS `-fakeData YES` plus `-seedAccounts YES`, `-openScreen login|hiveauth|accounts|backupKeys`, `-demoSigning upgrade`, `-openLink <url>`, `-showWelcome YES`; Android `--ez demoAuth true` (offline token endpoint and relay), `--ez seedAccounts true` (only together with demoAuth), `--ez acceptTerms true`, `--es openScreen login|hiveauth|accounts`, `--es demoSigning upgrade`. Fake accounts derive their keys from a public throwaway password.

Tests: shared `SessionModelTest`, `SigningModelTest`, `AuthLinkModelTest`, `HiveSignerPageTest` (fake token endpoint and HiveAuth relay in `AuthFakes.kt`); Android `LoginFlowsTest` (Robolectric: form validation and errors, HiveAuth QR, switcher and logout confirm, upgrade sheet, sign confirm and owner refusal, posting key handover, welcome, keys warning, VIEW intents); iOS `LoginTests` and `LoginUITests`. Mutation-checked: callback acceptance, confirm before handover, owner refusal in links and in app, the temp key's account binding, logout dropping keys and the held key, forgetting the previous account's state on login, the vote's session check.

## Profile, social graph and bookmarks

RN `screens/profile`, `follows`, `profileEdit`, `bookmarks`, `accountList` and `components/profileSummary`, `userAvatar`, `userRibbon`, `proBadge` on both platforms, over shared models (`shared/.../core/app`: `ProfileModel`, `FollowsModel`, `ProfileEditModel`, `BookmarksModel`, `ProMembersModel`, `AvatarStamps`). UI: Android `ui/ProfileScreens.kt`, iOS `Screens/ProfileViews.swift`.

```kotlin
env.profileModel(user, section)   // section: "", "wallet", "comments", "replies", "posts" (deep link tab)
model.toggleFollow(); toggleMute(); toggleFavorite(); report()   // report after the UI's confirm
model.feed(ProfileTab.BLOG)       // FeedModel per tab, owned and closed by the profile model
env.followsModel(user, following, count); env.profileEditModel(); env.bookmarksModel(tab)
```

- Profile: cover (proxied 640x320), avatar, name, Pro badge, `@user (rep)`, about, join date (device locale), website (opens https only), location, follower counts (`condenser_api.get_follow_count`, RN `makeCountFriendly`), voting power and RC bars (SDK mana maths, `rc_api.find_rc_accounts`; a tap shows "Full in N hours"). Tabs as RN: own blog, posts, waves, replies; others blog, posts, waves, comments; a link's section selects its tab or replaces the last one (`/wallet`, `/points`). Comments and replies are compact comment rows; another account's Wallet tab shows its wallet read only (see "Parity gaps (gaps2)"), the own one opens the Wallet tab. Muted accounts show RN's muted icon instead of the tabs.
- Actions (signed in, someone else): Follow / Unfollow, Message (chat placeholder), email digest (placeholder), menu: favourites (Ecency API), Delegate (transfer placeholder), Mute / Unmute, Report (confirm, `/private-api/report`, then auto-mute). Follow ops are the SDK's `custom_json` follow (`what` blog, ignore or empty), signed with the posting authority through `SigningModel`; a cancelled prompt shows nothing, a failure RN's "Fail!" alert; one action at a time.
- Models wait for the session to be read (`SessionModel.awaitLoaded`), so a screen restored before the vault was read never acts as a guest; an account change reloads in place.
- Followers / following: `get_followers` / `get_following` pages of 100, profiles from `bridge.get_profiles`, search over the loaded rows (RN). Rows open the profile (the quick profile sheet belongs to the feed wave).
- Edit profile: Display Name, About, Location, Website, and avatar and cover by image link (gallery and camera are placeholders until the editor wave builds uploads). Save broadcasts `account_update2` (posting key) exactly as the SDK's `useAccountUpdate`, through the one profile write path (below): `ProfileMetadata.buildPostingJsonMetadata` (a byte-exact port, JS property order, JSON.stringify escaping and number formatting in `JsValue`) merges the edit over the on-chain metadata. An account whose profile lives only in legacy `json_metadata` (the app shows it through the same fallback) starts the form and the merge from that profile, so its fields move into the posting metadata instead of vanishing. An account without an avatar writes `profile_image: null`, as RN. The new profile shows at once (account cache) and every avatar URL of the account gets RN's `?stamp=` (`AvatarCacheStamps`, read by `PostCard.avatarUrl`).
- Profile writes, one guarded path (`app/ProfileWrites.kt`, `api/broadcast/ProfileMetadataGuard.kt`), used by the profile edit and by pin / unpin to blog: the account and hivemind's `bridge.get_profile` are read again, and the write is refused ("Could not read your current profile, try again", nothing broadcast) when hivemind cannot be read, the posting metadata is not valid JSON (or nests past the parser's limit), its `profile` is not an object, or the profile holds fewer non-empty values than hivemind shows (a node that stripped the row; such a row is read once more first). An empty profile is a base only when hivemind shows nothing at all (any value of its raw `metadata.profile`, the SDK's `hasProfileValues`). The fresh row is first read the way hivemind's `bridge.get_profile` reads it, a port of the SQL `hivemind_postgrest_utilities.extract_profile_metadata` (hive/db/sql_scripts/postgrest/utilities/extract_profile_metadata.sql), so like is compared with like: hivemind reads the posting metadata unless it is empty, not jsonb (a NUL anywhere) or under 3 characters (`{}`), its `profile` or else its root, and only then the legacy `json_metadata` (`profile` or root); name, about and location are cut raw (no trim) to 17, 157 and 27 characters plus "..." when over 20, 160 and 30, a name starting with @ is dropped, a website of 100 or fewer gets `http://` unless it has a scheme (an empty one shows as `http://`, the shape Ecency web writes), images must be http(s) and at most 1024. When hivemind reads the posting metadata, the row's view must hold at least as many non-empty values as hivemind shows: count only, on purpose, because hivemind lags a write and a field match would lock the user out right after a save. When hivemind reads the legacy metadata, the view must equal what hivemind shows field by field: a node that blanks only `posting_json_metadata` leaves an old legacy profile with just as many values. One more case reads the legacy metadata although a posting row is present: some Steem-era accounts carry a `posting_json_metadata` that is only the copy hived made at the hardfork, never wrote `account_update2` and kept editing with `account_update`, so hivemind has no posting row of its own for them and shows the legacy profile, the current one. When hivemind shows something that is not the posting row's fields but is exactly the legacy fields, the legacy profile is the base (`legacyOverSnapshot`): its profile fields win entirely (a field it lacks was deleted since the snapshot and stays deleted), its other keys win, and what only the snapshot holds beyond the profile fields (tokens, a pin, unknown keys) is kept; the edit form shows the same values. This rule is off once this process has itself written a posting profile for the account, because hivemind still showing the legacy values is then only lag. And when the app itself knew a posting row for the account (an earlier read, or the row it remembered after its own write) and the fresh one is blank, the read is refused whatever hivemind shows, since a matching legacy profile says nothing about the tokens, pin and unknown keys of the real row. A posting row a node has shown is strong knowledge and keeps refusing a blank one; one the app only broadcast itself is weak (a broadcast can be accepted and still expire or be dropped by a fork). On a blank read with a weak mark the app compares hivemind with the row it wrote: hivemind showing the written fields means the write landed and the read is a strip (refused, the mark becomes strong); hivemind showing the values from before cannot tell "did not land" from lag, so the write is refused with "try again" until 3 minutes after the broadcast (a transaction lives 60 s, hivemind follows the chain within seconds), and only then is the account treated as the legacy one it was. The edit form does not open on a read a strong mark calls stripped. A refused row never stays in the account cache. Residual risks: a snapshot-class account whose legacy profile is empty (hivemind shows nothing while the chain posting row holds old values) passes the count rule, and a write publishes those old values; and a blank read before this process has ever read the account's posting row (every read since the app started was stripped) with a legacy profile that still matches hivemind. The write starts from the posting profile, or from the legacy one (wrapped or at the root) while the posting profile has no keys; unknown keys and the wallet token list are kept (tokens cleaned of private fields, as the SDK). Signed with the posting authority (a metadata-only `account_update2` is a posting operation on chain).
- Summary collapse (RN profileView): the summary sits above the tabs and the list, not in it. It closes as the list scrolls down (past 80 points with a downward run of 20) and RN's header takes its place (back, avatar, name; Android a bar, iOS the navigation bar's title); it opens within 8 points of the top, after a 60 point drag up anywhere in the list, or on a tap on that header. A 550 ms cooldown after each flip and a recheck when it ends stop oscillation, a single jump of 250 points or more (a tab switch, a reflow) is not a drag. One shared rule (`app/SummaryCollapse.kt`), fed exact nested scroll deltas on Android (`ui/SummaryCollapseUi.kt`) and the scroll offset on iOS (`Screens/SummaryCollapseViews.swift`). The open summary scrolls inside past its cap (large text), so the list keeps room. The tabs stay put; the summary slides at full motion, fades when reduced. Only the reader's scrolling counts: an offset that moves without a drag (the list clamping to its top once a short list got the closed summary's room, a relayout) is layout and holds a closed summary closed, so short content cannot loop open and closed; a tap, a pull down or the scroll to top popup opens it. Switching tabs opens it over the new list (RN reopened it at the new list's first scroll near the top). The state lives in the screen's stack entry, so a post opened from the profile and back (or a rotation) keeps it. Android reads the drags from nested scroll; iOS 18 from the scroll phase and geometry, iOS 17 from the offset and content height preferences (no scroll phase there: a list that can scroll is taken as the reader). The open summary is capped at 70% of the screen, which keeps the follower counts and the bars visible on a 360x640 dp phone; the trade-off is a short list window under the open summary on small phones (about 180 dp at 360x640), which closes the summary on the first scroll. Code that moves a scrollable list to its top (status bar tap, scroll to top) opens it, as reaching the top by hand does; a clamp when the viewport changes (the header closing) is layout wherever it lands.
- Mute, unmute and follow update the app-wide ignore list (`env.setAuthorMuted`, RN `currentAccount.mutes`): a mute drops the author from every open feed at once, the home, tag, community and profile feeds filter by the list, search results do not (as RN).
- Bookmarks: Bookmarks, Favorites and Tags tabs over `format=wrapped` paging (old plain-array answers end paging), newest first, long press to remove after RN's confirm.
- Account list (`AppScreen.AccountList`, e.g. poll voters): search and "time ago" rows.
- Golden vectors: `account_update2`, ignore and unfollow in `GoldenVectors.kt` (hived bytes, SDK signatures); `node native/tools/golden/generate-profile.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/api/ProfileMetadataFixtures.kt` writes the SDK's own `buildPostingJsonMetadata` output for synthetic edge cases (key order, integer keys, tokens, denied keys, numbers, escapes).
- Deliberate differences: RN sends `{account}` / `{bookmarkId}` objects where the SDK expects a name or id (favourite add and remove, bookmark delete); here the fields are what the SDK documents. RN unmute uses the unfollow toast and keeps the muted view until a reload; here it says "Unmute success!" and updates. RN's deep link section selects index 2 (waves) after replacing the last tab; here the replaced tab is selected. Follow paging starts from the last name of the follow list and drops the node's repeated start row. The website opens only as http(s), and `SafeTarget.httpUrl` refuses any link with userinfo (`ecency.com@evil.example`) for every tap target. A long account list is saved with its first 200 rows; a bookmark row without an id cannot be removed. Image URLs are an addition while uploads are pending.
- Debug: iOS `-fakeData YES -seedAccounts YES -openLink https://ecency.com/@spike-bob`, `-openScreen bookmarks|editProfile`; Android `--es openScreen profile:<user>|follows:<user>|bookmarks|editProfile` (with `--ez demoAuth true --ez seedAccounts true` for the signed-in screens). `DemoAuthServices` answers the profile area's Ecency API calls with synthetic data.
- Tests: shared `ProfileMetadataTest`, `ProfileModelTest`, `SocialModelsTest`, `SummaryCollapseTest` (plus the golden vectors); Android `ProfileFlowsTest` (summary collapse); iOS `ProfileTests`, `ProfileUITests` (summary collapse). The profile write guard's cases are in `FeedxTest` (`profileGuard...`, `pinToBlog...`, `unpinFromBlog...`) and `SocialModelsTest` (edit); mutation-checked: each refusal rule, the legacy fallback, the caller's use of the guard, and the ignore list updates.

## Communities and search

RN `screens/communities`, `community`, `communityMembers`, `communitySettings`, `communityActivities`, `searchResult`, `tagResult` and the `dappBrowser` home, on both apps over shared models (`app/CommunityService.kt`, `CommunityModels.kt`, `SearchModels.kt`). UI: Android `ui/CommunityScreens.kt`, `ui/SearchScreens.kt` (one entry, `CommunityAndSearchScreen`); iOS `Screens/CommunityViews.swift`, `Screens/SearchViews.swift`.

- Operations: `api/broadcast/CommunityOps.kt` builds every hivemind community op (subscribe, unsubscribe, setRole, updateProps, pinPost/unpinPost, mutePost/unmutePost, muteUser/unmuteUser, flagPost) as posting `custom_json` id `community`, `json` byte-identical to `@ecency/sdk` (golden vectors: `node --experimental-strip-types --no-warnings native/tools/golden/generate-communities.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/api/community/CommunityGoldenVectors.kt` from the RN root; offline, also covers the search query builder and RN `postUrlParser`). Broadcast through `env.signing` only. The post menu (feed wave) uses `env.communities.pinPost / mutePost / muteUser / flagPost` (suspend, `AppException` on failure, CANCELLED for a closed prompt) and the `ModNotesSheet` on both apps for the required reason (not blank, 120 code points, as hivemind asserts; `CommunityOps` refuses anything else). On an account change the screens drop what the previous observer was told (join state) before they reload.
- Reads (`api/community/CommunityApi.kt`, SDK parameters): `bridge.list_communities` (query `null` when empty), `get_community` (observer always sent, "" when none), `list_all_subscriptions`, `list_subscribers` (100 a page, no `last` on the first page), `account_notifications` (the string id cursor back as sent), `condenser_api.lookup_accounts` (refused locally over 16 UTF-8 bytes), `get_trending_tags` (RN only uses it in the waves tag picker; data layer only).
- `env.communities` (one per app): the account's subscriptions sorted by title, a join or leave wins until hivemind agrees (a fetched community's observer context next, then the list), RN toasts ("Subscribe success!", "Leave failed!", ...), one change per community at a time, reset on account change.
- Screens: Membership / Discover (top 50 by rank); community page with RN's Details card (description, stats, Join, New Post and Newsletter, both placeholders until their waves), TRENDING / HOT / NEW feeds and, for owner/admin/mod (team role, not subscription), the manage sheet; members (team then subscribers, community account dropped, sorted by role, editable per SDK `roleMap`, assign any existing account with `AccountLookupModel`, the row patched after a successful setRole because hivemind lags; hivemind drops a setRole it does not accept without an error, so `CommunityRole.setRoleRefusal` applies the rules of hivemind's `community_set_role` (hive/db/sql_scripts/community.sql) first, in its order and wording: mods and up only, never to or above the actor's own rank, an owner's role never changes, another account must be below the actor and really change, at most 100 accounts at mod or above; `get_community_context` is asked for an account that is not on the roster); settings (owner and admin edit, others read only with RN's notice, RN's required fields plus hivemind's `_read_props` limits in code points: title 3 to 20 and no @ or # first, about 120, description and rules 1000, language from hivemind's list; trimmed `updateProps`); activity log (50 a page, mentions tappable).
- Search: posts (search API, posts by default, RN filters sheet: author, category, tags, type, date, sort, validated like the API; a pasted post link shows that post; nothing typed shows @ecency's latest 7), people (`lookup_accounts`), groups (communities by rank). The topics tab stays out, as in RN. Tag screen: `#` field (1 s debounce) over the tag's three feeds. `AccountLookupModel` is the reusable account autocomplete (editor mentions later).
- Search requests (`POST {host}/search-api/search`) carry the SDK's client headers and go through `env.auth.http`. That transport names the app on every call to Ecency's own hosts (see "Notifications, editor and waves together") with the build's user agent (`ECENCY_USER_AGENT` or `ecency.userAgent` in `native/local.properties`, see docs/release-builds.md; a generic default otherwise).
- Explore: RN's dApp directory and URL field (`Dapps`, RN URL normalization). Opening a dApp or URL is `Pending(DAPP_BROWSER, url)` until the browser with the Hive signing bridge is built; the proposal vote request is not shown yet (governance wave).
- Navigation: typed `AppScreen.Communities`, `Community(name, filter)`, `CommunityMembers`, `CommunitySettings` (needs an account), `CommunityActivities`, `Search`, `TagResult(tag, filter)`, `Explore`; tag and community links and card taps open the tag and community screens (`NavEntry.feedScreen`). Debug launch: Android `--es openScreen route:COMMUNITY:hive-125125`, iOS `-openScreen route:COMMUNITY:hive-125125` (any route name and argument); iOS `-fakeData` answers the community calls and search offline.
- Community page: the Details card sits above the TRENDING / HOT / NEW tabs, which stay put, and closes and opens with the profile summary's rule (see Profile); its title toggles it, and a card closed by a tap stays closed at the top until the reader scrolls past the dead zone (iOS also starts it closed at accessibility text sizes).
- Moderation sheets: the manage sheet, set role and the mod notes of the post menu's mute and unmute share one layout, RN communityRoleEditSheet's (Android `ModerationSheet` / `ModerationRow` in `ui/CommunityScreens.kt`, iOS `ModerationSheet` / `ModerationRow` in `Screens/CommunityViews.swift`): centred title, the account or post it acts on, the rows, Cancel. iOS's manage sheet was an action sheet before. Behaviour is unchanged (the notes confirm row stays disabled until a note is typed; Cancel never broadcasts).
- Deliberate differences: only the visible search tab loads (RN ran all three); RN closed the community Details card on any drag and opened it only by its title, here it follows the profile rule (owner request); the manage sheet has a Cancel row (RN closes it by backdrop, swipe or back only), as the set role sheet does; TalkBack and VoiceOver scroll actions do not close the header (they move the list programmatically, which is layout), so the header stays open for them and the tap target opens it when closed; highlights are literal, case-insensitive matches (RN's highlighter took the text as a regex); RN's custom community tabs and the tag follow button (bookmarks area) are not here; set user title has no RN UI and no SDK builder, so none here.
- Tests: shared `CommunityOpsTest`, `SearchQueryTest`, `CommunityApiTest`, `CommunityModelsTest`, `SearchModelTest`, `CommunityNavigationTest`, `SummaryCollapseTest`; Android `CommunityFlowsTest` (Details collapse, manage Cancel), `FeedxFlowsTest` (mod notes); iOS `CommunityTests` (collapse driver), `CommunityUITests`, `FeedReadingUITests.testModNotesNeedANoteAndCancelCloses`. Mutation-checked: the nested scroll feed (both scroll tests fail), the disabled notes row, the collapse rule's dead zone, run and cooldown.

## Feed, posts and reading

The RN reading features over the existing feed and post slice, shared models in `shared/.../core/app` and `core/api`, UIs in Android `ui/PostMenuUi.kt`, `ui/ReadingScreens.kt` and iOS `Screens/PostMenu.swift`, `PostListsViews.swift`, `QuickProfileView.swift` (plus small edits to the card, post screen, vote sheet and root).

- Home feed: RN filters (`FeedFilter`, `FeedTabs`): signed in the saved tabs (Following, Communities, Trending, Hot by default; "My Blog", "My Posts", "My Comments", "New" available), signed out Trending, Hot, New; the "+" sheet ("Customise Filters", at least 3, no maximum) saves to `env.prefs` (`Prefs`: SharedPreferences, UserDefaults). Home and tag feeds inject Ecency promoted entries (`private-api/promoted-entries`) at RN's positions (4th, then every 4th, deduped, muted authors dropped) with "PROMOTED".
- Cards (`PostCard`): options button, "PROMOTED" / "POLL", NSFW placeholder image (`NsfwMode`, RN `nsfw` '0' show, '1' warn by default, '2' hide drops the posts), muted hint with "Tap to reveal" (in memory, as RN), pin icon, cross post label, votes open Voters, reblogs open Reblogs, gift opens the transfer placeholder, avatar and name open the quick profile.
- Post menu (`PostActionsModel`, RN postOptionsModal: items, order, labels, conditions): copy link and text, share sheet, reply, reblog / undo, pin to blog (the guarded profile write path of the profile area, posting authority), community pin and mute post (`env.communities`, so `CommunityOps` and its notes rules; moderators from the communities API's `bridge.get_community`; the one `ModNotesSheet` asks the reason for mute and unmute), pin comment (parent re-posted with `pinned_reply`), cross post (`CrossPostModel`, subscribed communities, payout declined), edit history, bookmarks (`private-api/bookmarks-add` with the session code), "Mute / Block" (ignore; the author's posts leave the lists), report (`private-api/report`, then mute), delete (confirm, `delete_comment`, the card leaves the lists and the post screen closes), promote (Redeem placeholder), translate on comments. Operations are `api/broadcast/PostActionOps` (reblog, ignore, delete, pin comment, cross post, poll vote; the community ones are `CommunityOps`); all go through `SigningModel`.
- Screens: Voters (`get_active_votes`, each vote's share of the payout, REWARDS / PERCENT / TIME with RN's ascending quirk, search), Reblogs (`get_reblogged_by`, search, reblog / undo button), Edit history (`private-api/comment-history`, bodies rebuilt from diff-match-patch patches, diff view with RN's colours; `core/text/DiffMatchPatch` is a port of the JS package with 694 golden cases), Bot comments (top-level comments by `private-api/public/bots`, previewed by avatars in the comments header), tag screen (TOP / HOT / NEW, follow tag via `favorite-tags`).
- Post screen: options and edit (own posts, editor placeholder) in the header, parent post box for comments (not waves), reading bar (Words, Reading N min; on-device text to speech is gone since RN replaced it with Listen, see "Listen"), the Listen bar, translation banner (`TranslateModel`: translate.ecency.com `/detect` and `/translate`, 43 languages, chunks of 1800, plain text in place of the body, RTL aware, dismiss per post), poll card (`PollModel`: json_metadata poll, poll.ecency.com results, `polls` custom_json vote shown at once), "Read next" (`search-api/similar`, two entries), comment options and tip.
- Vote sheet: RN's `$` estimate (`VoteValue`, SDK votingValue HF28 and legacy rules, golden against the SDK) and RN's downvote mode (first tap switches to red, second tap votes; RN has no other confirmation).
- Quick profile (`QuickProfileModel`): voting power ring, about, joined, followers, following, posts, resource credits, reputation, follow and favourite, "VIEW FULL PROFILE" (profile wave's screen).
- Other: `BadActors` (watchmen list and RN's transfer check) is ready for the wallet screens (RN uses it there only); Shorts have no entry point in RN's feed or post screens (Waves tab only), so nothing routes here.
- Golden vectors: `tools/golden/generate.mjs` (delete_comment, account_update2, undo reblog), `generate-feedx.mjs` (menu ops, pin to blog metadata, pin reply, vote values), `generate-dmp.mjs` (diff-match-patch).
- Safety and privacy: pin to blog never merges onto a stripped node read (the profile area's guarded write path, `ProfileMetadataGuard`); edit history patches are applied on the render dispatcher and refused over 300 KB of patch text; a post's language is guessed on the device first (`Languages.guess`: script and function words) and translate.ecency.com `/detect` is asked only when the guess is not the reader's language, never for muted or NSFW posts, once per post and session; the account's ignore list loads at sign-in (`condenser_api.get_following` "ignore") and filters feeds and promoted entries; the Listen player belongs to the post screen and plays on while scrolling.
- Deliberate differences: the on-device language guess is a heuristic, not RN's franc (Cyrillic and Arabic script and unsure Latin text go to `/detect`), and the card translate chip is not shown; the poll's optimistic vote moves counts only (HP sums arrive with the API); `Num.fixed` is exact for up to 3 decimals; tipping routes to the transfer placeholder (all tips need the active key and wallet balances, wallet wave).
- In-post anchors (#2403, beyond RN, as web): ids survive exactly as render-helper's sanitizer keeps them (`id` on a, span, div, p and headings when it is a name, `data-id` on a, which web's `MdHandler` jumps to; `name` is dropped there, so it is here too). `BlockBuilder` puts a `Block.Anchor(ids)` (drawn as nothing, zero height) right before the block that holds each one; `#id` hrefs (also on raw `<a>` without render-helper's classes) become `LinkTarget.Anchor(id)`, and a post link with `#id` (never the `#@author/permlink` comment form) becomes `LinkTarget.Anchor(id, post)`. `BlockAnchors.jump` resolves on the post being read: the element's id or data-id (first wins), else a heading whose GitHub style slug matches (render-helper gives headings no ids, so `[toc](#my-heading)` written for those sites still lands), else `#` and `#top` are the top. The post screen scrolls the body's lazy row to the top of the screen (Android `animateScrollToItem` through the body rows' `postBlockKey`, iOS `ScrollViewReader` on the body's block ids, both without animation under reduced motion); no screen is pushed, so back still leaves the post. Links in comments jump within the post body too (Android remembers where the body's rows start in the list, `CountingListScope`, for when no body row is on screen). Deliberate differences: another post's `#id` link opens that post at its top (the saved navigation has no fragment); an unknown id does nothing (a browser stays put too); `#id` links outside a post screen (waves) do nothing.
- Owner notes 10-29 (beyond RN's look): the lists' scroll to top (feeds, profile, Waves) is the FAB's twin (Android `FabButton` / `FabTokens`, iOS `FabButton` / `FabMetrics`: 56 dp circle, 8 dp shadow, white glyph, 16 dp margin) at the bottom start, opposite the compose FAB, mirrored in RTL, above the system navigation bar on pushed screens (profile, tag, community, search feeds; the tabs' bar already clears it) and only fading under reduced motion. It shows by RN's rules: post lists after 5000 dp scrolled back up (`ScrollTopRule`), Waves when 15 or more rows down and scrolling back up (Android) or by the same rule with `hidesOnScrollDown` (iOS). At the tabs the vote failure snackbar sits above the FAB row. The post bar shows RN's tip count beside the gift (`PostTipsModel`, `GET /private-api/post-tips`, compact, none at zero; RN prints a raw count, 0 included; unknown until a read lands, then nothing is said; `PostTipsState.SHOWS_ZERO` shows "0" as RN; a sent tip counts at once, confirm reads at `CONFIRM_DELAY_MS` (keeps the higher of server and optimistic) and `FINAL_CONFIRM_MS` (the server's count wins), a tip over an unknown count reads at once instead of counting one, the newest read wins) and has no Reply button: the comments icon (count, "View comments") scrolls to the comments header, which holds the Reply to the post (RN keeps a long press quick reply on the icon and a "Write a comment" field under the body; native has the header's Reply). iOS's bar now has RN's voters, reblogs, comments and tip too. iOS's profile and community header (`CappedHeader`) hugs its content (`HugTopLayout`, a fixed size whatever the stack offers): it once took extra height from its stack and centred the summary, a gap above the cover. At accessibility text sizes the profile summary and the community card start closed, close when the text size moves into that range and stay closed on a tab switch (`SummarySizeRule`, `closesSummaryForSize`); its compact height branch cannot happen on the portrait only iPhone app.
- Debug: Android `--es openRoute VotersScreen|ReblogsScreen|EditHistoryScreen|BotCommentsModal|PostScreen --es arg author/permlink`; the scroll to top shows at once with Android `--ez demoScrollTop true`, iOS `-demoScrollTop YES` (recordings, layout tests).
- Tests: shared `FeedxTest` (ops and pin metadata against the SDK, vote values, menu visibility per role and chain rule, delete, mute notes, report, bookmark code, feed tabs, promoted, NSFW, voters, reblogs, edit history, polls, languages, speech, formats), `DiffMatchPatchTest`, `GoldenVectorTest`; Android `FeedxFlowsTest`; iOS see the iOS tests. `FeedxFlowsTest.listeningGoesOnWhenTheReadingBarScrollsAwayAndStopsWithThePostScreen` drives the reading bar against Robolectric's TextToSpeech shadow (mutation-checked both ways); the iOS player has no engine seam yet, so its ownership (`PostScreen` holds the `SpeechPlayer`) has no behavioural test.

## Waves and Shorts

RN `screens/waves`, `components/quickPostModal`, `wavesTabBar`, `wavesTagPickerModal`, `providers/queries/postQueries/wavesQueries.ts` and `shortsQueries.ts`, `constants/waves.ts`.

### RN feature checklist (from the RN source)

RN no longer pages waves container by container: `getWavesFeedQueryOptions` reads one combined, cross-container feed (`GET /private-api/waves/feed`, keyset cursor, 20 a page) and every tab is a filter on it. Containers matter only when publishing.

| # | RN feature | Status |
|---|---|---|
| W1 | Waves tab bar: For you, Following (login gate), pinned source tabs, pinned #tag tabs, "+" picker; re-tap scrolls to top | done |
| W2 | Combined feed: `waves/feed` with `container`, `tag`, `following`, `author`, `observer`, cursor paging, short page ends the feed | done |
| W3 | Pull to refresh, 60 s refetch, scroll-to-top popup past 5000 px | partial: the refetch reads the first page only; Android's popup is keyed on rows, not pixels (iOS uses the shared `ScrollTopRule` on points) |
| W4 | Visibility filter (viewer mutes, muted or gray waves, bot authors); promoted waves (`promoted-entries?short_content=1`) after the 2nd wave and then every 4th, on For you and Following only | done. As in RN, the "muted or gray" test only ever fires for promoted entries: combined-feed rows carry no `stats`, `net_rshares` or reputation. Organic rows are gated by the server's observer mute (and Ecency's own moderation there), the client ignore list and the bots list |
| W5 | Wave card (RN comment view): avatar, name, age, body with images, vote and payout, reply count and reply, tip, options, Ecency badge | done (tip opens the transfer placeholder, as on the other cards) |
| W6 | Hashtag tap: transient tag filter chip over For you | done |
| W7 | Custom feeds picker: sources (Shorts, Waves, Threads, Snaps, Moments, Speaks), your tags, trending tags (`waves/trending/tags`), persisted | done |
| W8 | Wave thread: the post screen without the parent box; `/waves/<author>/<permlink>` links | done |
| W9 | Options menu on a wave (`isWave`: no pin to blog, canonical link), delete drops the wave from the feed | done (the existing menu; a wave is a comment there) |
| W10 | Composer (quick post modal, wave mode): text, 250 character limit with counter, short content hint, one image, PUBLISH | done (the image goes through the editor's upload, see "Wave images" below) |
| W11 | Composer extras: 3Speak video upload, poll, dictation, AI assist, clipboard image chip, draft kept across launches | partial: the video is done (below) and the poll is done (see "Waves gaps"); a wave may carry both, as in RN; dictation, AI assist and the clipboard chip in the composer are not done; the draft is kept in memory per account |
| W11a | Video in the composer (RN `quickPostModalContent`, `SpeakUploaderModal isShort`, `usePostSubmitter`, `providers/speak/speak.ts` `linkVideoToHive`). Read from the code: the video button (wave mode only) asks "Add Video": "Record Video" (`ImagePicker.openCamera`, video) or "Choose from Library" (`openPicker`, video); the uploader refuses a video over 60 s ("Short videos must be 60 seconds or less.") and has no size limit of its own; the upload asks for its token with `isShort: true`; afterwards the composer holds `videoEmbedUrl` and `videoThumbUrl`, shows "Video attached" with a minus badge (a tap removes it) instead of the image, the button is tinted and off, and PUBLISH is off while the upload runs. `_submitPost` writes `text\n\n<embed url>` (the video wins over an image, never both); the 250 counter is the typed text only; the thumbnail goes into `json_metadata.image` after the body's images (`extractMetadata({videoThumbUrls})`, with `image_ratios`) and is not part of the content permlink; the body's 3Speak embed adds the `comment_options` with the `threespeakfund` beneficiary. After the broadcast answers, RN finds the first `https?://[a-z.]*3speak.tv/embed[?/]...` in the body, takes its permlink (`extractPermlink`) and calls `POST <ecency>/api/threespeak/link-hive {permlink, hive_author, hive_permlink, hive_tags, code}` once, not awaited, errors only logged; without an access token it is skipped | done: `WaveComposeModel.video()` (the editor's `VideoUploadModel` with `isShort`, owned by the composer: owner-bound, cancellable, continued within the session), `setVideoEmbed` / `removeVideo`, `WavePublish.body / metadata / videoLinkPermlink`, `SpeakApi.linkHive`, `WavesService.linkVideo`. Differences: the link call is tried up to three times (at once, after 3 s, after 10 s) when no answer, a 5xx, a 408 or a 429 comes back (RN: once); it carries the same video and wave each time, and one wave is linked at most once per run of the app; a video permlink that is not letters, digits, `-` or `_` is not sent; a pasted 3Speak embed in the text also blocks a second video. When the call fails the wave is published all the same and the user sees "Success!" and nothing else (as RN): the video plays in the wave, it is only missing from 3Speak's own feeds; it is not tried again after the app restarts |
| W12 | Publish: newest container of `hive.flow`, else `ecency.waves`; content-hash permlink `wave-<16 hex>`; json_metadata (links, image, image_ratios, type wave, tags with hashtags, app, format); 3Speak beneficiary; optimistic card on For you; "Success!" toast, "Something went wrong" alert | done, except: `image_ratios` are known for images uploaded in the session (null otherwise, as RN writes when it cannot measure), and a wave HiveSigner refuses for the missing `ecency.app` authority opens the grant prompt (see "Editor and publishing") |
| W13 | Reply to a wave (quick reply sheet), vote with the wave vote weight memory | done: the existing sheets; waves keep their own last weight (`env.waves.voteWeightPercent`), in lists and on a wave's thread |
| W14 | Shorts: pinned `Shorts` source, `waves/shorts` feed, full-height reels, 3Speak HLS in the platform player (cover for portrait, contain otherwise), poster while loading, mute button, web player fallback, upvote, reply, tip rail, caption, one playing at a time, stops off screen | done in code; playback of a real stream was not exercised (see below) |
| W15 | Profile Waves tab (`author` filter, no mute filter, no promoted) | done (`ProfileModel.waves()`, rows inside the profile's own list on both apps) |
| W16 | `ecency.com/waves` opens the tab, `?text=` opens the composer with the text; OS share sheet "Wave" | partial: links done, no share extension |
| W17 | Getting-started checklist for new accounts and the daily check-in record | done on the Waves tab (see "Waves gaps"); RN also mounts the checklist on the Feed tab and records the check-in when a post is opened |

RN has no setting for autoplay on metered data, so there is none here.

### How it is built

```kotlin
val tabs = env.wavesTabsModel()            // WavesTabsState: tabs, selected, activeTag, pins, trendingTags
tabs.select(key); tabs.filterByTag("hive"); tabs.addTag("#Art"); tabs.toggleContainer("leothreads")
val list = tabs.feed(tabs.current.feedKey) // WavesFeedModel: start, refresh, loadMore, retry, setActive(true)
val reels = tabs.shorts()                  // ShortsModel: setFocused, setActive(key), playbackFailed(key), toggleMute
val composer = env.waves.composer()        // WaveComposeModel of the signed-in account: setText, attach, send
env.waves.openLink(url)                    // ecency.com/waves?text=... asks for the composer
```

- API (`api/waves/Waves.kt`): `WavesApi.feed / shorts / promoted / trendingTags / threeSpeakStream`, URLs built as `URL.searchParams` does and pinned to the SDK's own requests; rows normalized as SDK `normalizeWaveEntryFromApi` (`created` from `timestamp`, payout defaults). Feed rows are light (no `active_votes`, no `parent_author`, no reputation): the card shows `net_votes`, the host stands in as the parent, and whether the reader voted is only known for votes made in the app.
- Publishing (`api/waves/WavePublish.kt`): RN's `_submitPost` body (`text\n\n ![](url)`), `generateContentBasedPermlink`, `extractHashTags`, `extractUrls`, `extractImageUrls`, `extractMetadata` + `makeJsonMetadata` and the SDK's 3Speak beneficiary, as one `CommentDraft` sent through `env.signing`. Golden vectors (`node native/tools/golden/generate-waves.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/api/waves/WavesGoldens.kt` from the RN root, offline: fetch is recorded) run the RN helpers copied into the generator and the SDK's op builders, and pin the operations, transaction ids and deterministic signatures with the throwaway key.
- Lists (`app/WavesModels.kt`): one keyset pager per list; RN's visibility filter plus the app's NSFW setting ("Always hide" drops, "Always warn" hides the body behind "NSFW Tap to reveal"); promoted waves at RN's cadence; a wave published here stays on top of For you for up to 10 minutes or until the feed returns it; while a list is on screen its first page is read again every minute (new waves on top, fresh counts).
- Publishing guards: a container is only the host's own root post with a well-formed permlink (`WavesService.isContainer`; a reblog row, a comment or a wrong node answer is skipped, and with no container nothing is signed); container tags are read as RN's `tags || ['ecency']` (an empty array stays empty); a retry first asks the chain (`condenser_api.get_content`) and treats the same body as published, and an existing comment is never sent `comment_options` again.
- Share text (`?text=`): waits through the login it sends a signed-out reader to, never replaces a draft already in the composer, and reaches an open iOS sheet through `WaveComposeState.prefillSeq`.
- User agent: `ClientIdentity.headers` adds the build's user agent only for the first-party hosts (`FIRST_PARTY_HOSTS`: ecency.com and hivesigner.com with their subdomains, https, default port); 3Speak and GitHub get the platform default.
- Android web data: cookies and web storage are one process-wide WebView profile, so a closing video player skips its wipe while a HiveSigner page is alive (`WebData`); the auth page wipes before and after itself.
- Account binding: a list drops everything it shows the moment the account changes and ignores an answer still on its way for the previous account; the composer belongs to the account that opened it, refuses to send or attach as anyone else and is disposed on the session change; both apps close an open composer sheet on an account change. The reels start over too.
- Shorts (`app/WaveCompose.kt` `ShortsModel`): exactly one reel may play, `ShortsState.playingKey`, the reel in view while the tab is focused. Android composes a Media3 ExoPlayer (1.4.1, HLS) only for that reel and releases it on dispose; focus follows the lifecycle (RESUMED). iOS builds an AVPlayer view only for that reel; focus follows the tab's appearance and the scene phase. A stream that is not https, cannot be resolved or fails in the player falls back to 3Speak's web player through the existing embed web view (allowlisted, same-host navigation only).
- Deliberate differences: a body of only whitespace is refused (RN would broadcast it); a retry after a failed publish resends the wave built the first time, even when the day's container changed meanwhile (RN rebuilds and could post twice); `links_meta` carries the linked post's title only (what RN's raw SDK entry leaves after `JSON.stringify`); NSFW waves follow the app's NSFW setting; the refetch merges the first page instead of refetching every loaded page; the list jumps to a wave just published; the iOS reels cannot be muted before a stream plays; iOS has no scroll-to-top popup (the status bar tap and a re-tap of the tab do it).
- Debug: iOS `-fakeData YES -startTab WAVES` (offline rows; the reel player is a labelled stand-in there); Android `--ez demoAuth true` answers the waves endpoints with synthetic rows (`DemoAuthServices`). Take screenshots from these, never from the live feed.
- Open: the NSFW setting only knows the `nsfw` tag. The live combined feed carries explicit waves without that tag, and they show ungated (as in RN, whose wave list has no NSFW gate at all).
- Tests: shared `WavesGoldenTest` (also the video cases and the link call's permlink, from RN's own code), `WavesModelsTest`, `WaveVideoTest` (upload as a short, the wave RN would broadcast, the link call's request, its retries and that it never touches the publish, owner binding); Android `WavesFlowsTest` (Robolectric, a recording `ReelPlayer`); iOS `WavesTests`, `WavesUITests`.

## Notifications and push

RN `screens/notification`, `components/notification`, `notificationLine`, `leaderboard`, `foregroundNotification`, `providers/queries/notificationQueries.ts`, `unreadActivityCount.ts`, `utils/pushRegistration.ts`, the push and websocket parts of `screens/application` and the push section of settings. Shared: `api/ecency/NotificationsApi.kt`, `api/broadcast/NotificationOps.kt`, `app/NotificationFormat.kt`, `NotificationsModel.kt`, `PushPayload.kt`, `PushModel.kt`. UI: Android `ui/NotificationScreens.kt`, `push/AndroidPush.kt`; iOS `Screens/NotificationViews.swift`, `App/Push.swift`.

```kotlin
env.notifications.start(); show(); selectFilter(f); refresh(); loadMore(); open(id); markAllRead(); refreshUnread()
env.leaderboardModel()                          // day, week, month; public
env.pushPlatform = ...; env.push.start()        // each app sets its platform first; PushPlatform.None: push inactive
env.push.opened(data); received(data, title, body); onTokenRefreshed(); onForeground(); onBackground()
env.push.setEnabled(on); setType(PushType.VOTE, on)   // PushState.settings, available, open (a NavEntry to open, then consumeOpen())
```

- Requests are the SDK's: `POST /private-api/notifications {code, filter?, since}`, `notifications/unread {code}`, `notifications/mark {code, id?}`, `register-device {code, username, token, system, allows_notify, notify_types}`, `GET /private-api/leaderboard/<day|week|month>`. "Mark all" also broadcasts the SDK's two `setLastRead` `custom_json` operations (`notify`, `ecency_notify`) through `SigningModel`.
- Rows (`NotificationFormat`): RN's copy per type (en-US `notification.*`), the transfer amount, delegations in HP or RC, the vote percent, the account update variants, the post or parent title, a 96 px proxied thumbnail for RN's image types, and the tap target. Number and time formatting is pinned to RN's own helpers: `node --experimental-strip-types --no-warnings native/tools/golden/generate-notifications.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/app/NotificationGoldens.kt` (from the RN root; offline, synthetic values).
- Account and lock: nothing is requested while signed out or while the lock hides content. An account change clears rows, count and badge before the session shows the new account (`AppEnvironment.forgetSessionState`), every request carries the generation it was started in, and a late answer for the previous account is dropped. UIs also draw rows only while `NotificationsState.account` is the signed-in account.
- Unread count: read when the account is loaded or unlocked, on foreground, after a foreground push or websocket event, on pull to refresh, and replaced by the server's number after a mark. RN has no timer for it (the SDK query's `refetchInterval` has no observer in RN), so there is none here. A failed read keeps the last count; a grown count reloads the list.
- Push payloads are untrusted (`PushPayload`, `PushRouting`): on Android any installed app can start the exported activity with extras that look like a tapped push. Only the known string fields are read, oversized values are dropped whole, accounts, permlinks and tags must pass `SafeTarget`, texts are capped single lines. A payload cannot name a screen, a URL or a link of any kind: RN's `hiveuri` type (a signing link from a push) is not ported (the notification server never sends it).
- A tapped push is kept in memory (the latest only) until the accounts were read and the lock is open; it then sets `PushState.open` for the platform's navigation. Nothing of it shows on the lock screen. Its id is sent to `notifications/mark` only when the push names the current account as its target and the id is id-shaped (the server marks only the caller's own unread rows); a row already known to be read is skipped, anything else is dropped without a request.
- A push addressed to another account signed in on the device (RN registers every account) shows no banner, does not touch the current account's count and marks nothing; tapped, it still opens its public content. A push in front does nothing while locked or signed out.
- Token lifecycle (`PushModel`, RN `pushRegistration.ts`): every signed-in account with an access token registers the device token at start, on reconnect (60 s dedupe), on foreground after 24 h, when it becomes current, on token refresh, and again after a 401 or 403 once its access token changed. A logout reads the leaving account's token before the vault forgets it and disables its row in the background; the device token is deleted when no account is left or a row could not be disabled (then the others register the new token). Registrations wait up to 30 s for running logouts and register once more if they gave up waiting; logouts wait for registrations already sending. A wipe deletes the device token.
- Releases that cannot finish (offline logout or wipe, the process killed mid-logout): written to `Prefs` under `push.pendingRelease` before any request, as the device token and the account names (a wipe stores `*` instead of names). No access token is stored, so a finished logout leaves no credential behind; the preferences are not part of the vault and survive the wipe. At the next start, reconnect or foreground the entry is finished by deleting the device token (the backend's sends to the old token then fail as unregistered), or dropped when the device token is already a different one. While an entry exists nothing registers, so the old token is never refreshed for the account that left; afterwards the accounts that stay register the new token.
- Timeouts: every notification and push call to the Ecency API gives up after 30 s (`NotificationsApi.TIMEOUT_MILLIS`; the shared HTTP client has none on Android), and one release holds its lock for at most 120 s, after which its entry stays pending for the next retry.
- No token before consent: Firebase's auto-init is off (`firebase_messaging_auto_init_enabled` false in the manifest, `FirebaseMessagingAutoInitEnabled` NO in the iOS push include), the platforms never ask Firebase for a token while notifications are not allowed, and they turn auto-init on once they are (also for users who allowed them earlier). On Android the one-time dialog counts as used only when an answer arrives, and an answer given while the activity is recreated reaches the waiting callers (they live in the push platform, not the activity).
- Websocket (`ActivitySocket`): only where push is unavailable, only signed in, unlocked and in front, only `wss://`; RN's backoff (2, 4, 8, 16, 30 s, then it waits for the next foreground, unlock or account change).

### RN feature checklist

Done on both apps unless noted. Everything that needs a real Firebase project (the system permission prompt, a real token, a delivered or tapped system notification) is built against the shared model and covered with fakes only; it has not run on a device.

Notifications tab
- [x] N1 two top tabs: Notifications and Leaderboard
- [x] N2 filter selector with RN's 15 filters, labels in capitals
- [x] N3 flat list, newest first, unread rows highlighted (RN no longer groups by day: its `recent` / `yesterday` / `this_week` strings are unused)
- [x] N4 row per type with RN copy: bold source, Pro badge, text, extra, post or parent title, description, time, avatar, thumbnail (hidden with "Show Images" off)
- [x] N5 paging by `since`, pull to refresh (also the badge), footer spinner, placeholder, "No recent activity", error with retry
- [x] N6 row tap marks it read and opens the post, the follower's profile, the tag feed, the wallet tab, boost (placeholder) or the editor (placeholder)
- [x] N7 avatar tap opens the quick profile
- [x] N8 mark all as read: API plus on-chain `setLastRead`, optimistic, "Fail!" on failure, nothing while offline
- [x] N9 signed out: "Login to see", Leaderboard stays visible
- [x] N10 leaderboard: daily, weekly, monthly; rank or quests check, user, activities, reward; pull to refresh; tap opens the quick profile; a tap on the reward shows RN's "Earn points" hint
- [x] N11 tapping the selected Notifications tab scrolls to the top
- [x] N12 (#2686, beyond RN) swipe an unread row from the end towards the start (mirrored in RTL) to mark it read without opening it: SDK `notifications/mark {code, id}` through the same optimistic path as a tap (`NotificationsModel.markRead(id)`, the row springs back and stays). TalkBack custom action and VoiceOver rotor action "Mark as read" on unread rows. Android `SwipeToDismissBox` (end to start only), iOS a horizontal `DragGesture` beside the scroll (a mostly vertical drag or a diagonal flick stays the scroll's: only a drag the row followed can mark; the drag is flipped in right to left layouts; the list is a `LazyVStack`, so `swipeActions` is not available)
- [x] N13 (owner note, beyond RN) quick chips for Replies and Mentions next to the filter menu (`NotificationFilter.quick`, `NotificationsModel.toggleQuickFilter`): a chip is the same as choosing that filter in the menu (both stay there), the selected chip is filled blue (Android a checkbox role, iOS the selected trait) and a tap on it goes back to All; menu and chips wrap under each other on a narrow phone or with large text. The earlier All / Unread / Read view (#2686) was removed: two "All" menus side by side confused

Unread count
- [x] U1 badge on the tab icon ("99+" from 99)
- [x] U2 refreshed at account load and unlock, on foreground, after a foreground push or websocket event, on pull to refresh, after mark as read; the list reloads when it grows
- [x] U3 delivered notifications cleared at start (and the app icon badge on iOS)

Live updates
- [x] L1 foreground push: count refresh and the in-app banner with RN's texts and routing
- [x] L2 websocket fallback with RN's backoff (inactive until `ecency.activityWebsocketUrl` is set)

Push
- [x] P1 permission prompt at app start (only in builds with a Firebase configuration)
- [x] P2 token registered for every signed-in account on RN's triggers, system `fcm-android` / `fcm-ios`
- [x] P3 logout disables the leaving row, deletes the token when needed, the others register again
- [x] P4 registrations and logouts wait for each other; a leaving account is never registered
- [x] P5 settings: master switch and 14 type switches, kept on the device, sent for every account, the unavailable notice
- [x] P6 tapped push: RN's routing table (without `hiveuri`), marks read when the id is the current account's, waits behind the PIN
- [x] P7 availability (configuration, permission, token) decides between push and the websocket

### Rich push (iOS)

RN's `ImageNotifi` notification service extension: `NotificationService/` with `App/RichPush.swift` attaches the payload's `fcm_options.image` (https only, 2 KB URL cap, 20 s, image types only, at most 10 MB: refused on its announced length, and streamed with the request cancelled past the cap; anything else leaves the notification as it came). It needs no Firebase code (RN used FIRMessaging's helper). Built with push: `push-extension.yml`, enabled by `ECENCY_PUSH_EXTENSION=true` (ios-sim.sh sets it with `ECENCY_FIREBASE`). Owner: nothing new, it is RN's App ID `app.esteem.mobile.ios.ImageNotifi` with RN's profile (CI secret `NOTIFI_PROVISIONING_PROFILE`, named per target through `$(ECENCY_NSE_PROFILE)`). Android shows notification images itself (FCM).

### What the owner must provide for push

Nothing below is in the repository, and none of it may be committed. Without it both apps build, run and pass their tests with push inactive (settings show RN's "not supported on this device" notice).

- Nothing new in Firebase: the RN app's Firebase apps (`app.esteem.mobile.android`, `app.esteem.mobile.ios`) and their files, whose sender the Ecency notification backend already uses. CI decodes them from the existing `GOOGLE_JSON` and `GOOGLE_PLIST` secrets.
- Android: `native/androidApp/google-services.json` (RN's `android/app/google-services.json`). `androidApp/build.gradle.kts` reads it at build time and writes the values Firebase needs as string resources (no google-services Gradle plugin); a file without an `app.esteem.mobile.android` client fails the build with a message.
- iOS: `native/iosApp/GoogleService-Info.plist`, an APNs key uploaded to the Firebase project, and the Push Notifications capability on the app id with a matching provisioning profile. `scripts/ios-sim.sh` sets `ECENCY_FIREBASE=true` when the plist exists; `xcodegen generate` then merges `iosApp/push-firebase.yml` (the FirebaseMessaging package, the plist as a resource, the `aps-environment` entitlement). CI has to place the plist and export `ECENCY_FIREBASE=true` before `xcodegen generate`. The simulator never registers (no APNs token there, as in RN).
- Optional: RN's `ACTIVITY_WEBSOCKET_URL` as `ecency.activityWebsocketUrl` in `native/local.properties` (Android also takes `ECENCY_ACTIVITY_WEBSOCKET_URL`), for the websocket fallback.
- Dependencies: Android `com.google.firebase:firebase-messaging` 25.1.3 (built with Kotlin 2.0.21); iOS firebase-ios-sdk 12.4.0 (Swift and Objective-C).

### Deliberate differences from RN

- The list ends only on an empty page or a page of rows seen before (RN's cursor asks for the first page again after an empty one). Like RN's `maxPages: 10` the last 10 pages stay in memory and the page loaded first leaves; a refresh starts from the newest again (RN has no other way back either).
- RN's `hiveuri` push type goes to the auth link model like any `hive://` or `ecency://` link (it asks before signing), and only when the push is not addressed to another account signed in here. A tapped push marks its activity read only under the conditions above (RN marks whatever id the payload carries, with the current account's token). A link the app refuses (an operation it cannot sign, a malformed link) shows nothing when it comes from a tapped push (`AuthLinkModel.handleFromPush`): any app can hand the exported activity a push-shaped intent.
- Pushes for another signed-in account are ignored in front (RN shows their banner and refreshes the current account's count).
- A failed list request shows the error with a retry (the SDK query turns HTTP errors into an empty list, which RN shows as "No recent activity").
- A failed mark always puts the row back and shows "Fail!" (RN stays silent when the failure comes within 5 s), and the count is read again.
- Turning one push type off sends `allows_notify` from the master switch. RN sends the toggled value there, which turns every push off on the backend until the next registration. `notify_types` are sent in numeric order.
- A delegation row without an amount shows no extra (RN throws); an RC amount with text after the number uses the number.
- The websocket's attempt count starts over after a message or 30 s of connection (RN resets it on every open, so a server that accepts and drops reconnects every 2 s forever), and it disconnects in the background.
- The banner shows one line each for title and body, as RN, with the actor's avatar only when the name is a valid account.
- "Show Images" (settings, RN `hidePostsThumbnails`) is wired to the notification rows only; feed cards do not read it yet.
- The leaderboard's "Earn points" tooltip is a toast.
- RN's push switches are imported once from the RN app's storage (see Settings); a fresh install starts with every switch on.
- The Android status bar icon is a plain white bell vector: the repo has no monochrome brand vector (the launcher icon is a PNG), and no new art was drawn.

### Debug

iOS `-fakeData YES -seedAccounts YES -startTab NOTIFICATION`, `-demoPush tap|banner` (a tapped reply notification, or one arriving in front; with `-lockDemo YES` the tap waits behind the PIN), `-openScreen settings`. Android `--ez demoAuth true --ez seedAccounts true --es openTab NOTIFICATION`, `--es demoPush tap|banner`. `DemoAuthServices` answers the notification calls with one synthetic row per type.

### Tests

Shared `NotificationsTest` (requests, RN goldens, copy per type, routing, paging, filters, errors, lock, account switch with a held-back answer, unread count, mark as read, leaderboard) and `PushTest` (registration triggers, logout ordering, settings, tapped and foreground pushes, hostile payloads, the lock, the websocket and its backoff); Android `NotificationFlowsTest`; iOS `NotificationTests`, `NotificationUITests`.

## Editor and publishing

RN `screens/editor`, `components/markdownEditor`, `editorElements`, `uploadsGalleryModal`, `snippetsModal`, `beneficiarySelectionContent`, `pollsWizardModal`, `screens/drafts` and `utils/editor.ts`, on both apps over shared code. Pure logic: `shared/.../core/editor` (`Slug`, `EditorFormats`, `EditorMeta`, `EditorTags`); REST: `api/ecency/EditorApi.kt`, `ImageUpload.kt`; models: `app/EditorModel.kt`, `EditorSideModels.kt`, `DraftStore.kt`. UI: Android `ui/EditorScreens.kt`, `EditorPlatform.kt`; iOS `Screens/EditorViews.swift`, `App/EditorPlatform.swift`.

```kotlin
val editor = env.editorModel(EditorArgs(EditorKind.NEW_POST))   // or AppScreen.Editor.of("hive-125125" | "author/permlink" | "reply:a/p" | "draft:<id>" | "template:<id>")
editor.start(); editor.setTitle(t); editor.setBody(text, TextSelection(start, end)); editor.setTagInput("photo ")
editor.bold(); editor.insertLink(label, url, EditorFormats.LinkType.PLAIN); editor.setPreview(true)
editor.submit()            // after the UI's "Are you sure?"; state.done tells the UI where to go
editor.flush(); editor.leave()
env.uploadsModel(editor).upload(listOf(PickedImage(bytes, name, mime, w, h)), insert = true)
env.draftsModel(tab); env.snippetsModel(); env.communityPickerModel()
```

- Operations: a new post is `comment` + `comment_options` (always both, as the SDK's `useComment` builds them for RN), a reply and every edit one `comment`. `json_metadata` is RN's `extractMetadata` + `makeJsonMetadata` (JS property order kept with `JsValue`): `links`, `links_meta`, `image` (thumbnail first, 10 max), `image_ratios`, the poll fields, `type`, `tags`, `app`, `format`; an edit merges over the post's own metadata and keeps its `app`. An edit's body is the diff-match-patch patch when it is shorter than the old body in UTF-8 bytes, else the full body. Permlinks are a port of speakingurl 14.0.1 plus RN's rules (5 words, random suffix when taken). All broadcasts go through `env.signing`.
- Golden vectors (RN's own TypeScript, speakingurl and the SDK run offline; RN sources load through `tools/golden/rn-hooks.mjs`, which compiles them with the app's TypeScript and stubs React Native):

```sh
# from the RN app root
node native/tools/golden/generate-slug-data.mjs native/shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/editor/SlugData.kt   # when speakingurl changes
node --no-warnings native/tools/golden/generate-editor.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/editor/EditorGoldenVectors.kt
```

- Never losing text: every change is written to the local draft 300 ms later (`LocalDrafts`: one private file per account and slot, the key hashed into the file name), at once when the app leaves the foreground and when the screen goes away. The navigation only saves ids, so after process death the editor screen comes back and reads its local draft. Leaving a new post saves a server draft (RN), a reply stays a local draft (shared with the quick reply sheet), a failed publish keeps everything.
- One publish, never two, and never a silent edit: the first attempt's permlink is kept in memory and in the local draft together with a hash of exactly what was sent (`PendingPublish`: parent, title, body; several hashes when the same permlink went out with changed text). A retry asks the chain itself (`condenser_api.get_content`, no hivemind lag) through `PendingCheck`:
  - on chain with the content the editor holds: published, nothing is sent;
  - on chain with content that was sent, and the editor now holds something else: the user is asked "Your post was already published. Update it, or publish this as a new post?" (`publishConflict`, `resolvePublishConflict`). Update is one `comment` without options; new gets a fresh permlink;
  - on chain with content that was not sent from here (the post was edited elsewhere): asked as well, "A post already exists at this link and differs from what you sent. Update it, or publish as new?";
  - not on chain: the same permlink again (same signed transaction while the text is unchanged). With changed text the permlink is kept only while the first transaction could still land (2 minutes), and after such a send the chain is read again: when it holds another version than the one just sent, the user is told and asked.
  A new permlink is always checked against the chain (never hivemind, which lags) and is never the pending one. Tags are part of what is compared. A clock that steps backwards keeps a publish pending.
  The pending publish is forgotten by Clear, by opening another draft and after 24 hours. Replies follow the same rule in the editor and in the quick reply sheet (`ReplyState.conflict`), which share one draft slot and format.
- Edits: a recovery copy remembers the post's `updated` time. A copy made from the version on chain comes back by itself; one made from an older version is offered ("Restore unsaved changes?"), never applied. Submitting re-reads the post and refuses when it changed since the editor opened, so a patch never reverts someone's newer edit.
- Cleanup and privacy: local drafts live in one folder per account (names hashed). Logging an account out clears its drafts, reply drafts and recovery copies; a wipe (third wrong PIN, forgot PIN, log out of all) clears every account's, plus Android's camera temp files. The folder is excluded from backups (iOS `isExcludedFromBackup`, Android backup and data extraction rules; Android's cache folder is never backed up). RN keeps drafts after logout; this app does not.
- Posting authority for scheduling: when `ecency.app` is missing from the posting authority the app asks first, then builds the SDK's grant (`api/broadcast/PostingGrant.kt`, `account_update` with the posting authority only) from an account row read at that moment. It adds the one account auth and keeps every key and account auth with its weight, the threshold and the memo key, written in hived's order (accounts by name, keys by key bytes; the serializer sorts too). The grant is refused when the row does not look like a complete account (no owner or active authority, unknown fields, unreachable threshold, bad keys), when a key account's own posting key is not in it, or when it disagrees with the authority read a moment before. Signed with the active authority through the auth upgrade. `OperationJson.decodeOwn` is the only decoder that accepts it; links (`canDecode`) still cannot ask for authority changes. Vectors: `node native/tools/golden/generate-grant.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/api/GrantGoldenVectors.kt`.
- Account ownership: an editor, its uploads and its drafts belong to the account that opened it. `submit` refuses when another account is signed in, saves and upload signatures name the owner, local draft keys carry the account, and an account change takes editor and drafts screens off the stack (`requiresLogin`).
- Uploads: Android system photo picker (5 at most), a files fallback, camera through a `FileProvider`, paste from the clipboard; iOS PHPicker, files, camera, paste. Images are read and resized off the main thread and never decoded at full size (Android subsampled decode, iOS ImageIO thumbnail after a file size and pixel count check). Every still image is written again without its metadata (no EXIF GPS, device or time, no XMP), with the EXIF orientation applied to the pixels (all eight values): over 1920 px becomes a 1920 px JPEG at quality 85, HEIC becomes JPEG, a PNG within 1920 px stays a PNG. A GIF goes as it is (re-encoding would drop the animation; GIFs carry no EXIF, comment and XMP blocks are not removed), 30 MB at most.
