An animated WebP is re-encoded like any other still image, so it loses its animation (first frame, as a JPEG). A camera capture's temp file is deleted once read or cancelled; Android keeps the capture uri across process death. The url the host answers with must be a clean https url on the image host before it reaches the body, and file names in placeholders are reduced to safe characters and made unique. The upload goes to `<image host>/hs/<token>` where the token is RN's `signImage` (posting key message, or the access token for HiveSigner, HiveAuth and active key accounts), with RN's retries (502, 503, 504, timeouts, network errors, twice), progress and cancel. The body shows RN's "Uploading..." placeholder until the url replaces it; a failed or cancelled upload takes its placeholder away.
- Fake-data and test runs never reach the image host: the transport is `ImageUploadTransport.refusing()` unless an app sets one; debug fake-data runs use `ImageUploadTransport.demo()`.
- Debug: iOS `-fakeData YES -seedAccounts YES -openScreen editor | editor:draft:demo-d1 | drafts`; Android `--es openScreen route:EDITOR` or `route:DRAFTS` (with `--ez demoAuth true --ez seedAccounts true`).

### RN feature checklist

Editor modes
- [x] New post (optional community from the caller, preset tags)
- [x] Edit post: patch or full body, metadata merged over the old, no `comment_options`
- [x] Reply and edit reply in the editor (parent summary on top). Entry points as RN: the action bar and comment reply buttons open the quick reply, whose expand button continues in the editor; the post menu's "reply" opens the editor. Partial: no account switcher tile
- [x] Open a draft by id; "DRAFT" button for the most recent remote draft; local id-less draft first
- [x] Template drafts: apply as a new post, "Save as template"
- [ ] Share intent (text and files from other apps): not built
- Cross post is not in RN's editor (post menu, built in the feed area)

Markdown editing
- [x] Toolbar: text format row (bold, italic, table, link wrap), insert link sheet, snippets, poll, image gallery, clear. Order (owner, 10-28): Aa, Image, Video, AI Assist, Snippets, Link, Poll (new top-level posts only), Translate, Clear; Aa to AI Assist are on screen without scrolling on a 320dp Android phone at any text size (labels stop growing at 1.2 times; a long press shows the name in a tooltip at the full text size) and on a 375 point iPhone at the largest text (labels stop at the first accessibility size, the large content viewer shows them beyond). AI Assist is the editor's single AI hub: the image generator and dictation have no toolbar buttons and open from an "AI tools" group of cards above the actions in the AI Assist sheet (as RN's quick post composer does for the image card; locked while an assist run is running or has no answer yet, since leaving closes it), each sheet with its own prices, gating and charging unchanged (the image card names no starting price as RN's does: opening the hub asks no image price, the generator shows its cost before anything is charged). `EditorFlowsTest.theToolbarLeadsWithTheWritingToolsAndAiAssistIsOnANarrowScreenAtLargeText`, iOS `EditorUITests.testTheToolbarLeadsWithTheWritingToolsAndAiAssistIsOnANarrowScreenAtTheLargestText`.
- [x] Selection-aware insertion and caret placement as RN's `formats/*` (golden against the RN sources)
- [x] Preview through the native renderer (title, tags, body, poll)
- [x] Words count, caret restore per local draft, sweep of dead "Uploading..." placeholders
- [x] Username suggestions for `@` mentions
- [x] Compose translate (RN `composeTranslateModal`, `translateMarkdown.ts`, `translation.ts`, `emojiStrip.ts`; `editor/TranslateMarkdown.kt`, `app/ComposeTranslateModel.kt`). Read from the code: the sheet needs 30 characters of plain summary (first 2000 raw characters, 600 of the summary); the source is guessed on the device and defaults to English, the target is English, or for an English draft the app language (Spanish when that is English or unknown); choosing a source resets the target, and the target list never holds the source. Translate sends `POST <translate host>/translate {q, source, target, format: "text", api_key: ""}` once per request, one after another: blocks split on blank lines; code fences (also unclosed), blocks that start with `<`, GFM tables, rule-only, image-only and URL-only blocks are not sent; plain paragraphs are batched up to 1500 characters; a block with heading, quote or list markers goes line by line with the marker put back; longer text is chunked on whitespace. Emoji are cut out of each request and appended to its answer. As in RN, Translate sends the unpublished draft's text to the translate host when the button is tapped. Any failed request fails the whole run ("Translation failed. Please try again."), nothing partial is kept, and a run for another language pair or a closed sheet is dropped. Apply appends `\n\n---\n\n## <target name>\n\n<translation>` to the body as it is then, caret at the end, and ` [EN | ES]` to the title when the box is ticked and the title is not blank. Free. Golden: `node --no-warnings native/tools/golden/generate-translate.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/editor/TranslateGoldenVectors.kt` (RN's sources with a stand-in translator). Differences: the guess is this app's script and function word heuristic, not franc, so fewer languages show "Detected"; the heading is the English language name (RN asks `Intl.DisplayNames` for the name in the target language, which Hermes does not have, so RN writes the English name too); the title marker must fit the title field's 250 characters (RN allows 255 in all, a title its own form then refuses to publish); a retired run is cancelled at once (RN lets the request in flight finish)
- [x] AI assist (RN `aiAssistModal`, SDK `useAiAssist`; `app/AiModels.kt`, `app/PaidCalls.kt`, `api/ecency/AiApi.kt`). Read from the code: five actions (improve, suggest_tags, generate_title, summarize, check_grammar); prices from `POST /private-api/ai-assist-price {code}` (`cost`, `free_remaining` per action) and the balance from `POST /private-api/points {username}` are both on the sheet before anything runs; the text field starts with the body (10000 characters at most) and needs 50 characters for improve and grammar, 100 for the others; **there is no confirmation step, "Run AI Assist" charges**. The SDK sends `POST /private-api/ai-assist {code, us, action, text, idempotency_key}` with a new random key on every call, the app's mutations never retry (`retry: false`), and every error but 402, 422 and 429 is "Something went wrong. Please try again." (a 409 and a timeout included); "Try again" goes back to the form. Apply: a body action replaces the whole body and sets `ai_tools.writing_edit`, a title is set, tags replace the tags. Server (`ePoints` `ai_assist.py`, read from the repository): the points are taken before the model runs; the same key within 24 h answers 200 with the stored result, or 409 `in_progress` (`retry_after: 5`) while the first request is still running; 402, 429, 400 and a 500 `server_error` charge nothing; a 500 `generation_failed` was refunded; 422 is charged and not refunded
- [x] AI image generator (RN `aiImageGeneratorScreen`, SDK `useGenerateImage`; `AiImageModel`). Read from the code: a prompt (1000 characters; the post's title is offered as one), 7 aspect ratios (4:3 first), the server's power tiers when there is more than one; `POST /private-api/ai-generate-price {code}` gives the base cost per ratio and a multiplier per tier, cost = base times multiplier; balance and cost are on the screen; **no confirmation step, "Generate Image" charges**; the request is `POST /private-api/ai-generate-image {code, us, prompt, aspect_ratio, power, idempotency_key}`; RN passes no key, so the SDK makes a new one per call, and RN shows its generic failure for the SDK's HTTP 202 ("paid, delivery pending, retry with the same key") and for a timeout. Insert puts the image at the caret and sets `ai_tools.media_generation`. Server (`ePoints` `ai_image.py`): 202 `delivery_pending` and 409 `in_progress` both mean the request with this key was taken and paid, and the same key fetches it (`retry_after: 5`); refusals as for assist, plus 429 `daily_limit` and 503 `ai_unavailable` (nothing charged). Presented as a sheet over the editor, and as a screen of its own on the `AiImageGeneratorScreen` route (`AppScreen.AiImageGenerator`, `env.aiImageScreenModel()`): as RN's screen without `onInsert` it offers Share and Generate Again only, the account signed in when it opens pays, and its unanswered request waits under its own place (`paid:image:standalone`)
- [x] Dictation (RN `dictationModal`, `useDictationRecorder`, SDK `useAiTranscribe`; `app/DictationModel.kt`, Android `DictationPlatform.kt`, iOS `App/DictationPlatform.swift`). Read from the code: AAC in an .m4a file, timed on the wall clock, stopped by itself one second before the server's `max_seconds`; `POST /private-api/ai-transcribe-price {code}` gives `unit_seconds`, `unit_cost`, `free_remaining`, `max_seconds`; the sheet shows the rate, then the running cost (started units, minus the free ones), **and Stop uploads and charges at once, with no confirmation**: a multipart `POST /private-api/ai-transcribe` with `code`, `duration_ms`, `idempotency_key` and `audio` (no `us`). One key per clip, kept in memory across "Try again" after any error and dropped on success, on an empty transcript and when the sheet closes. The transcript **replaces the selection at the caret**, after one space unless the text before it is empty or ends in whitespace (not "appended"); the sheet stays open ("Record more"). RN's sheet shows no balance. Server (`ai_transcribe.py`): same key rules as assist (200 replay, 409 `in_progress`)
- [x] 3Speak video upload (RN `speakUploaderModal`, `providers/speak`, `speakQueries`; `api/speak/Tus.kt`, `SpeakApi.kt`, `app/VideoUploadModel.kt`, Android `VideoPlatform.kt`, iOS `App/VideoPlatform.swift`). Read from the code: the toolbar's video button refuses a second video ("A post can contain only one video..."); the picker gives one video; five frames are cut at the middle of five equal slices (10 s stands in for an unknown duration), the middle one is the default thumbnail and another image can be picked instead; `POST <ecency>/api/threespeak/upload-token {owner, isShort, code}` answers `{token, upload_url, permlink, embed_url}`; the file goes to `upload_url` or `https://embed.3speak.tv/uploads` with tus (`Authorization: Bearer <token>`, metadata `filename`, chunks of 5, 10 or 20 MB by size, three parallel parts over 10 MB, retry delays 0, 3, 5, 10, 20 s); an older backend without a permlink is uploaded in order and read from the creation's `X-Embed-URL` header; then the thumbnail goes through the normal image upload and `POST /api/threespeak/thumbnail {permlink, thumbnail_url, code}` (not critical); the bare embed address is inserted on a line of its own and the thumbnail joins `meta.image` while that exact address is in the body (`collectVideoThumbUrls`, restored for a draft with exactly one video). RN has no size limit of its own (3Speak answers 413 over 1 GB) and limits the duration only for shorts (60 s, the wave composer). Toasts for 413, 429, 503, 401/403 and a generic one. Here: the tus client is this app's own, over the shared HTTP seam (so the build's user agent, which the transport adds for ecency.com hosts only, never reaches 3Speak): creation, PATCH pieces of 2 MB in order (no parallel parts), the server's `Upload-Offset` checked on every answer (it must move forward and not past what was sent), HEAD after any failure and before a continued upload, a piece counts as stalled only when no byte went out and no answer came for 60 s (10 minutes at most a piece), creation carries no body and no content type and the one metadata field RN sends (`filename`), RN's retry delays, cancel. A failed upload keeps its place in the editor screen's model: "CONTINUE UPLOAD" asks 3Speak how far it got and goes on with the same token, or starts over with a new one when the upload is gone; another account never continues it. The upload endpoint and a `Location` must be on 3speak.tv, the embed address must be a 3Speak embed. Android: the shared media picker (see Media picking), the file is copied into the app's cache and read in pieces from there, frames with `MediaMetadataRetriever` off the main thread. iOS: PHPicker (videos), the lent file is copied to `tmp/video-upload` and deleted after the upload, frames with `AVAssetImageGenerator`. Video files go up as they are on both apps, as in RN: their metadata (location, device, time) is not stripped; only the thumbnail frame is re-encoded without any. Video in the wave composer: see W11a under "Waves and Shorts"
- [x] 3Speak uploader, the rest (RN `speakUploaderModal`, `uploadsGalleryModal` `_handleOpenCamera`, `quickPostModalContent` `_openVideoCamera`). Read from the code: "Record Video" is `ImagePicker.openCamera({mediaType: 'video'})`, the system camera UI with no length or quality option, and the recording then takes the same path as a picked video (the 60 s check for shorts included); the pencil on the current thumbnail opens the photo library for one image (`_handleOpenImagePicker`), which replaces the frame and is uploaded by the same `signImage` + `uploadImage` call (RN sends the picked file as it is); the preview is `<Video repeat resizeMode="contain" paused={isUploading} volume={0}>` over the local file, one per open uploader. Here:
  - Record: Android asks the camera app (`ACTION_VIDEO_CAPTURE` through `ActivityResultContracts.CaptureVideo`) to write into `cache/camera/capture_<time>.mp4` through the editor's `FileProvider`, so the app needs no camera permission. The launcher belongs to the activity, not to a screen, and `VideoCaptures` keeps what is open (uri, account, asking screen) in the activity's saved state: the result finds its recording after the app lock covered the screen or the process was recreated. iOS presents `UIImagePickerController` in video mode and moves the recording into the app's `tmp/video-upload` at once; the usage texts for camera and microphone name video. Without a camera (the simulator, a device without a camera app) the button says so.
  - A recording belongs to the account and the screen that started it: it is handed over once, only to that screen, only while that account is signed in; for anyone else it is deleted.
  - No temporary file stays: a cancelled or empty recording is deleted when the camera closes; one that was not taken is deleted by the next recording, by an account change and at the next cold start; one that reached the upload is deleted by the upload model (`PickedVideo.letGo`) when it is uploaded and placed, replaced by another video, refused (too long, empty), when the sheet is closed without an upload, and when the editor or composer goes. A failed upload that can still be continued keeps its file until then. On iOS the same hook deletes the app's copy of a picked video, so the editor and the wave composer never delete each other's file; the folder is emptied at app start.
  - Thumbnail: the picked image goes through the existing preparation (decoded and written again without EXIF or XMP, orientation applied, 1920 px) and the existing signed image upload; choosing a frame again drops it.
  - Preview: one player (Android Media3 `ExoPlayer`, iOS `AVQueuePlayer` with a looper) over the local file, looping, muted, fitted; it plays only while no upload runs, the app is in front and the app lock is open (`VideoUploadState.previewPlays`, the lifecycle or scene phase), and it is released when the sheet goes (on Android nothing is composed under the lock, so it is released there too).
  - Differences: RN keeps a recording in the picker library's cache until the system clears it; RN's uploader keeps the picked video when it is closed, here closing the sheet forgets a video that was never sent (a failed upload keeps its place). A recorded video's file goes up as it is, as in RN: its metadata (location when the camera app writes one, device, time) is not stripped
- RN has no undo and no heading, list, quote or code buttons; neither has this (the system undo of the text fields works)

Fields and options
- [x] Title, tags (RN's rules, toasts and the 7 warnings in order), community select with the posting restriction
- [x] Post options: schedule, reward with "Set Default", reblog for community posts, thumbnail, short description
- [x] Beneficiaries: author row, add, remove, 3Speak row locked, "Support Ecency" chip, injection of the saved percent when the list was never set
- [x] Poll: question, choices, end time, age, max options, interpretation, toggles; attach, update, reset

Publishing
- [x] `comment` + `comment_options`, permlink rules, `json_metadata`, reply and edit operations (byte-identical, golden)
- [x] Publish confirm, failure toast with everything kept, success toast, local draft cleared, "Post published" prompt, profile opens
- [x] Reblog after publish (best effort, as RN)
- [x] Schedule through `schedules-add` with options, granting `ecency.app` posting authority first when missing (confirmed, active key)
- [x] RC offer after an out-of-RC publish error (`RcErrors`: RN's regex and copy; "Top up RC with Points" and "Boost account" open their routes, both still placeholders)
- [x] RC precheck banner (RN `rcPrecheckBanner`, `useRcPrecheck`, `utils/rcPayload.ts`; SDK `estimateRcPrecheck`, `estimateCommentTransactionBytes`, `countCommentResourceUsage`, `priceRcUsage`, `computeResourceCost`; here `math/UBig.kt`, `hive/RcCost.kt`, `EditorModel.rcPayload`). Read from the code: 800 ms after the last change the editor builds the operation it would broadcast (a new post with its `comment_options` and the beneficiaries the submit adds, a reply with the parent's tags and no options, an edit with its patch; image ratios left out) and prices it: transaction bytes (11 header, the operation count, one varint-prefixed field per string, 16 for the asset, 65 per signature), usage per resource from `size_info`, and per resource with a share `((regen * share / 10000 * coeff_a) >> shift + 1) * usage / (coeff_b + pool) + 1`, summed; the mana is `current_mana + elapsed * max_rc / 432000` (not capped at the maximum, as the SDK); the banner ("This post may be too large to publish" / "Your resource credits look too low for a post this size." / "Top up RC with Points") shows when the mana is below 1.2 times the cost, never blocks the submit, and a tap opens the same offer a failed broadcast raises. Without any of the three node answers (`rc_api.find_rc_accounts`, `get_rc_stats`, `get_resource_params`) nothing is said. `UBig` is a minimal unsigned big integer (add, multiply, shift right, divide, compare, decimal and double conversion with `Number(bigint)` rounding), because `coeff_a` is about 1e19. Golden: `node --no-warnings native/tools/golden/generate-rc.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/math/RcGoldenVectors.kt` (207 BigInt operations, 600 curve costs and 32 comment estimates from the SDK); `RcPrecheckEditorTest` also checks that the estimate's byte count equals the app's own serialized transaction plus one signature. The account's RC and the stats are read once per editor, the curve parameters once a day
- [x] Quest chip on a new post (`POST /private-api/quests`, RN `deriveQuestChipState`; tap opens Perks, a placeholder; closed for the run of the app) and the short reply hint (SDK `earnsQuestContentCredit`: more than 25 code points, links not counted) in the editor's reply mode and the quick reply sheet
- [x] Posting authority prompt ("Enable Faster Posting", RN copy and condition: HiveAuth, HiveSigner, or a key login without a posting key but with a token, and no `ecency.app` posting authority). Grant uses the guarded grant above with the active authority; skip remembers nothing, as RN
- [x] Posting authority prompt after an `unauthorized_client` error in the quick post paths (RN `usePostSubmitter` `_promptPostingAuthorityRecovery`, `hive.ts` `isMissingEcencyPostingAuthorityError`; `app/PostingAuthorityFlow.kt` `AuthorityRecovery`, in `WaveComposeModel` and `ComposeReplyModel`). Read from the code: when the broadcast of a wave or a quick reply fails and the error says "permission to broadcast" (in `error_description` or the message, lower-cased), or its code is `unauthorized_client` and it mentions ecency.app, RN opens the same prompt instead of an alert; "Grant Permission" grants and sends once more (`isAuthorityRetry`, so the retry cannot ask again and its failure shows as an error); "Skip for Now", a grant that failed or a prompt that was closed ends the publish without an alert and keeps the draft. The bare `unauthorized_client` code is not enough (HiveSigner answers it for an expired token too). Here the grant is the guarded one that scheduling uses (`PostingAuthorityFlow.grant`: built from two agreeing reads made now, active authority through the auth upgrade), the prompt is the editor's dialog, and it only grants and resends while the composer's owner is signed in. Golden: `WavesGoldens.authorityErrors` from RN's function. Not built: RN's quick post also asks before the first send (`shouldPromptPostingAuthority`); here the quick paths ask after the refusal only (a HiveAuth account signs in its wallet as before)
- [x] Rating prompt (RN `maybeRequestReview`, `utils/storeReview.ts`; `AppRating`, `AppEnvironment.maybeRequestReview`). Read from the code: asked for after a successful publish (`editorContainer`) and after a vote that went out with a weight other than zero (`upvotePopover`), never on their failure paths; once (`hasRequestedReview`), not before 3 days since first use and 5 app starts, 1.2 s after the action, one at a time (`reviewRequestInFlight`); `hasRequestedReview` is only set when `StoreReview.requestReview()` did not throw, so a request the store could not take is tried again at the next occasion. iOS asks StoreKit. Android asks Google Play's In-App Review (`PlayReview`: `com.google.android.play:review` 2.0.1; it and what it pulls in are Java-only, no class carries `kotlin/Metadata`, checked in the unpacked `classes.jar` of each; the `-ktx` artifact is not used): the flow runs over the activity in front, nothing is asked for while the app is in the background, and a flow that failed (no Play Store) leaves the one request unspent, as RN. Play does not say whether its dialog was shown (it has its own quota); a flow that completed counts as the request, as in RN. Never in demo or fake-data runs
- [x] First publish digest offer (RN `utils/firstPublishDigest.ts`, `components/newsletterDigestSheet`, `providers/queries/newsletterQueries.ts`, SDK `subscribeDigestRequest`, `getDigestSubscriptionsRequest`, `leaveDigestRequest`; `api/ecency/NewsletterApi.kt`, `app/DigestModel.kt`, Android `ui/DigestUi.kt`, iOS `Screens/DigestViews.swift`). Read from the code: one second after a new post was published (never a reply, an edit or a schedule), when the account's `post_count` from before the publish is known and zero and the flag `first_publish_digest_<user>` is not set, the flag is written and the `NEWSLETTER_DIGEST` sheet opens for the own-notifications list with "Congrats on your first post!"; an account change within that second skips it without writing the flag; nothing of it can fail or delay the publish. The sheet reads `GET <ecency>/api/newsletter/subscriptions` (header `X-HS-Token: <code>`), shows nothing but a spinner until that answered and "Email digests are not available right now." when it failed; it asks for an address only when the service holds none for the account (`/^\S+@\S+\.\S+$/` on the trimmed text), offers Weekly or Monthly, and sends `POST /api/newsletter/subscribe {email, type, target, cadence, source: "mobile-app", code}`: `pending_confirmation` shows "Check your inbox", `refused` an alert, a 429 "Too many attempts", any other failure "Something went wrong", success a toast and the sheet closes; an existing subscription shows its status, "Update" (off while the cadence is unchanged) or "Resend confirmation", and "Leave digest" (`DELETE /api/newsletter/subscriptions/<id>`). Not paid: no points, no idempotency key, a tap sends again. Here: the calls go through `EcencyPrivateApi.relay` (30 s bound, the owner's own code); the sheet belongs to the account it opened for, drops an answer that arrives after an account change and closes on it; the post count is read from the node before the first send of a new post (3 s at most, kept across retries of that publish, never asked again once the account was offered or is known to have posts); the flag is `digest.firstPublishOffered.<user>` in the app's preferences. Golden: `node native/tools/golden/generate-newsletter.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/api/ecency/NewsletterGoldens.kt` (the SDK's requests and answer handling, RN's form rules, address test and offer rule). The other places RN opens this sheet from (a profile, a community, the end-of-post prompt, the own profile's sender row) are in "Parity gaps (gaps2)"; Settings "Email digests" and "Stop all" are in "Settings"

Drafts and schedules
- [x] Local draft per account, reply drafts per parent
- [x] Save draft (update or save as new), save on leaving
- [x] Drafts screen: Drafts, Schedules, Templates, paging by 20, unsynced local draft row, open, delete, clone, batch delete
- [x] Schedules: status, delete, move to drafts

- [x] Gallery picker, files picker, camera, paste
- [x] HEIC to JPEG, resize over 1920 px, JPEG quality 85
- [x] Signed upload with retries, progress and cancel, placeholders

### Paid AI features: the money rules

AI assist, the AI image generator and dictation spend Ecency points. All three go through `PaidCalls.kt` (`PaidIntent`, `PaidIntents`, `PaidRunner`, `PaidCalls.classify`) and `PaidFlow` in `AiModels.kt`:

- Price and balance come from the server and are on screen before the charge; without a price nothing can be sent (RN's image screen falls back to a made-up 150). As in RN there is no confirmation step, the action button charges; here its label states the cost: "Run AI Assist (5 POINTS)" or "(Free)", "Generate Image (150 POINTS)", "Stop (15 Points)".
- One idempotency key per intent (one assist run, one image, one recorded clip): 32 hex characters from the secure random source, made once, written to the account's local store (`paid:<kind>:<editor>`, one place per editor: its draft, reply or edit, so two editors of one account never overwrite or delete each other's request; next to the drafts: private, cleared at logout, never backed up) together with what was asked, before the request leaves. Every later request for that intent carries the same key and the same payload, also after the app was killed: the sheet then opens on "An earlier request has no answer yet" with the stored text or prompt, and its inputs stay locked. The key is dropped when the result was delivered, on a definite refusal, or when the user gives up (confirmed: "the points were spent and the result will be lost"; a dictation clip that was held back and never sent, or that the server refused without charging, says instead that nothing was charged and that it will be deleted). A record older than 20 hours by the device's clock, or dated in the future (the clock was set back), is dropped unread, because past the server's 24 hour replay window its key would pay again. A record is only removed by the intent whose key it holds. No monotonic guard was added: a monotonic clock does not survive a reboot or count across process death in a way both platforms expose alike, and there is no server time on these answers to anchor to; the wide margin and the refusal of future-dated records are what stands in for it. Logging out clears the store, so an unanswered paid request of that account can no longer be fetched from this device. RN: a new key per tap (assist, image), or one kept in memory only (dictation).
- A paid request is sent once per tap and never again by itself. The two JSON calls wait 130 s for their answer and the transcription upload as long (above the gateway's 120 s, so a slow generation is an answer; everything else keeps the 30 s bound). No answer within that, a lost connection, a 5xx that says nothing about the charge, and a 200 that cannot be read are "result unknown": "No answer yet. The request may still be running. Check again to fetch the result: it is not charged a second time." The only button is "Check again", which sends the same key (production dedupes on user and key: a repeat answers the stored result or 409 `in_progress`). A request that failed before anything left the device (a clip that cannot be read, no session) is not "unknown": it says nothing was sent. There is no "Try again" that could charge twice.
- 202 `delivery_pending` (images) and 409 `in_progress` (all three) are "paid, not ready": asked for again with the same key after the server's `retry_after` (2 to 60 s), at most 12 times, then left to "Check again". RN treats both as a failure and, for assist and images, forgets the key.
- A refusal ends the intent: 400, 402, 429 and 503 charge nothing, the 500s the server names (`generation_failed`, `transcription_failed`, `server_error`) are refunded or unpaid, 422 is charged and says so. 401 and 403 keep the intent ("Sign in again, then check again"). Dictation keeps the clip and its key after 402 and 429, as RN ("Try again").
- Every response field is untrusted: costs and balances must be finite and not negative, texts must be strings within a size bound, a generated image's address must pass the same check as an uploaded image's (`UploadNames.isSafeUrl`: https, on the image host, no brackets or whitespace) before it is shown or inserted, suggested tags go through the tag field's own rules.
- Account binding: an intent names its owner, the request uses the owner's session, and nothing is sent while another account is signed in ("This belongs to @owner"). A result is only written into the editor while the editor's owner is signed in (`applyAiResult`, `insertAiImage`, `insertDictation`). The stored intent stays for the owner's return.
- Dictation: the clip's file lives in a folder the system does not purge and that is not backed up (Android `noBackupFilesDir/dictation`, iOS `Application Support/dictation`, excluded from backup), is known by its file name only (the folder's path changes with an app update), is checked for readability and size before it becomes a paid request, and is deleted when its request is settled, given up or interrupted; stray files go when the sheet opens. A clip that was sent and is gone at "Check again" is said plainly (the points may be spent, the transcript cannot be fetched). iOS puts the audio session's category back after a recording. Nothing records while the app is locked or in the background; a recording stops and is thrown away, without any upload or charge, on lock, on leaving the foreground, on an account change and when the sheet closes. The balance line is an addition (RN's sheet shows the rate only). Transport: the upload is one multipart request through the same `AuthHttp` seam (`AuthHttpRequest.bytes`), bounded at 130 s.
- Production forwards `idempotency_key` for assist and images and the points service dedupes on (user, key) (stated by the owner; the local backend checkouts predate it). A "(Free)" label older than a minute is checked again before a new run: when the free allowance is gone meanwhile nothing is sent, and the button shows the cost. Dictation does the same at Stop: a clip stopped under "Stop (Free)" on a price older than a minute is held back when it is no longer free, and only goes out on "Transcribe (N Points)"; "Give up" throws it away uncharged. A paid run needs a balance that was actually read.
- Fake-data and test runs never reach these endpoints: `DemoAuthServices` answers them with synthetic prices, balance and results; iOS fake-data runs use `DemoDictationRecorder` (no microphone).

### Deliberate differences from RN

- Retry after a lost answer: RN checks whether the permlink exists and, when it does, publishes again under a random suffix (a duplicate post). Here the pending publish is remembered with a hash of what was sent: the retry confirms that post, or asks whether to update it or publish a new one.
- Logout clears that account's local drafts (RN keeps them); a wipe clears all of them.
- The posting authority grant asks first and writes the account's `json_metadata` back unchanged (RN grants without asking and writes `posting_json_metadata` into `json_metadata`).
- Reply permlinks keep the base's zero-padded UTC form (RN's unpadded local form collides).
- Leaving an edit with changes asks "Discard changes?" (RN drops them silently), and an edit keeps a recovery copy until the screen is left, so process death does not lose it. An edit is refused when the post changed on chain since the editor opened.
- The local draft is written 300 ms after the last keystroke (RN: 500 ms typing pause, then 300 ms).
- A draft save keeps the author's short description (RN overwrites it with the body summary on every save). As in RN, the description is stored in drafts only and is not part of the published `json_metadata`.
- Beneficiaries need a whole percent from 1 (RN accepts 0, which the chain refuses); the list is sorted by plain byte order (RN's `localeCompare` gives the same order for account names, and hived requires byte order).
- `image_ratios` are known for images uploaded in the session; other images get JSON `null`, as RN writes when it cannot measure one.
- `links_meta` holds the linked post's title (the fields RN reads besides it, `summary` and `image`, do not exist on the SDK's entry).
- RN orders the images with an inconsistent one-argument comparator; V8 leaves the thumbnail first and the rest in order for up to 63 images (golden). With 64 or more, V8's merge path may order the rest differently; here they stay in order.
- The image url validator caps input at 2048 characters; a 429 upload says "Upload quota exceeded" (RN shows the raw message id, the string is missing from its locale file).
- AI assist: suggested tags go through the tag field's rules, ten at most (RN sets whatever the answer holds); a title list that is empty after cleaning shows as text (RN would apply `undefined`). The AI image generator is a sheet over the editor, not a route.
- iOS shows the words count above the toolbar, not in the navigation bar; toolbar buttons are text labels on both platforms, not RN's icon fonts, in the owner's order rather than RN's, and RN's full editor keeps separate AI image and dictation buttons where native lists both inside AI Assist; schedule and poll dates use the system pickers.

Tests of the prompts: shared `AuthorityRecoveryTest`, `RatingPromptTest`, `DigestModelTest`; Android `WavesFlowsTest` (the grant prompt in the composer), `PlayReviewTest` (Play's own fake review manager), `EditorFlowsTest` (the digest sheet); iOS `EditorUITests`.

Tests of the RC precheck: shared `RcCostTest` (golden), `RcPrecheckEditorTest`.

Tests of the video upload: shared `VideoMetaGoldenTest` (RN's `collectVideoThumbUrls`, `restoreVideoThumbs` and `extractMetadata` with a video; `node --no-warnings native/tools/golden/generate-video-meta.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/editor/VideoMetaGoldenVectors.kt`), `TusClientTest` (an in-memory tus server), `VideoUploadModelTest` (also: when a video's file is let go, another image as the thumbnail, no preview behind the lock); Android `EditorFlowsTest` (a recording through a fake camera app, a recording preview player), `VideoPlatformTest` (`VideoCaptures`: owner and screen, saved state, every path that deletes the file); iOS `EditorExtrasTests`, `EditorUITests`.

Tests of the paid features: shared `AiModelsTest`, `DictationModelTest` (fake recorder, scripted endpoint), `TranslateMarkdownTest`, `ComposeTranslateModelTest`; Android `EditorFlowsTest` (assist, image, dictation, translate), `DictationPlatformTest`; iOS `EditorExtrasTests`, `EditorUITests`.

Tests: shared `EditorGoldenTest` (slugs, formats, media placeholders, urls, metadata, edits, beneficiaries, operations with transaction ids and signatures, upload token), `EditorModelTest` (publish, pending publish and conflicts, replies in both surfaces, recovery, cleanup, validation, grant flow, uploads), `PostingGrantTest`, `JsRegexTest`; Android `EditorFlowsTest`, `EditorPlatformTest` (metadata stripping with a generated GPS fixture, orientations, capture cleanup, draft files, backup rules); iOS `EditorTests`, `EditorUITests`.

## Notifications, editor and waves together (wave 3)

What holds across the three areas above; tests in shared `Wave3IntegrationTest`, Android `Wave3NavigationTest` and `NotificationFlowsTest`, iOS `ShellTests`.

- Logout, logout-all and the wipe share one teardown, `AppEnvironment.accountsLeft`, in this order: push (the leaving rows are written to `push.pendingRelease` at once, their requests run in the background), the in-memory session state (`forgetSessionState`, every part cleared on its own), then the local drafts (`LocalDrafts.clear` / `clearAll`, waited for at most 5 s; a delete that is still running finishes by itself). Each step is guarded, so one that throws or never answers does not skip the next, and the whole runs non-cancellable because the vault has already changed. The vault removes an account before the steps that can still fail (choosing the next account, turning the lock off): after such a failure the vault is asked which accounts are really gone, those are torn down, and the error is thrown afterwards. The draft clear first marks the accounts as gone (`LocalDrafts.departed`; all of them for a wipe or logout-all), and from then on `LocalDrafts.save`, the one writer of drafts, reply drafts, recovery copies and pending publishes, refuses them until that account signs in again: the editor or reply sheet the platform closes on the session event that follows flushes its text into nothing. Writes and clears take turns, so a write that got in first is deleted by the clear. The wave composer keeps its text in memory only. Not done: a draft delete that failed or timed out is not retried at the next start (the push release is).
- Account changes: editor and drafts screens need an account, so `NavState.afterAccountChange` drops them with their dialogs and sheets on every switch, sign-out or link login; banners go with `UiMessages.clear`, the notification rows with `NotificationsModel.forgetAccount`. A tapped push that waited behind the lock is judged against the account signed in when it opens: one addressed to another account opens public targets only (a post, a profile, a tag), never the editor. A navigation saved before process death (Android saved state, iOS saved navigation) records its owner, and `NavState.restoredFor` keeps gated screens only for that account; a saved navigation without an owner loses them.
- Notification targets: the `inactive` reminder (list row and tapped push) opens the editor (`AppScreen.Editor`, a new post); `scheduled_published` opens the published post, as RN. Wallet, Boost and Perks targets are still placeholders.
- Timeouts: every private API call (`EcencyPrivateApi.post`: drafts, schedules, snippets, gallery, support settings, bookmarks, favourites, notifications) gives up after 30 s with an `EcencyHttpException` of status 0, since the Android HTTP client has no read, write or call timeout; a write that timed out may have reached the server and callers treat it like any lost answer. The quests call has the same bound. An image upload try has no flat limit: it gives up when no further byte went out and no answer came for 60 s (`ImageUploader.STALL_MILLIS`), at most 10 minutes a try on Android (on iOS the session's 120 s resource timeout ends a try first), and is retried like RN's timeout (RN: a flat 120 s). As in RN, a draft or schedule add is not idempotent: when its answer is lost to a timeout the server may have stored it, and saving again can store a second one.
- Preferences (`Prefs`): new keys are `<area>.<camelCaseName>` (`push.enabled`, `push.typesOff`, `push.pendingRelease`, `content.showImages`, `editor.defaultRewardType`, `rating.*`, `digest.firstPublishOffered.<user>`); the older keys keep RN's names (`customTabs.mainTabs`, `nsfw`; `tts_settings` is no longer read since Listen replaced text to speech, and new keys must not reuse it). "Show Images" applies to notification rows and feed card images (see "Parity gaps (gaps2)"); RN also reads it for post body images, similar entries and the image viewer, which are not done here yet.
- User agent: the build's user agent goes only to the first-party hosts, `ClientIdentity.FIRST_PARTY_HOSTS`: ecency.com and hivesigner.com, each with all of its subdomains (not a list of exact hosts, because the app calls several and the API and image hosts are build settings under ecency.com), https on the default port only. HiveSigner's API is on the list because sign-in goes through it. HiveSigner's pages in the in-app web view (login, signing) are not given the header: a web view's user agent would go to every host the page loads from and to every redirect, so the web view keeps the system's own. That covers every first-party host the app calls: the private API, search, the token endpoint and waves on ecency.com, images.ecency.com and i.ecency.com, translate.ecency.com and poll.ecency.com. Every other host reached through `KtorAuthHttp` (3Speak's embed API, the bad actors list on GitHub) gets no user agent from the app, so the platform's default; another port, a look-alike domain, or a URL with userinfo, a backslash or a control character is never first-party. `KtorAuthHttp` follows no redirect (a 3xx is an error), so the header is never re-sent to a host an Ecency answer names; the image upload client follows none either. The Hive RPC, image upload and websocket clients never set one.
- Transport bound: `KtorAuthHttp` itself gives up after 30 s (`EcencyTimeoutException`), which covers every caller: the token exchange, HiveSigner's API, the private API, search, waves, translation, polls, the Pro and bad actors lists. A sign-in whose token exchange timed out is not retried: the error shows and the button is back after one bound. Uploads keep their stall watchdog.
- Wave images: the composer's image buttons use the editor's upload. `WaveUploadAttacher.install(env)` (both apps, at start) sets `env.waves.attacher` and `env.waves.imageRatios`; the composer screen sets its `onPick` (Android: the system photo picker and the camera through the editor's `FileProvider`; iOS: `PhotoPicker` with a limit of one and `CameraPicker`), prepares the picked file exactly like an editor upload (`EditorImages.prepare`: metadata stripped, 1920 px, off the main thread) and hands it to `picked`. The upload itself is `ImageUploads.upload`, the one signed upload shared with `UploadsModel`: signed for the composer's owner whoever is signed in when it runs, 30 MB guard, three tries, stall timeout, and only a clean https address on the image host is accepted. One image a wave, as RN; a closed picker, an unreadable file, a failed upload or leaving the sheet end the attach and leave the composer as it was. The ratio comes from the picked image's size (as the editor's `image_ratios`); an image pasted as a link has none.
- Publish retries: `HiveApi.content` (`condenser_api.get_content`) is the one "is this permlink on chain, and with which body" read; the editor's pending publish, the reply composer and a wave's retry all ask it before sending again. A new post's permlink that is still taken after five regenerations is refused ("change the title"), never used. As in RN, the wave container is the newest post of the first host that answers with one: a first host that fails falls through to the second. The posting grant is built only when two reads made at that moment agree on every key and account with its weight, the threshold and the memo key, from the newer of the two.
- Waves and the rest: a profile's Waves tab (`ProfileModel.waves()`) empties and loads again on an account change, and a late answer for the previous account never shows. No notification type opens the Waves tab in RN; a reply, mention or vote on a wave opens the post screen, which is the wave's thread (the screen an ecency.com/waves/author/permlink link opens). The player's cookie and storage wipe is skipped while a HiveSigner web view is alive (`WebData.authViews`); the editor and notifications added no other web view (the editor's HiveSigner pages go through the same counted view). A share link's text (`ecency.com/waves?text=`) waits for the composer at most 5 minutes and only for the account it arrived for (signed out: for the next sign-in); a logout, switch, wipe or a login closed without signing in drops it.
- Profile writes, one more known limit: a first posting write that changes nothing hivemind shows (pin to blog on an account whose profile is still legacy only) cannot be confirmed through hivemind, so on blank reads it is refused for 3 minutes and after that the account counts as legacy again; against a node that keeps stripping the row, the next write would then start without that pin.
- Android demo runs (`--ez demoAuth true`, debug builds only) are offline for Hive too: `DemoHiveTransport` answers the read calls from synthetic fixtures and refuses everything else, broadcasts included, so the seeded accounts (which exist on no chain) never reach a real node as an observer. iOS keeps its own `FakeHiveTransport`. Avatars and other images still come from the image host.
- Golden files: regenerating every generator under `tools/golden`, `tools/legacy` and `tools/render` reproduces the committed files, except the values that are random by design and checked by recovery or decryption instead (the SDK's randomised signatures in `GoldenVectors.kt`, `AuthGoldenVectors.kt` and `EditorGoldenVectors.kt`, crypto-js salts in `AuthGoldenVectors.kt` and `LegacyFixtures.kt`).

## Accounts and shell gaps

Parity gaps closed in areas built earlier. Each item starts with what RN does (read from the RN source), then how it is built here.

### Backup keys ("Manage Authorities")

RN `screens/backupKeysScreen` (screen and `importPrivateKeyModal`), its one entry point (`settingsContainer` `BACKUP_PRIVATE_KEYS`) and `getUpdatedUserKeys` in `providers/hive/auth.ts`. Shared `app/BackupKeysModel.kt`, `AccountManager.importKeys`; Android `ui/KeysScreen.kt`; iOS `Screens/KeysViews.swift`.

RN checklist
- [x] Entry: Settings, "Manage Authorities" (signed in only)
- [x] Gate, lock on: the PIN pad first (RN `PINCODE` with `navigateTo`), with the biometric prompt when biometric unlock is on (RN `_processBiometric`)
- [x] Gate, lock off: "Warning", `settings.keys_warning`, Cancel or Set PIN; after the new PIN was set the keys show at once (RN navigates on); a cancelled PIN flow shows the warning again. This is RN's behaviour, kept on purpose: with the lock off, whoever holds the unlocked phone can choose a PIN and then see the keys; the PIN only protects them from then on
- [x] Four rows in RN's order (owner, active, posting, memo), label "<Role> Key (Public)" or "(Private)", one line value with the copy button (RN `TextBoxWithCopy`), toast "Key copied to clipboard"
- [x] Public value: the account's first key of the role on chain (`key_auths[0][0]`, `memo_key`)
- [x] Button per row: "Reveal Private" / "Reveal Public" for a role with a stored private key, "Import Private" for one without
- [x] Import modal: "Enter Password or Private Key", description, "Master password or Private Key" field, Sign. The secret is checked against the account's `get_accounts` row read at that moment: a private key is accepted for each role where its public key is one of the role's `key_auths` with a weight that reaches the role's `weight_threshold` by itself (or is the `memo_key`); a master password is accepted when at least one of its posting, active and memo keys passes that test, and then stores exactly the ones that passed; the password and the owner key are not stored (RN stores the password and all four derived keys). An owner key is refused. Failure alert "Keys Import Failed" with `auth.invalid_credentials` and `login.login_failed_body`; a key that is on the account but cannot sign alone gets its own sentence
- RN derives nothing on this screen: it decrypts what the device stored at sign-in (a master password login stored the password and all four keys). The native vault holds posting, active and memo only, so the owner row shows the public key with "Public only. Ecency never stores the owner key." in place of RN's "Import Private", and has nothing to reveal

Native rules on top (`BackupKeysModel`)
- The keys belong to the account signed in when the screen opened. Every action (reveal, copy, import) checks that it is still that account and that the app is unlocked; `AccountManager.importKeys` checks the current account again inside the vault lock.
- The screen closes, and the private keys leave memory and state first, when the app locks, leaves the foreground (`AppEvent.Backgrounded`, emitted by `AppLockModel.onBackground`), the account changes or the data is wiped. `KeysPhase.CLOSED` tells the UI to leave.
- The route is never part of saved navigation (as before); the model keeps private keys in fields that no `toString` prints (`KeyRow`, `KeyCopy`, state); nothing is logged; the typed import secret is handed over once and not kept. "Dropped from memory" means the references are dropped: keys are immutable strings on both runtimes and cannot be overwritten in place, so their bytes stay in the process heap until the garbage collector (Android) or the allocator (iOS) reuses the memory.
- Owner key and master password: never stored (see "What the app stores" below). The owner row is public only and cannot be imported; an owner key typed into the import of another row is refused with its own sentence.
- Auth type: an imported key never changes how the account signed in. A posting-key account that imports its active or memo key is still a posting-key account that holds one more key; only a master password that gave both the posting and the active key turns a key account into a master-password account (the type names how the account signed in; no password is kept). What an account may do is read from the keys it holds: `VaultAccount.shareablePostingKey` (the `ecency://login` hand-over: a key account's posting key, unless the same key is also its active key) and `VaultAccount.signsWithPostingKey` (image upload signing).
- Android: a rotation or any other configuration change keeps the screen (`AppHost.isChangingConfigurations`); only a real stop closes it. A revealed private key is drawn but replaced, for accessibility services, by "<role>, private key shown" (`password()` semantics), as on iOS.
- Copy: a public key is an ordinary copy. A revealed private key first shows a warning (not in RN) and then goes to the key clipboard: Android `KeyClipboard` (sensitive clip, cleared after 2 minutes), iOS `KeyPasteboard` (local only, 2 minute expiry).
- Screenshots: Android FLAG_SECURE while the screen is on top, and its dialogs are secure windows. iOS has no public switch: key values are drawn in `ScreenShield`, inside the canvas view UIKit uses for secure text entry (`_UITextLayoutCanvasView`), which captures leave out. It fails closed: when a secure field has no subview of that class, nothing is drawn there, and the sheet shows a value only while its box is pressed and held (`HoldToShowValue`; debug `-keysNoShield YES` runs that path). In both modes values are hidden while `UIScreen.isCaptured` (recording, mirroring), and a screenshot (`userDidTakeScreenshotNotification`) turns every revealed private key back to its public key and shows a warning to delete the screenshot. `KeysTests` checks the container's class on the running iOS, the fail-closed path (no label anywhere, nothing drawn) and that the shielded label is laid out and drawn in-process; the simulator screenshot taken by `KeysUITests` shows the rows with empty value boxes.
- App switcher: Android FLAG_SECURE. iOS: the sheet puts its own cover over itself at `willResignActive` (before the system's snapshot) and takes it off at `didBecomeActive`; the lock window's cover is not enough here, since it only exists once the app has locked. The sheet is also closed when the app reaches the background.

What the app stores (owner decision)
- The most the app stores for an account is its active private key, with the posting and memo keys. It never stores the owner private key and never stores the master password. `VaultAccount` has no field for either, so no code path can write one, and the vault writes no `ownerKey` or `masterPassword` entry.
- Sign-in or import with a master password: the posting, active and memo keys are derived, each is checked against the account's authorities (on the account, and able to sign alone by weight and threshold), the ones that pass are stored, and the password and the derived owner key are let go when the check returns. The Ecency session code is signed with the least powerful key that works: the posting key, else the active key, at sign-in and in `ensureKeySession` (RN signs it with the owner key). `authType` is `MASTER_PASSWORD`: it only names how the account signed in, and such an account is stored and treated exactly like a key account holding those keys. Nothing reads a stored password (there is none to read).
- Sign-in or import with an owner key alone is refused with a clear message; a key that is the owner key and also the active or posting key is kept for that other role. A master password whose posting, active and memo keys are no longer the account's (only the owner key still matches) is refused the same way.
- Owner operations are never signed in the app, from links or from screens.
- Entries of earlier builds: `KeyVault.dropRetiredSecrets()` runs at every app start, before the lock state is read. It rewrites any entry that still has an owner key or a master password without them, keeps posting, active and memo keys, tokens and the auth type as they are, does nothing to a clean vault, and leaves an entry it cannot read or write alone without stopping at it: the entries after it are still cleaned, and that one is tried again at the next start. Only a lost device key ends the pass. Reading an old entry before the cleaning never returns either secret.
- An old entry with a master password and without one of the three keys: the missing keys are derived from the password, offline, in the same single write that drops it (the vault is protected by the device key, not by the PIN, so this needs no unlock). They are not checked against the chain at that moment: a derived key that is no longer on the account behaves like any stale key, the chain refuses the signature and the user signs in again. This is the same state RN left such an account in.
- RN import (`LegacyMigration`): the RN master password and owner key are read only to derive missing keys and to name the auth type; neither is written to the vault. An RN account whose only credential is an owner key (no token, no other key) is skipped and its owner signs in again; one that also has a token or another key is imported without the owner key.
- An account left with a token and no key (an old owner-key sign-in) stays signed in to Ecency; the keys screen's import adds a posting or active key.
- Notices (`app/AccountNotices.kt`, an alert with OK on both apps, shown with the unlocked app, first among the root prompts, see "Root prompts, teardown and transport after the merge"):
  - when the cleaning or the RN import leaves a key account with no posting and no active key: "Sign in again as @name", saying that the owner key and the master password are no longer kept and pointing to a new sign-in or to Manage Authorities. Shown once: the account's name waits in the device preferences (`accounts.noSigningKeyNotice`) until the notice was dismissed, so a start that ended before the user saw it shows it at the next one; it is dropped when the account signs in again, leaves the device, or the device is wiped. An RN account that had nothing but an owner key is not imported at all, so there is no account left to name: its owner finds the app signed out.
  - after a master password sign-in that gave fewer than posting and active: which role the password no longer matches, what is stored, and what the app cannot do until that key is imported.
  - after a sign-in with a key that is also the account's owner key: see the next point.
  - "posting key is required to perform this transaction" (RN's sentence) is followed by "Add it under Settings, Manage Authorities (Import Private), or sign in again with it."
- A typed key that is the account's owner key and also its active or posting key (one key for several roles): kept for the non-owner role, with a warning after the sign-in and a red line on the keys screen for as long as the chain shows the shared key, both asking to give that role a key of its own. OPEN for the owner of the project: refuse such a key instead. It is one switch, `KeyLogin.refuseKeySharedWithOwner` (false now); when true, sign-in and import refuse with "This key is also this account's owner key..." and nothing is stored.

Deliberate differences from RN
- RN stores the master password and the owner key, signs in with an owner key, and offers "Import Private" on the owner row; none of that here.
- A private key is copied only after a warning, into an expiring clipboard (RN copies at once into the plain clipboard).
- A HiveSigner or HiveAuth account keeps its sign-in method and its tokens after an import. RN's `getUpdatedUserKeys` means to (`_prevAuthType`), but reads the auth type from the wrong object, so there the account turns into a key account, and it also overwrites the stored access token with an empty one.
- A private key is stored for every role it matches on chain (RN: only the last matched role), the same rule as the native key login.
- The account's auth type is kept on import (RN switches it to the imported key's type; see "Auth type" above).
- A key whose weight is below its role's threshold (or whose weight or threshold is zero or less) is refused in all three places a key is taken: at sign-in, at import, and in the auth upgrade sheet (`SigningModel.submitUpgradeKey`, which calls `KeyLogin.keyMatchesRole`; nothing is held for such a key). The message is "This key cannot sign for the account on its own...". RN stores it and every broadcast then fails on chain.
- Without the node the public value falls back to what the stored private key derives to (RN shows the cached account).
- iOS shows the screen as a sheet and the import as an alert with a secure field.

Tests: shared `BackupKeysModelTest` (gate, biometrics, rows, copy, import and what it keeps, closing, account binding), `KeyLoginTest` (weights and thresholds); Android `KeysFlowsTest`, `LoginPrivacyTest` (FLAG_SECURE); iOS `KeysTests`, `KeysUITests`. Mutation-checked (each made a test fail): the owner check of every action, closing on background, on lock and on account change, the PIN result, the copy warning and the "hidden again" check, the cancelled set-PIN flow, the import's current-account check and its "keep the sign-in method" rule, biometrics offered in a settings flow. One survivor: dropping `role in privateKeys` from the row's "revealed" flag changes nothing, because `toggle` never marks a role without a stored key.

Shared `app/Welcome.kt`, `UsernameRules.kt`, `RegisterModel.kt` (with `TurnstilePage`), `api/ecency/OnboardingApi.kt`; Android `ui/RegisterScreen.kt`; iOS `Screens/RegisterViews.swift`.

RN checklist
- [x] Welcome shows when no app version was stored or the stored one is older (first launch and after each update), `parseVersionNumber` compared; "Get started!" needs the terms box and stores the version and the acceptance; after an update the box starts ticked (`WelcomeGate`). Copy and "Read Here!" (terms page) as RN
- [ ] Welcome art: RN's mascot image, line icons and the 3.5 s launch animation over the screen are not drawn
- [x] Register form: "Pick a username", email, "Referred user (Optional)", the terms line, Continue, "Already have an account? Login Here"
- [x] Username: lowercased as typed; RN's rules in RN's order (3 to 16, dot separated segments of 3+, starts with a letter, letters, digits and single hyphens, no trailing hyphen, no underscore), then the exchange look-alike check and the `uid` + digits check, each with RN's message; then, 500 ms after the last keystroke, `condenser_api.lookup_accounts [name, 50]` and "Username already exists, try a different one" when the name is in the answer
- [x] Offline the name is not accepted (no message, as RN); a failed lookup shows "Error / An error occurred"
- [x] Email: RN's pattern; referral: empty, or an account the same lookup finds
- [x] Continue is disabled as RN, and checks the rules again on tap
- [x] "Get Hive Account": the name and email, the free card with the Turnstile challenge and "Register Free" (disabled until a token arrived), "Registering Your Account...", then "Registration Successful / Check your email for instruction" with Continue (back to the app, mail app opened)
- [x] Free signup: `POST /private-api/account-create {username, email, referral, captcha_token}`, no account token. Failure copy as RN: 500 `register.500_error`; a backend `code` through `register.error_codes`, else the backend's message; anything else "An error occurred"; always under "Fail!", and a new challenge afterwards
- [x] Turnstile: `https://ecency.com/embed/turnstile` in a web view whose top document may only be ecency.com or challenges.cloudflare.com, sub-frames free (RN `_shouldStartLoad`); bridge messages `verify` / `expire` / `error`; a failed page load shows "Failed to load the page / Retry" without asking for a new challenge
- [x] Entry points: "Sign up now!" and the header button on the login screen ("Sign up", `register.title`, plain weight so the filled LOGIN stays the one primary action; RN says "JOIN NOW"), "Create a new account" in the account switcher, and `ecency://signup` / `ecency.com/signup` links (referral, username and email prefilled and validated like typed values)
- [ ] Paid account (in-app purchase `999accounts`, "Buy Account - {price}", "Claim Paid Account", the `purchaseOnly` link mode): not built, in-app purchases come in a later wave. The card shows RN's title and description with "Buying an account is not available in this version of the app yet." and no button

How it is built
- `UsernameRules` and the email pattern are pinned to RN's own functions: `node --no-warnings native/tools/golden/generate-signup.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/app/SignupGoldens.kt` (from the RN root; 118 names, 48 addresses, 17 version strings).
- The signup request is sent once per tap and never retried: a second tap while one runs does nothing, a lost answer is a failure the user sees, and every failure drops the single use captcha token.
- A bridge message counts only from the main frame while the web view's own document is the embed page, and only in RN's three shapes; a token is a short string without white space. Android provides `ReactNativeWebView` (the name the hosted page posts to) through an origin-bound message listener (`WebViewCompat.addWebMessageListener`, `https://ecency.com` only, androidx.webkit 1.12.1, a Java library), so Cloudflare's frames never see the object; on a WebView older than version 83 it falls back to a JavaScript interface, which every frame can reach, and only the top document check and the token's shape filter there. iOS maps the name onto one script message handler, injected into the main frame only.
- Web data: iOS runs the challenge in a non-persistent data store. Android runs it in its own WebView profile (`signup-challenge`) where the WebView supports profiles; on older WebViews it shares the app's one cookie jar with HiveSigner's pages and the video players (the challenge then counts as an auth view, so a closing player does not wipe cookies under it).
- Debug fake-data runs set `env.captchaStandIn`, so a demo never loads the hosted page; the demo services answer the signup with an empty 200 and create nothing.
- The register screen's saved navigation holds only the referral (a public account name). A link's username and email wait in memory (`env.signupPrefill`) and are taken once.

Deliberate differences from RN
- RN's header shows a "LOGIN" button that does nothing; it is left out ("Login Here" below does it).
- RN shows the avatar of a taken username inside the field; here only the message shows.
- A stale lookup answer never judges a newer name (RN applies whichever answer arrives last).
- The availability lookup is not cached (the SDK caches each name forever in the session, so a name taken meanwhile still reads as free there).
- A link's referral is taken only when it is a well-formed account name.
- iOS shows the register screen as a sheet, like login.

Tests: shared `SignupTest` (23: the four golden sets, welcome gate, form, link prefill, signup request and failures, captcha, Turnstile rules); Android `RegisterFlowsTest`; iOS `OnboardingUITests` (file of the same name), `TurnstileRulesTests` (in `EcencyAppTests/KeysTests.swift`).

RN checklist
- [x] Entry: side menu "Refer & Earn" (signed in)
- [x] "Earned Points" = rewarded x 100, "Pending Points" = (total - rewarded) x 100 from `GET /private-api/referrals/<user>/stats`
- [x] "Refer": the share sheet with `https://ecency.com/signup?referral=<user>`
- [x] List from `GET /private-api/referrals/<user>`, next pages by `max_id` = the last row's id, loaded near the end; pull to refresh; footer spinner; "Refer your loved ones today and earn free points" when empty
- [x] Row: `$$` (blue once rewarded; RN's popover text "Referral points rewarded" / "Referral reward pending" shows as a toast), avatar, name, date in the device locale, "Delegate HP"; a tap on the row opens the quick profile
- [ ] "Delegate HP" opens the transfer placeholder with the account as its argument: RN opens the transfer screen in delegation mode, which is the wallet wave's

Rules: both requests are public GETs (no token, as RN's no-token list). The list belongs to the account signed in when it loaded: an account change empties it and loads the new account's, a late answer for the previous account is dropped, and the UIs draw rows only while `ReferState.account` is the signed-in account. A page that brings no new row ends the list.

Deliberate differences: a failed list shows the error with "Try again?" (RN shows the empty hint); without the stats the points read 0 and the list still shows.

Tests: shared `ReferModelTest` (11); iOS `OnboardingUITests.testReferShowsPointsRowsAndTheShareButton`. Android: see the Refer case in `GapsFlowsTest`.

### Delete account

RN `screens/settings` (the row, signed in only), `settingsContainer._handleDeleteAccount`, `providers/ecency/ecency.ts` `deleteAccount`. Shared `DeleteAccountModel` in `app/ShellPrompts.kt`, `OnboardingApi.requestDelete`; Android `ui/ShellPrompts.kt`; iOS `Screens/ShellPromptsViews.swift`.

What RN does: Settings, "Delete Account" opens one confirmation ("Confirm Delete", "Are you sure you want to delete account? It will erase all of your data", Cancel / Delete). Delete calls `POST /private-api/request-delete {username, data: "", code}` (the account's token, added by RN's API interceptor), then shows the toast "Your request for deletion is sent" and dispatches `logout()`: the normal sign-out of the current account. It is a request to Ecency to delete what it stores for the account; nothing is broadcast, nothing else is deleted on the device beyond what a sign-out removes, and a Hive account cannot be deleted.

- [x] The row, the one confirmation and its copy, the request and its body, the toast, the sign-out
- [x] The sign-out is `SessionModel.logout(<that account>)`, so the one teardown (`AppEnvironment.accountsLeft`: push release, in-memory state, local drafts) runs, and the next saved account becomes current, as RN
- Rules: the request names the account that opened the confirmation and carries that account's code; it is refused when another account is signed in by the time Delete is tapped; it is sent once and never retried (a second tap while it runs does nothing); the confirmation closes on an account change; a request already on its way still signs out its own account, by name, after a switch.

Deliberate difference: when the request fails, RN still shows "Your request for deletion is sent" and signs the user out. Here a failed request shows "Fail! Your request for deletion could not be sent. Please try again." and the account stays signed in, so nobody is told a request was sent that was not. A request that timed out counts as failed; trying again may reach the server a second time, which asks for the same thing.

Tests: shared `ShellPromptsTest` (delete cases); Android `GapsFlowsTest`; iOS `ShellPromptsUITests` (`EcencyAppUITests/ShellPromptsUITests.swift`).

### App shell: update prompt and announcements

RN `applicationContainer._compareAndPromptForUpdate` with `providers/github`, `utils/parseVersionNumber.ts`; `providers/queries/announcementsQueries.ts`, `utils/announcementAction.ts`, the `announcementsMeta` reducer. Shared `UpdateModel`, `UpdateLinks`, `AnnouncementsModel` in `app/ShellPrompts.kt`.

Update prompt, RN checklist
- [x] Source: RN uses no update library and no remote config; it reads the latest GitHub release of the app's repository (`GET https://api.github.com/repos/ecency/vision-mobile/releases/latest`, `tag_name`). Same call here
- [x] Decision: at app start, skipped while "Remind Later" was tapped under 48 hours ago; asks when `parseVersionNumber(tag) > parseVersionNumber(app version)`; any failure is silent
- [x] Prompt: "Update available v<version>", "Using latest version of Ecency gives you the best experience", "Remind Later" (stores the time), "Update Now" (stores nothing, so the next start asks again until the app is updated)
- [x] "Update Now" target by installer, as RN, for the app id: Android `market://details?id=app.esteem.mobile.android` for a Play install (the Play web listing when nothing takes `market://`), else the GitHub releases page; iOS the App Store page for an App Store install, else the releases page
- [ ] RN's header image on the prompt is not drawn

Differences: on iOS the store page comes from Apple's lookup by bundle id (`itunes.apple.com/lookup?bundleId=app.esteem.mobile.ios`) at the tap (RN hard-codes the listing's numeric id 1451896376, which is now this app's too and could replace the lookup); the answer must be an https page on apps.apple.com, else the releases page opens. A tag counts only when it is the build's tag prefix followed by plain `X.Y.Z`: `v4.1.0`, `4.1`, `4.1.0-beta` or anything else is ignored (RN reads any tag with `parseInt`).

Release process: the source is a build setting, `ecency.updateReleasesUrl` and `ecency.updateTagPrefix` in `native/local.properties` (Android also `ECENCY_UPDATE_RELEASES_URL`, `ECENCY_UPDATE_TAG_PREFIX`), read by `UpdateSource.of`; only an https `api.github.com/repos/...` URL is taken. The default is RN's: `ecency/vision-mobile` releases with no prefix. Since native 4.x is the update of the RN app, that default is right: a plain `4.x.y` release prompts RN 3.x users to update (to native) and native users on older 4.x, and there are no further RN releases to confuse it. A prefix or another repository is only needed if RN 3.x releases continue in parallel.

Announcements, RN checklist
- [x] `GET /private-api/announcements` without a token, cached for an hour (SDK `staleTime`)
- [x] Skipped on the first launch of a new version (the welcome screen's turn); runs again when the welcome was accepted and on every account change
- [x] The first announcement that is not a proposal prompt (`proposal_ids`); nothing when that one needs a signed-in user (`auth`) and there is none
- [x] Memory per announcement and account (`<id>_<account or guest>`): not shown again once its button was used, and not within 48 hours after "Later" or after it was closed
- [x] Shown 3 s after it was chosen: title, description, "Later" and the announcement's button text
- [x] Button: RN's rule (a link starting with `https://` as it is, anything else as a path on `https://ecency.com`), the server's `ops` signing blob never read, then marked as processed

Untrusted payload: rows without an id or without text are dropped; texts lose control and bidi override characters and are capped (title 200, description 2000, button 60); a link over 2048 characters or with a control character is no link. A link is either absolute, starting with `https://`, or a path starting with exactly one `/`, which gets `https://ecency.com` in front. Anything else is dropped and the button only closes the prompt: RN prefixes any text with `https://ecency.com`, which turns `.evil.example/x` into `https://ecency.com.evil.example/x`. Absolute links are not limited to Ecency's hosts (RN does not limit them either, and announcements link to partner pages); they open in the system's in-app browser (Custom Tabs, SFSafariViewController), never in the app; a page there that redirects to an app link (`ecency://`, `hive://`) is handled like any web page doing so, and the link handler's own confirmations are the guard. The link must pass `SafeTarget.httpUrl` as https and then opens either an in-app content screen (`ContentLinks`: post, profile, tag, community, waves) or the in-app browser. It never goes through the auth link handler, so an announcement cannot start a sign-in, a signature, a transfer or a signup (RN hands the URL to its general link processor). The memory lives in `Prefs` (`announcements.meta`, the newest 50 entries).

Account rules: a prompt belongs to the account it was chosen for; an account change closes it without marking it and the choice runs again for the new account; a prompt chosen during the 3 s for an account that left is not shown; the update prompt goes first when both are due.

Debug: Android `--ez demoAuth true --ez demoAnnouncement true` / `--ez demoUpdate true`; iOS `-fakeData YES -demoAnnouncement YES` / `-demoUpdate YES`.

Tests: shared `ShellPromptsTest` (with the delete cases); Android `GapsFlowsTest`; iOS `ShellPromptsUITests` (`EcencyAppUITests/ShellPromptsUITests.swift`).

Daily check-in, RN checklist
- [x] Recorded when the Waves tab is on screen (first shown, each time the app comes back to it, and after an unlock: the tab is not composed while the lock hides the app, and Android's effect is keyed on the lock state as well): `POST /private-api/usr-activity {ty: 10, code}`, at most once per 15 minutes and account, the window claimed before the request and given back when it fails
- [ ] RN also records it when a post is opened (the reading area), retries a failed request twice and replays failed activities at the next start; here a failed check-in is simply tried again at the next visit. The backend records at most one per window either way

Wave composer
- [x] Poll: RN's wave composer has the poll button; it opens the editor's poll wizard (`PollSheet`, the editor's `PollDraft`), and the wave is published with the poll: the poll is part of the content permlink key (`JSON.stringify(pollDraft)` in RN's key order) and of `json_metadata` (`content_type: "poll"` first, the poll fields before `type`), then removed from the composer
- [x] Video: done by the editor media area (row W11a above): `WaveComposeModel.video()` uploads, `setVideoEmbed` takes only a 3Speak embed address (or null to remove the video). A wave may carry a poll and a video together, as RN (`submitWave(body, pollDraft, videoThumbUrl)`): the poll is part of the permlink key and the metadata, the thumbnail joins `image` and is not part of the key

Account rules: the card and its latches belong to one account; an account change drops the card before the next account's data is read, a late answer for the previous account is ignored, and a latch is written only for the account that earned it. The check-in carries the code of the account signed in at that moment and is never sent while signed out or locked.

Golden vectors: `node --no-warnings native/tools/golden/generate-waves-gaps.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/app/WavesGapsGoldens.kt` (from the RN root: `isNewAccount`, 35 derivation cases, five waves with a poll, two of them with a video as well, through RN's `extractMetadata`, `makeJsonMetadata` and `generateContentBasedPermlink`).

Tests: shared `WavesGapsTest` (18); Android `GapsFlowsTest` (waves cases); iOS `WavesGapsUITests` (`EcencyAppUITests/WavesGapsUITests.swift`).

### Session start

`SessionModel.start()` is called by each app at launch and again by `AppLockModel.start()` after an RN migration. It never runs two reads of the vault at once: a call while a load runs asks for one more read after it, so the state always comes from the last read; one app start counts as one session for the rating rule. `awaitStartFinished()` suspends until no such load runs (tests use it instead of sleeping).

### Mutation checks for these gaps

Each guard below was removed or weakened on a green baseline and a test failed (shared host tests; the backup keys guards are listed in their own section):

- Register: the single signup request per tap, the captcha token requirement, the token dropped after a failure, the rules re-checked before the backend, the stale lookup, the offline rule, tokens only while the options are open, the challenge page's host pin, messages only from the embed page, the link referral's account name check, the welcome consent and the "older version shows again" rule.
- Refer: the reload for the new account, the list emptied on an account change, the repeated page ending the list. A late answer is stopped three times over (the reload cancels the running page, the account change cancels it, the answer is checked against the account): removing one of them alone changes nothing, removing the cancel on change together with the check fails `aLateAnswerAfterSigningOutNeverShows`.
- Delete account: refused for another account, the confirmation closed on an account change, one request however often Delete is tapped, no close under a running request, no sign-out after a failure, the sign-out by name of the account that asked.
- Update prompt: the 48 hour window, "higher version only", the tag reduced to a version, the App Store page host.
- Announcements: the new version launch skip, the signed-in rule, proposals skipped, the processed and 48 hour memory, the close on an account change, the https and safe link rule, texts cleaned and capped. The account re-check after the 3 s delay is the second of two guards (the account change also cancels the pending prompt): removing it alone changes nothing, removing both fails `aPromptChosenForOneAccountIsNotShownToTheNext`.
