- Review round: the announcement link's leading-slash rule and its second-slash rule; the import keeping the auth type and a master password becoming the master type; the posting-key hand-over refusing a key that is also the active key, and non-key accounts; upload signing by stored keys; the weight against the threshold, at sign-in, at import and in the auth upgrade's key check; an unreadable threshold or weight not counting as 1; the plain `X.Y.Z` tag rule, the tag prefix, the GitHub-only source and the configured source being the one asked; the session start's single load, its second read after a call during a load, and one session per app start; `hideAll`. Android: a configuration change not counting as a background while a real stop still closes the keys, the private key hidden from accessibility, the challenge bridge's main-frame and origin checks. One survivor: the Android check-in effect's lock key, because the Waves tab is not composed while the lock hides the app, so the effect starts fresh after every unlock anyway (`aCheckInRefusedBehindTheLockIsRecordedAfterTheUnlock` passes either way).
- Second review round: the auth upgrade refusing a key below the threshold (through `SigningModel`); the cleaning going on after an entry it cannot read, in both orders, and still ending on a lost device key; the notice for an account left without a signing key (its trigger, its "key accounts only" rule and its persistence until dismissed), the missing-role notice and the shared-owner notice; zero or negative weights and thresholds; posting before active for the session code, at sign-in and in `ensureKeySession`; the shared-owner switch.
- Storage rule: the derived owner key kept out of a master sign-in's result, an owner key alone refused, a master password keeping only the keys it was verified for, the vault writing no owner key or master password, the cleaning dropping both and keeping posting, active and memo, the derivation of missing keys before the password goes, a stored key never replaced by a derived one, the RN import writing neither, the owner row without import, owner operations refused before any key or prompt.
- Waves: the latch only for the wave's author, items staying latched, Dismiss, the one celebration, the check-in window, no check-in while locked, the window given back after a failure, only a valid poll attaches, the poll in the permlink key, the video address rule (a 3Speak embed only). One survivor: clearing the card's state at the start of a reload is repeated by the next line once the session is known, so removing it changes nothing.

## Root prompts, teardown and transport after the merge

What the accounts and media gaps needed from each other.

- **One order for the prompts that open by themselves** (`app/RootPrompts.kt`, `RootPromptRule`, `RootPromptsModel`, `env.rootPrompts`): a notice about an account's keys, the update prompt, an announcement, the email digest offer, in that order. The prompt on screen stays until it is answered (an update that arrives while the digest sheet is open waits). Nothing shows while the app is locked or before the welcome screen is done (each app's root reports the latter with `env.setWelcomeDone`, closed until it does), over any PIN pad (the confirmation before the keys or a link, the settings flows), over the keys screen (`BackupKeysModel` counts itself in `env.keysScreens` from its start until it shuts) or under the "sign in again" prompt of a reset secure storage; the prompt that was showing comes back afterwards. Both apps draw only `RootPromptState.prompt` (Android `ShellPrompts`, `DigestSheetHost`; iOS `ShellPromptsHost`, `DigestSheetHost`). The store rating prompt is the system's own: it is only asked for when the app is in front, unlocked, past the welcome screen and no root prompt is pending; otherwise the one request stays unspent (`RootPromptsModel.mayAskForRating`). So a first publish that opens the digest offer does not also ask for a rating. `RootPromptsTest`.
- **One teardown** (`AppEnvironment.accountsLeft`: logout, logout-all, the wipe). Besides push, memory and local drafts it now covers what the media area stores: the unanswered paid requests (AI assist, AI image, dictation) live in the account's local store and go with it, and `LocalDrafts` refuses to write them again for an account that left; the leaving accounts' dictation clips are deleted (`DictationClips`: a clip's file name starts with a tag for its owner, the first 8 bytes of the SHA-256 of the account name, so no name is on a path; another account's waiting clip and its paid request stay, and its retry uses the same idempotency key; clips without a tag, from an earlier build, go on any departure; a wipe removes all); a dictation sheet still open for an account that left deletes its clip on the session change and keeps none when it closes; a video upload model lets its file and upload place go as soon as its owner left, also with its editor still open. The shared camera folder (Android `cacheDir/camera`) is emptied by a wipe only: one account's logout leaves it, another account may be mid-capture, and each capture is deleted by whoever holds it. `DictationModelTest`, `VideoUploadModelTest`.
- **Redirects** (`EcencyHttpException.ofSendFailure`, `isRedirect`): the transport follows none; the refusal now keeps its status (3xx) through every caller instead of becoming status 0 "network error" (search and signup carry it in their own errors), the token exchange does not retry it, a tus upload ends on it at once (as on any definite 4xx other than 404, 408, 409, 423, 429 and 460), and the user reads "The server sent an unexpected redirect..." (`AppError.UNEXPECTED_REDIRECT`). `KtorAuthHttpTest` (GET, HEAD and POST for 301, 302, 307 and 308).
- **Keys**: the vault holds no owner key and no master password. The posting authority grant (the editor's schedule prompt, the quick post recovery) needs the active authority: a posting-only account gets the upgrade sheet and its key must reach the account's active threshold, a master password account signs with its stored active key without a prompt, a HiveSigner account gets a prompt and its token is never used for it. `AuthorityRecoveryTest`.
- **Video files**: Android's photo sweep (an hour) never deletes a recording; a recording is deleted by the upload model that holds it, and every leftover goes at the next cold start, so "Continue upload" is an offer of this run. If the file is gone all the same, the upload ends with "This recording is no longer available on the device..." and no offer to continue.
- **Account notices**: a name whose vault entry could not be read stays on the pending list for the next start; an account list that could not be read drops no notice; one notice of a kind per account; a tap answers the notice it was shown for (by id); a HiveSigner or HiveAuth sign-in voids the "no signing key" notice, and a key entry that holds neither a posting nor an active key becomes a HiveSigner account on a HiveSigner sign-in (its memo key stays).
- **A wipe leaves no name behind** (`AccountPrefs`, run by `resetAfterWipe` before its event): the preferences that name an account or what it read go (`digest.firstPublishOffered.<user>`, `waves.onboarding.<user>`, `translate-dismissed:<post>`; `Prefs.keys()` lists them); device settings without a name stay (the rating counters, `waves.voteWeightPercent`). Android also clears the signup challenge's own WebView profile (cookies, web storage, then the profile itself when no view uses it) when the register screen leaves and on a wipe (`TurnstileBridge.clearProfile`). `AppLockModelTest`, `RegisterFlowsTest`, `LockFlowsTest`.
- **Android "Record Video"** (`VideoCaptures`): an account change drops and deletes the recording still open in the camera app too, and whatever the camera writes afterwards is deleted when its result arrives (also after a new process); a second "Record Video" while the camera is open answers `Start.BUSY` and launches nothing (the sheet says "A recording is already in progress..."); a recording is tagged with its screen kind plus an id of the editor or composer instance (saved with the screen), so it never lands in another editor of the same account. `VideoPlatformTest`.
- **The digest offer's post count**: the read before a publish is bounded at 3 s (`DigestModelTest` holds the transport and checks the virtual clock, so the bound is the digest's and not the RPC client's), and an account whose read failed or timed out is not waited for again in this app run.
- **Paid sheets**: one balance format everywhere (`PaidCalls.balance`: "1,250.500 POINTS"); the AI image generator opened on its own is in the gated route list of `Wave3IntegrationTest`, and its stored intent and 130 s wait have their own tests (`AiModelsTest`).
- **Copy and looks**: the posting permission confirmation names `@ecency.app` and the active key (RN's three benefits and its security note stay); the wave composer's PUBLISH is off, and grey, while there is nothing to publish (`WaveComposeState.publishEnabled`; RN's `canPublish` is unchanged); Android's dictation sheet holds the room of its three late lines from the first frame; the standalone AI image screen has one title.
- The editor no longer shows "Could not store the draft on this device" when the save was refused because the account left.
- Android Compose tests that wait on background work use `ComposeTestRule.await` (`ComposeAwait.kt`): it runs the main looper each round and has no 10 s wall-clock cap (`SliceFlowsTest`).

## RN changes after the spike base (net-sync)

RN `feef7d110b..3fc267058e` (17 commits). Ported here:

- **A token renewal never writes after a removal** (RN `storage/authWriteGuard`, 4ebf0f3da4 b3102e1dd7 db5b539d43, and the token parts of 550bf29f21 f035ab83a0). Native had most of it already: every vault write and every removal run under `AccountManager`'s mutex, and a session is never written for an account that is gone (`saveSession` refuses, it never creates the entry). What was open: a renewal answered after a sign-out followed by a sign-in of the same account wrote the old token lineage over the new session, and a failed renewal of an account that just left could still raise "session expired". Now `remove` and `logoutAll` bump removal marks when they finish, and the PIN wipe bumps them before it removes anything (`PinLock.beforeWipe`) and again after (`resetAfterWipe`), as RN's `withAuthRemoval`; `refreshSession` and `ensureKeySession` take the mark before their first suspension and write, under the mutex, only when it has not moved, returning null otherwise. `AccountManagerTest` (the renewal is held open with `FakeAuthHttp.beforeAnswer` while the account leaves). Listen's renewals go through it (see "Listen").
- **Waves the API flags as explicit** (d9f64de0bc): `WaveEntry.flaggedNsfw` (only a JSON `true`), `WaveNsfw.isHidden` (hidden unless NSFW is "Always show", also under "Always warn" since waves have no placeholder; never the viewer's own wave), applied in every waves list including promoted waves. The shorts reel keeps its own filter (RN changed only `useWavesQuery`). `WavesModelsTest`.

Later waves (not ported): Spin (ab9a588a82, 184d50d2c8, 764e5a24d9), chat groups and reactors (4e8ea6078e, 03c324070b, 144e088763, bce22f3c39).

## Listen (natural-voice post audio)

RN 760b9070be with b4e1245dca, 30fb0c17de, aa897cc190, f035ab83a0 and 550bf29f21 (`components/listen/listenBar.tsx`, `listenAudio.tsx`, `utils/listen.ts`, `hooks/useRefreshAccessToken.ts`, SDK 2.4.26 `getListenStatusQueryOptions` and `useListenUnlock`). It replaced on-device text to speech: the Listen column and the settings cog left the reading bar, the TTS settings sheet and its helpers are gone (here `SpeechUi.kt`, `SpeechPlayer`, `TtsSettingsSheet`, `TtsSettings`, `Speech.chunks` and `Speech.detectLanguage`; the plain text, word count and reading time stay, as RN keeps them).

Shared: `api/ecency/ListenApi.kt` (status and unlock, the answer read as untrusted: only a JSON `true` unlocks, a track needs a language and an https URL), `app/Listen.kt` (`Listen` helpers, `ListenPlayerState`, `ListenBarState`, `ListenModel`, `SessionRenewals`), demo answers in `DemoAuthServices` (fake-post-1 ready, fake-post-2 at 42 %, others locked; nothing is charged, the audio URLs lead nowhere). Android: `ui/ListenUi.kt` (`ListenController`, `ListenSection`), `ListenPlayback.kt` (`ListenPlaybackService`, `MediaListenEngine`). iOS: `Screens/ListenViews.swift` (`AVListenEngine`, `ListenNowPlaying`, `ListenAudioController`, `ListenScreen`, `ListenSection`).

### RN behaviour checklist

- [x] The bar sits above the post body, after the reading bar (RN `postDisplayView`). Nothing for comments (the status is not even asked), posts without a body, posts the server calls `unsupported`, and posts mostly in a script the voice cannot read (Thai, Bengali, Hebrew, Tamil, Telugu, Chinese without kana or hangul; links and image markup left out) until the server's analysis finds a voice. No minimum length (b4e1245dca).
- [x] Status `POST /private-api/listen-status {code?, author, permlink, updated?}`: anonymous without a session token; with one, the reader's own answer with signed track URLs. Polled every 5 s while `preparing` or `analyzing`, then it stops. Three retries 1, 2 and 4 s apart on other errors (React Query's defaults).
- [x] "Listen to this post", "English + German · ~3 min" (the longest track, else the word count at 150 a minute), "· Audio could not be made, your Points were refunded" for a failed job. Log in for a visitor (the login screen), a spinner while a reader's status loads, "Unlock · 50 Points".
- [x] Unlock: a confirmation first ("Spend 50 Points to listen to this post? The audio is yours to replay any time."), never on one tap. `POST /private-api/listen {code, author, permlink, idempotency_key, updated?}`; the answer is the new status at once, then polling goes on from it. A transport error or a gateway status (502, 504, 520, 522, 524) is sent again with the same key twice, 2 s apart (the SDK's rule). The key is kept until the outcome is known: a 4xx clears it, a lost answer keeps it, so the next tap is a replay and never a second charge. 402: "Not enough Points" / "Listening needs 50 Points and you have 3." with Get Points (the Boost route, as RN). Any other failure: "Fail!" / "Could not unlock the audio, please try again". After a failed unlock the status is asked again (the SDK's invalidation).
- [x] Preparing: the server's percentage once the worker has the job (capped at 99), else "Waiting in line, N ahead…" / "Next in line…", else "Preparing audio, about N min…" (0.3 s of work per second of audio), else "Preparing audio…".
- [x] Player: play and pause (nothing loads before the first play; from then the source stays while paused, so the media controls stay), the elapsed time, a seek slider, the length, a speed chip 1x, 1.25x, 1.5x, 1.75x, 2x, 2.5x and back, a chip per language when there are several (the reader's interface language first, else the main one; a switch starts the other track from its start), the lock screen and notification following both ways (not while loading or seeking), back to the start at the end.
- [x] Expired URL: the status is asked again for fresh URLs and playback picks up where it failed; after three renewals that did not help it stops with "The audio could not be loaded." and "Try again" (which starts the count over). The count starts over once playback runs 30 s past the point that failed (30fb0c17de). A failed or track-less renewal is an error, not a silent player (b4e1245dca).
- [x] Tokens (aa897cc190, f035ab83a0, 550bf29f21): a status refused with 401 renews the access token once and asks again with it, and is allowed to renew again after a failed renewal; an unlock refused with 401 renews and resends the same request (same key) once; the player renews the token when fresh URLs do not come with the old one. A renewal that brings nothing newer than the token in use ends it.
- [x] Background playback with lock-screen and notification controls: iOS `UIBackgroundModes: audio`, the `.playback` session (plays with the silent switch on), `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` (title, @author, Ecency, length, position, rate) and `MPRemoteCommandCenter` (play, pause, toggle, seek). Android: Media3 `MediaSessionService` (`ListenPlaybackService`, not exported, `foregroundServiceType="mediaPlayback"`) with `FOREGROUND_SERVICE` and `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK`; the app's player is a `MediaController`, the media notification and the lock screen come from the session.
- [x] One audio source app-wide: Listen goes through the 3Speak voice players' coordinator (`SpeakPlayback`), so starting either pauses the other.
- [x] Removed with RN: on-device text to speech, its settings sheet and the `tts_settings` reads.

### How it is built

- `ListenModel` belongs to the post screen's stack entry (Android) or the screen's `@StateObject` (iOS), as does its player, not the lazy list row: the audio goes on while the post scrolls, behind pushed screens and in the background, and stops when the screen closes (RN unmounts the post screen then). The reader follows the session: another account (or a sign-out) drops the status, the busy unlock and its key, and asks again for the new reader.
- `ListenPlayerState` is RN's player logic as an immutable value; the platform controllers drive their stream from it and report the stream's events to it. A source is opened only after the first play, never while new URLs are being asked for and never after they could not be had (until "Try again"). Progress is read every 500 ms from the stream while playing, in the background too (the renewal count depends on it).
- **One renewal per account at a time, app-wide** (`SessionRenewals`): the Listen bar's status, its unlock, its player, every screen and the app start's own session refresh (`SessionModel`) share one `AccountManager.refreshSession` per account; a second caller waits for the first one's answer instead of spending the refresh token again.
- **No write after logout**: the renewal is `AccountManager.refreshSession`, which never stores for an account that left while it ran (the removal marks above), and `SessionRenewals.renew` hands a token out only while its account is still the current one (RN's `isCurrent`). An unlock is never sent while another account is signed in.
- Status renewal after a 401 runs in its own job: a reload asked for meanwhile (the foreground, an edit, a failed unlock) waits for it and runs after it; a renewal that failed or was cancelled is allowed again (review M1).
- No polling in the background (React Query's focus manager): the screen's owner, not the bar's lazy row, reports the app's background and foreground (Android `ProcessLifecycleOwner` in `ListenController`, iOS `didEnterBackground` / `didBecomeActive` in `ListenScreen`); the foreground asks again for a failed status or a paused poll.
- An unlock on its way runs in the app's scope and is kept app-wide per account and post (`ListenUnlocks`) with its key: neither the screen closing nor another reader cuts it off; a reopened screen, a second open screen of the post or a reader switching back joins it (busy, then its answer as the status) instead of spending a second key; a lost answer leaves the key for the next tap from any screen; the key goes once the outcome is known (delivered, or a 4xx).
- The bar stays hidden until the script guess for the body is in, so an unreadable post never flashes the bar.
- Android: renewed URLs and a switched language load into the same stream and media session (no stop, no clearing), so the media notification and the foreground service stay up; the player keeps the network awake with the screen off (`WAKE_MODE_NETWORK`, `WAKE_LOCK`). Android 12+ refuses a foreground service started from the background (a renewal resuming there): Media3's listener then posts a plain "Listen paused" notification that opens the app. iOS: a renewed URL or another language loads into the same AVPlayer (`replaceCurrentItem`), keeping the session; one `.playback` session counter shared with the 3Speak voice streams (`PlaybackAudioSession`), held by a Listen stream from its first play to the end of the track or its release, given back to other apps (`notifyOthersOnDeactivation`) when the last holder lets go.
- Android's playback service has one ExoPlayer: each stream has its own `MediaController` and media id, acts only while its own item is in the player, and puts its item back at its last place when it plays again (another post screen's Listen may have taken the player).

### Deliberate differences from RN

- Fresh URLs from a renewal always reload the source at the place it failed, even when the server hands back the same URL; a source change for any other reason does too. RN reloads only when the URL string changes, and an unplanned refetch with a new URL started RN's player from 0.
- No refetch on focus or reconnect: the status is asked again on the app's return to the foreground only when the last load failed (RN's React Query refetches on focus and reconnect).
- Play on a player whose URL could not be renewed tries a renewal first (RN plays the dead URL, which fails again).
- A track URL that is not https, or has white space or control characters, is dropped; a status word longer than 32 characters reads as "none".
- The unreadable-script guess knows letters outside the BMP from a list of letter blocks (historic scripts, kana supplements, mathematical letters, Mende, Adlam, CJK extensions), the same on both platforms (Kotlin/Native's regex has no categories there); JS reads `\p{L}`. Only rare characters can count differently.
- iOS shows the system's headphones symbol and SwiftUI's slider and alerts; Android uses Material 3's slider and dialogs.

### Play Console (owner action)

Android 14 requires the media playback foreground service to be declared: Play Console > App content > Foreground service permissions, declare `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK` for "Media playback" (Listen: post audio the reader started, playing in the background with media controls), with a short video of playback continuing with the screen off. Without the declaration Play rejects the release that adds the permission.

### Tests

Shared `ListenTest` (23: the helpers against RN's own `listen.ts` through `ListenGoldens`, generated with `node --experimental-strip-types native/tools/golden/generate-listen.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/app/ListenGoldens.kt` from the RN root; the untrusted status; visitor, hidden cases, reader token, the place in line, percentage and tracks; the confirmation; 402; the replayed key after a lost answer; the 401 renewals of status, unlock and URLs; one renewal across screens; no token handed out after a switch; nothing written after a logout; another reader; the player's renewal count, its reset, speeds, languages and the lock screen). Android `ListenFlowsTest` (8, Robolectric with a scripted engine: log in, the confirmation then the place in line, Get Points, playing on while the bar scrolls away and stopping with the screen, renewal and language reloads at their places, the error and Try again, one audio source with the voice players, the service and permissions declared). iOS `ListenTests` (5, the controller with a scripted engine over the demo answers) and `ListenUITests` (3: log in, the confirmation then the place in line, the unlocked player). Mutation checks below.

### Mutation checks for Listen

Each guard was removed in turn and the suites run (`listen-mutate.py` in the session scratchpad); every one but the last made a test fail: one renewal per account in flight (`SessionRenewals`), a renewed token handed out only to the current account, no unlock without the confirmation (the model, and the Android button), a lost answer keeping its key, the 401 unlock resent once, the status renewal allowed again after a failure and not repeated after a success, the renewal count's 30 s reset and its cap, the lock screen ignored while loading, no status for comments, one audio source app-wide (Android and iOS), no source after a failed renewal (Android), the screen closing stopping the audio (Android and iOS). Review fixes, each mutation-checked the same way: a cancelled renewal allowed again, a reload not cancelling the renewal, no background polling (both of its checks together; each alone is backed by the other), the foreground resuming a paused poll, a lost answer by a thrown transport error or an unreadable 200 keeping its key, an in-flight unlock not cut off, hidden until the guess, the same Android stream for new URLs, the process lifecycle's background, the iOS session given back on release; then a joined in-flight unlock (two open screens, a reopened screen shown busy), the key kept app-wide after a lost answer, an answer that came in the background scheduling no poll, the process observer removed on close, the resume notification, the same AVPlayer on iOS. Survivor: the unlock's own "another account is signed in" check at send time; an account change already cancels the unlock before it can run, so the check is a second line that no test can reach on its own.

## Background media and widgets

Listen in the background with lock screen controls, dictation that goes on in the background, and lock and home screen widgets. Shared: `app/BackgroundMedia.kt` (`NowPlaying`, `NowPlayingCard`, `BackgroundSessions`), `app/DictationModel.kt` (`DictationHost`, `DictationSessions`), `app/Widgets.kt` (`AppShortcuts`, `WidgetSnapshot`, `WidgetFeed`, `WidgetStore`). Android: `ListenPlayback.kt`, `DictationService.kt`, `widgets/EcencyWidgets.kt` (Glance). iOS: `Screens/ListenViews.swift`, `App/DictationPlatform.swift` (`IosDictationHost`), `App/WidgetStore.swift`, the `EcencyWidgets` extension (`iosApp/Widgets`).

### Background media

- **Listen** keeps playing with the screen off and the app in the background (iOS `.playback` session with `UIBackgroundModes: audio`; Android Media3 `MediaSessionService`, `mediaPlayback` foreground service, see "Listen"). Lock screen, Control Center and the media notification: play, pause, skip back and forward 15 s (`NowPlaying.SKIP_SECONDS`; iOS `skipBackwardCommand` / `skipForwardCommand`, Android the session's custom layout of two custom session commands (media3 1.4's notification drops custom layout buttons without one), handled by `seekBack` / `seekForward` with ExoPlayer's 15 s increments, previous and next taken away), scrubbing, and the now playing card (title, @author, "Ecency", artwork). iOS: an interruption (call, Siri) pauses and resumes when the system says so; headphones unplugged pause and stay paused. Android: audio focus and becoming noisy are ExoPlayer's (`handleAudioFocus`, `setHandleAudioBecomingNoisy`).
- **Now playing privacy** (`NowPlaying.card`): the card shows the post's title and its author, which are public on chain. The artwork is the post's image (the author's avatar for an NSFW post or one without an image) and **none while the PIN lock is on**: a locked phone shows only title and author. The card is read again for every source the player loads and when the PIN lock is turned on or off while it plays (`retitle`: the same source, no reload).
- **Teardown**: every Listen player registers with `BackgroundSessions`; `AppEnvironment.accountsLeft` stops them all when the current account leaves (logout, logout-all, the PIN wipe): the stream, the lock screen card, the media notification and Android's "Listen paused" notice go. Another account leaving leaves the reader's audio alone. An account switch stops it through the model as before.
- **Dictation** starts only in the foreground (as before) and now goes on in the background: Android starts `DictationService` (`microphone` foreground service, not exported) with an ongoing notification "Recording for dictation" and a Stop action, started from the Record tap (Android 14 refuses a microphone service started in the background); iOS keeps the `.playAndRecord` session active with the `audio` background mode and the system shows its recording indicator. The notification is private on the lock screen (a public version without actions); its Stop holds the clip unsent ("Recording stopped from the notification"), because a notification can be tapped without unlocking: the user confirms "Transcribe (N Points)" in the app. When the platform cannot show the recording in the background (Android: the foreground service refused, or notifications off) it records in front as before and is held unsent the moment the app leaves the foreground. It stops and is thrown away (nothing charged) on logout, wipe, an account change and when the sheet or its composer closes. These stop it and **hold the clip unsent**: the app lock engaging on the return (after the lock timeout, also when the lock cover closes the sheet first), the notification's Stop, leaving the foreground without a visible notification (above), on iOS an audio session interruption (a call, Siri), on Android a transient audio focus loss (a call, an alarm), on Android 10+ the system silencing the recording, and below Android 10 any audio focus loss (a permanent loss, music or Listen starting, is ignored from 10 on). The clip is kept until the user taps "Transcribe (N Points)" or "Give up" in the app (after the unlock when locked); the hold outlives the sheet (`DictationSessions.Held`, memory only: Android leaves the composer out behind the lock cover), and the next sheet of that account and editor takes it back. The clip ownership rules stay: a clip carries its owner's tag, a held clip is kept by the purges of its own account and goes with the account.

### Widgets

Three widgets, both platforms:
- **Notifications**: the unread count; opens `ecency://notifications` (the Notifications tab).
- **Quest streak**: the daily quest streak as Perks last read it; opens `ecency://perks`.
- **Write**: quick New wave (`ecency://compose/wave`, the Waves tab with an empty composer) and Write (`ecency://compose/post`, the editor); shows no data.

iOS: a WidgetKit extension `EcencyWidgets` (`app.esteem.mobile.ios.widgets`, App Group `group.com.ecency.eshare`), families `accessoryCircular`, `accessoryRectangular`, `accessoryInline` (Lock Screen) and `systemSmall` (Home Screen); counts are also `privacySensitive`. Android: Glance home screen widgets (`NotificationsWidget`, `StreakWidget`, `ComposeWidget`). Android phones have no lock screen widgets; the provider info lists `keyguard`, which only tablets and hub mode on recent releases use.

- **Data**: the widgets read ONLY `WidgetSnapshot` (`{"v":1,"signedIn","locked","unread","streak"}`), which `WidgetFeed` writes from the signed-in account, the PIN lock setting, the unread count (as the notifications model refreshes it) and the streak (as Perks reads the quests): iOS into a file in the App Group container (`Widgets/snapshot.json`, its folder left out of backups, then `WidgetCenter.reloadAllTimelines`), Android into the app's own `ecency.widgets` preferences (left out of backups, then Glance `updateAll`). No name, key, token or session code; the widget never reaches the network; anything unreadable reads as nothing.
- **Privacy per widget**: the unread count and the streak are hidden while the PIN lock is on ("Unlock Ecency to see") and cleared on logout and wipe ("Log in to Ecency"); the Write widget has no data.
- **Links**: `AppShortcuts.parse` takes exactly the four shapes above (no query or argument is used, nothing is prefilled, signed or sent). `AppEnvironment.shortcutEntry` turns one into a `NavEntry` that the platforms open as any link entry (New wave asks for the empty composer only once the entry opened, `entryOpened`, and never over a shared text that waits): after the accounts were read, behind the app lock, login gated by `NavState.open` (Perks and the editor need an account; the tabs show their login prompt). The auth link parser never claims them.
- **Language**: the widgets' text, picker names and descriptions follow the app language (README "Localization", OS-read strings).
- **Owner action**: the widget App ID and its profile, secret `WIDGET_PROVISIONING_PROFILE` (docs/release-builds.md, "Widget extension"); CI installs it as `ECENCY_WIDGET_PROFILE` like the other extensions.

### Play Console (owner action)

Besides Listen's `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK`, declare `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MICROPHONE` for "Microphone" (dictation the user started keeps recording in the background, with a visible notification and a Stop action), with a short video of a recording continuing in the background and stopped from the notification.

### Tests

Shared `BackgroundMediaTest` (card, privacy, skips, teardown on logout and wipe), `DictationModelTest` (background recording, notification Stop, lock and call holds, the hold outliving the sheet, teardown), `WidgetsTest` (shortcuts, snapshot privacy and decoding, the feed). Android `BackgroundMediaFlowsTest` (dictation service and notification, widgets), `ListenFlowsTest` (card, teardown, 15 s skips), `LinkEntryTest` (shortcuts). iOS `ListenTests` (card, teardown, skips), `WidgetViewTests` (snapshot reader against the shared encoder, copy, links, App Group store, a rendered sheet).

## Wallet (wave 4)

RN `screens/wallet`, `assetDetails`, `assetsSelect`, `transfer`, `trade`, `redeem`, `components/tipping`, `providers/queries/walletQueries` and the wallet mutations of `providers/sdk/mutations`, over @ecency/sdk 2.4.26. Shared code: `hive/WalletOperations.kt` (serializers), `api/broadcast/WalletOps.kt` (the SDK builders), `api/broadcast/Amounts.kt`, `api/wallet/` (reads, history), `app/WalletModel.kt`, `AssetDetailsModel.kt`, `TransferModel.kt`, `StakeModels.kt`, `WalletActionModels.kt`, `WalletSends.kt`, `SendJournal.kt`, `WalletCopy.kt`, `DemoWallet.kt`. UI: Android `ui/WalletScreens.kt`, `ui/TransferScreens.kt`; iOS `Screens/WalletViews.swift`, `Screens/TransferViews.swift`. The full RN inventory with what is done lives with the wave notes (wallet-inventory.md).

- **Data**: as RN, the Wallet tab renders Ecency's portfolio (`POST /wallet-api/portfolio-v2 {username, onlyEnabled, currency}`): balances, fiat rates, APR, pending rewards, the actions per asset and the extra rows are the server's. Order: POINTS, HP, HIVE, HBD, then the profile's tokens (`posting_json_metadata.profile.tokens`, ENGINE and CHAIN entries that show). SPK, LARYNX and LP are dropped (RN removed them). Polled every 2 minutes while shown; pull to refresh; an account change empties it. The currency comes from the `settings.currency` preference (the settings wave writes it; USD until then).
- **History**: hive layer `condenser_api.get_account_history` with RN's per-tab operation sets as the bitmask (never an empty request), the SDK's per-asset filters, RN's grooming and ticker match; pages arrive ascending, so rows are deduped by history index and ordered newest first by it (`WalletHistory.order`), and an empty filtered list advances by itself at most 5 pages. Hive Engine `/private-api/engine-account-history` (newest first by time after dedupe), Points `/private-api/point-list` (API order). Pending requests (savings withdrawals, conversions, open orders with cancel), recurrent transfers (with delete), outgoing and expiring delegations and incoming ones (balance-api REST, read only), the filter sheet, transaction id copy (hivexplorer link) and the repeat button (prefill kept out of the saved navigation, `AppEnvironment.transferPrefill`).
- **History categories** (#2747, web's wallet history): chips over the history of HIVE, HBD and HP with web's groups (SDK `HIVE_ACCOUNT_OPERATION_GROUPS`: Transfers, Market orders, Interests, Stake operations, Rewards; `HistoryCategory`), each cut to the asset's own operation set and offered only when that leaves something (HIVE: transfers, market, stake, rewards; HBD: transfers, market, interests, rewards; HP: stake, rewards). A chip sets the operation filter, so the chain filters by the category's `get_account_history` bitmask exactly as web does, and the existing per-operation sheet still refines it (no chip is selected for a set the sheet made). HBD's history now also asks for and shows `interest` and `proposal_pay` (web's HBD list has them, RN did not). Points: web's type filter over `point-list {type}` (the server filters), All plus web's 12 types labelled with the rows' own titles, except the earning types, which drop the rows' "Points for" (Heartbeats, Login, Post, Comment, Vote, Reblog; `native.wallet_history.point_type_*`, owner note 10-28), and the two transfer types, whose rows both read "Transfer/Gifting", are "Transfers sent" (998) and "Transfers received" (999). Deliberate differences: Transfers also holds `fill_transfer_from_savings` (web's group lacks it while its HIVE and HBD lists show it); Hive Engine tokens get no categories (web has none, and the engine history API has no operation filter).
- **Operations**: transfer (multi recipients up to 50 in one transaction), recurrent transfer (and delete with amount 0), to and from savings (request id `Date.now() >>> 0`), power up, power down and stop (0 VESTS), withdraw routes, delegation and undelegation (the amount is the new total; at the max the whole delegatable VESTS go, never a rounded figure above them), convert, claim rewards (only the clicked asset, posting), points transfer, Hive Engine transfer, stake, unstake, delegate, undelegate (quantity cut to the token's precision, RN #3569) and the engine reward claim, internal market swap (`limit_order_create`, order id `9` + now % 1e8, 27 days) and order cancel, promote, Boost+ and RC top-up with points, tips (HIVE, HBD, POINTS, engine tokens, memo `Tip for @author/permlink`). Amounts are exact decimal text, truncated as RN (`Amounts`, `Dec`), never through a float. Memos go as plain text, as RN (memo encryption is a post-migration item).
- **Golden vectors**: every new serializer is pinned by `WalletGoldenVectors` (33 transactions): the operation JSON is the SDK builders' and mutations' own, the bytes are dhive's serializer, the transaction id and digest the SDK's (they must hash the dhive bytes), the signature the deterministic RFC 6979 one. Fully offline: `GOLDEN_MODULES=<scratch npm install of @ecency/sdk@2.4.26 @hiveio/dhive@1.3.6 @noble/curves@2 @noble/hashes@2 @tanstack/react-query@5 react> node native/tools/golden/generate-wallet.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/hive/WalletGoldenVectors.kt`. The wallet serializers also make those operations signable from hive:// links for key accounts (they were refused as unsupported before).
- **One action, one id**: every value-moving send goes through `WalletSends.send(user, ops, authority, actionId)` with an id made once, when the user confirms (`env.idempotencyKey()`); a second tap or a retry of that action resumes its transaction, a new confirmation is a new action. Claims are not repeat safe and get an id too. Active operations take the existing routes: stored active key, the auth upgrade sheet (temporary active key, 60 s, one signature), HiveSigner's page, HiveAuth's wallet.
- **Check your wallet**: a send that may have landed (`BroadcastException.Network` after an uncertain attempt, or `undecided`) is `SendPhase.Checking`: a spinner and "This may already have gone through..."; no retry button. The send runs in the app's scope and asks the chain again through `SigningModel.resume` with the pending's own key (no prompt): the same signed transaction is re-sent every 5 s while it can still be accepted (`PendingBroadcast.resendDeadline`), after that `find_transaction` decides, asked after 5, 10 and 20 s, then every 30 s; an "unknown" answer (not expired yet by the chain's clock) needs no read of the chain's head. A new transaction is signed only after the chain said the earlier one did not land. `BroadcastException.Unconfirmed` (by the chain's clock) ends it as "Could not confirm..."; after 15 minutes without an answer the screen stops waiting and the journal keeps asking. A failure where nothing reached a node is an ordinary failure (RN: back, and "{operation} failed: {reason}"). For HiveSigner and HiveAuth accounts, a failure after the request may have been answered (HiveAuth's request expiring included, and the HiveSigner page closed by the user, which may have broadcast before) says "This may already have gone through..." too, never "try again" (only a refusal before anything left the app, or a wallet that rejected, is a plain failure).
- **Leaving a running send**: back asks first ("Still sending"); the send goes on without its screen (`WalletSends` counts the screens that own it, `SendOwnership`) and a send nobody owns any more reports its outcome as one toast and is forgotten. An account that leaves (logout, logout-all, wipe) stops its sends at once: nothing signs again for it.
- **Links**: hive:// and ecency://sign operations go through the same `WalletSends` (one action per confirmed link, "check your wallet", same transaction); the requesting app hears only the final word: `status=success&id=...`, `user_cancelled`, `unconfirmed` when the chain cannot tell, else `qr.transaction_failed`.
- **One amount reading**: every amount, the checks and the signed text alike, goes through `Amounts.normalize` / `Dec.of`: ".5" is 0.5 (it was signed as 0.000 before the review, which for a recurrent transfer deletes a schedule), "", "." and "1e3" are not amounts. Single-recipient kinds refuse several typed names instead of sending to the first.
- **Restart journal** (`SendJournal`, the README L5 design note): when a key account's send turns uncertain, `{account, actionId, txId, expiration, operation name}` goes into the secure store entry `wallet.sendJournal` (no key, signature, amount, recipient or memo; `WalletModelsTest` checks the stored text); it is removed when the send settles in this run. At launch (`env.sendJournal.start()`, called by both apps at launch) every entry left is resolved with `transaction_status_api.find_transaction`: in a block or the mempool "went through", `expired_irreversible` "did not go through", `too_old` or the chain's head time past expiration + 10 minutes "could not confirm"; anything else keeps checking (after 5, 10, 20 s, then every 30 s for about 10 minutes while the app runs, again at the next start). The result shows as a notice on the account's Wallet tab until dismissed. Logout removes the account's entries, undecided ones included (the account's sends stop at the same time; the user checks that account's wallet after signing in again). Stopping a send does not take back a request already on the wire: a broadcast a node had received before the logout may still land; a wipe removes the store. At most 32 entries: past that, decided entries whose notice was not read give way, oldest first; undecided ones never do, and while 32 undecided entries wait no new value send starts ("Too many earlier sends are still being checked...").
- **Deliberate differences**: link decoding wants JSON numbers for percents, recurrence and executions; withdraw route percents go on chain in chain units (RN sends the typed 0..100 value as 0..10000 units, which reads as a hundredth of what was typed); the transfer screen allows several recipients only where RN sends them (plain transfers, points, engine transfers), one elsewhere; a send's outcome is followed after its screen closed (one toast); the trade order book stays the RN walk (its double count of the first level kept, `SwapCalculator.price`); the price chart (dead code in RN) and the balance analytics sheet are not built; the transfer favourites sheet and QR scan into the recipient are not wired yet (the parser is: `TransferRecipientParser`); Get Points and Account Boost are the in-app purchase screens (see In-app purchases).
- **Debug** (fake data only): Android `--ez demoAuth true --ez seedAccounts true --ez acceptTerms true --ez walletTab true`, `--es walletScreen transfer,transfer,HIVE,hive,spike-bob` (a saved-navigation line, ',' separated), `--ez demoUndecided true` (broadcasts never answer: the check-your-wallet state); iOS `-fakeData YES -seedAccounts YES -startTab WALLET` or `-openScreen wallet:<line>`. `DemoWallet` answers the wallet reads offline; broadcasts are still refused.
- **Tests**: shared `WalletGoldenVectorTest`, `WalletModelsTest`, `WalletActionsTest` (and the link and navigation cases); Android `WalletFlowsTest`; iOS `WalletTests`, `WalletUITests`. Mutation-checked (each removed or weakened on a green tree, a test failed): the journal's record, its "only an irreversible expiry is final" rule, the chain's clock, its removal with the account, no operation body stored; a send that never reached a node not being "checking"; one action per confirmation; amount above the balance, low liquidity per recipient, the exchange memo, executions >= 2, picking yourself, the engine precision cut; history order, dedupe, ticker match and the auto-advance bound; the claim of only the clicked reward and its mask; the delegation max and "0 only to remove"; route chain units and duplicates; promote's post check, the Boost+ block, tip decimals; HIVE tokens never written and the saved selection over a lagging node; the signed-out transfer link. Two guards survive by design because a second guard holds (the builder's recurrence >= 24 and the decoder's uint32 check, both repeated by the operations' own checks).

## Settings

RN `screens/settings` (screen and container), `constants/options` (currency, language, nsfw, theme, imageServer), `providers/ecency/supportBeneficiary.ts`, `utils/{settings,storeReview,sendEmail,autoLocale}.ts`, `screens/emailDigests`, SDK support-settings and newsletter calls. Shared `app/SettingsModel.kt` (`SettingsCopy`, `SettingsOptions`, `StoreListing`), `app/EmailDigestsModel.kt`, `legacy/LegacySettings.kt`, `NewsletterApi.unsubscribeAll`; Android `ui/SettingsScreens.kt`; iOS `Screens/SettingsViews.swift`. Inventory: RN screen row by row, in RN's order.

```kotlin
env.settings.start(deviceLocale)        // at launch, after env.prefs: saved values apply at once, the RN import runs in the background
env.settings.onShow(); onHide()         // the screen: server list, support settings (after queued saves)
env.settings.setCurrency("eur"); setLanguage("de-DE"); setNsfw(NsfwMode.HIDE); setTheme(ThemeMode.DARK)
env.settings.setImageServer(url); selectServer(url)
env.settings.setSupportBeneficiary(on); setSupportBeneficiaryPercent(p); setSupportCuration(on); setSupportCurationPercent(p)
env.settings.contentLanguage            // translation target and reader language (RN appLang)
env.emailDigestsModel()                 // start(), open(type, target), stopAll(email)
```

- [x] General: Currency (24, stored lower case as `settings.currency`, which the wallet reads), Language (39; at the first start the device locale, mapped like RN `mapToRegisteredLocale`, is saved once, as RN autoDetectLocale; RN's imported language replaces it, except RN's initial en-US: an RN store holding en-US counts as a choice only when RN's own `locale_auto_detected` key is set, otherwise the detected language stays), Server (groomed names, "Checking..." and a spinner while `HiveNodeSettings.select` checks it, RN's three toasts), Image Server (RN's three; the proxy and the body renderer switch at once, toast "Successful"), NSFW (`nsfw`, read by feeds and waves at their next page), Appearance, Show Images, PIN code, Finger Print / Face Unlock, Reset Pin Code
- [x] Push Notification card (built with notifications)
- [x] Support Ecency (signed in, only after a valid load): the two toggles (5 % when turned on) and their presets (1/5/10/25, 5/10/25/50/100); both percents sent every time (`support-settings-update`), optimistic, in order, a superseded save not sent, only the newest operation applies its answer, a failure goes back to the last confirmed values with "Fail!", an account change hides the card and loads the new account's (while the screen shows), loaded again when the screen shows (the editor chip saves too)
- [x] Rate Ecency (Android Play app, else the web listing; iOS the App Store page Apple's lookup names for the bundle, on its review form, else RN's web listing), Send Feedback (`mailto:bug@ecency.com`, RN's subject and body with account, version (build) and device, qs encoding; RN's two toasts)
- [x] Email digests (signed in): `AppScreen.EmailDigests` (gated, built): subscriptions grouped by address, "<Cadence> • <status>", "Manage" opens the digest sheet, "Stop all" confirms then `POST /api/newsletter/unsubscribe-all {email, code}` with RN's toast or alert, "Get more by email" offers own and the Ecency newsletter; the list loads again when the sheet closes; 503 and other errors with RN's texts
- [x] Manage Authorities, Delete Account (earlier waves)
- [x] Chat Privacy (signed in, after Language): Everyone / Only people I follow / No one (disable DMs), read when the screen shows and saved at once (`GET`/`PUT /api/mattermost/me/dm-privacy`) with RN's two toasts (see Chats)
- Not in RN's settings, so not here: clear cache, about and privacy links, default footer (dead redux field)

RN settings import (once per install, `settings.legacyImported`; on iOS the RN storage reader is locked per call because the account migration's cleanup rewrites it at the same time): `LegacySettingsReader` reads RN's `settings` key (`server`) and `persist:root` `application` (currency, language, nsfw as string or number, colorTheme, hidePostsThumbnails, imageServer, isNotificationOpen, notificationDetails). Only values from RN's option lists are taken, and only into settings this app has no value for yet; the push switches move together. Storage that cannot be read now is tried at the next start; absent storage or bad data counts as done. After the import the theme, image proxy, node pool (`HiveNodeSettings.applySaved`), push switches and "Show Images" reload. No secret is read.

Deliberate differences
- The language choice switches the app at once (catalog, direction; see Localization, app language) with no restart prompt (RN asks to restart when the direction flips); most copy is still English until it moves to the catalog. It also drives content features (translation target, reader language).
- A stale server stops the spinner and says "Server not available" (RN leaves its spinner on and shows no toast).
- iOS shows the cards as form sections, dropdowns as menu pickers, and the PIN rows as buttons with On/Off (RN latching toggles); Android draws RN's cards, bordered dropdowns and switches.
- Feed cards do not read "Show Images" yet (notification rows do).
- A theme saved by an earlier preview build (platform key `themeMode`) moves into the shared settings once.
- Rate Ecency on iOS needs Apple's lookup to name this app's page; the lookup is capped at 5 s; offline, slow or before the listing exists it opens RN's web listing (`StoreListing.IOS_WEB`), Android falls back to the Play web listing.
- "Finger Print / Face Unlock" shows only when the device has biometrics enrolled (RN shows it whenever the PIN is on).
- Push registrations of one account go out one at a time and read the switches when their turn comes, so the RN import (or a quick toggle) during the first registration is always the last write.

Debug: Android `--es openScreen settings|emailDigests`, `--ez demoDigests true`; iOS `-openScreen settings|emailDigests`, `-demoDigests YES` (two synthetic subscriptions; demo support settings keep their values for the run).

Tests: shared `SettingsModelTest` (options, locale mapping, feedback URL, review links, device settings, image proxy swap, server toasts, support settings rules, the RN import: full, existing values win, invalid values, unreadable and absent storage) and `EmailDigestsModelTest`; Android `SettingsFlowsTest` (and the settings cases of `ShellFlowsTest`, `NotificationFlowsTest`, `LockFlowsTest`); iOS `SettingsTests`, `SettingsUITests`, `ShellTests.testThemeChoiceIsSaved`.

## Localization

Status: the core is in (catalog, formatter, plural rules, generator) and the language setting drives it (app language, below): the language, its layout direction and the OS per-app language switch live. The shared core's copy (the copy objects, model, error and enum texts) reads the catalog (shared copy, below), numbers, dates and times follow the app language (formatting, below), and so do the strings the OS reads (widgets, notification channels, permission prompts; OS-read strings, below). The Android screens read the catalog too (screen copy, below); the iOS screens too, and the RTL pass is done (RTL, below).

How a key flows:
1. Source: RN's `src/config/locales/en-US.json` (Crowdin source, ICU MessageFormat, `{name}` placeholders). Crowdin writes the 38 translations next to it (`crowdin.yml`), the same files RN reads.
2. Build: `:shared:generateL10n` (included build `build-logic`, `L10nGenerator`, no Node) reads every `src/config/locales/*.json` and `shared/l10n/cldr-plural-rules.json` and writes into `shared/build/generated/l10n` (not committed; part of commonMain for Android and all iOS targets):
   - Kotlin `L`: one accessor per en-US key, nested as the JSON (`L.settings.language`, `L.post.a11yVotes(count = 3)`). Keys are camel cased (`chain-error` is `chainError`, a leading digit gets `_`, Kotlin keywords get a trailing `_`, a later sibling that collides keeps its raw key). Parameters come from the English message: `String` for text and select, `Int` for plural, `Double` for number. A placeholder added or renamed in en-US changes the signature, so call sites fail to compile instead of showing `{name}`. A broken en-US message fails the build, and so does syntax the formatter does not render (`selectordinal`, number styles such as `{x, number, percent}`, dates). The tree is `@HiddenFromObjC`, so the iOS framework links only the accessors Kotlin code uses.
   - Swift `L` (`shared/build/generated/l10n/swift/L.swift`): the same keys as nested enums for the iOS app (`L.Settings.language`, `L.Post.a11yVotes(count: 3)` with `Int` for plural and `Double` for number, Swift keywords in backticks such as ``L.Alert.`continue` ``), calling the exported `L10n.text(index:)` / `formatArgs(index:names:values:)`. The app's "Build Kotlin shared core" phase copies it to `iosApp/Generated/L10n.generated.swift` (gitignored, an optional source in `project.yml`), so a clean checkout and CI build it with the framework.
   - One object per language with its messages as string constants (only those that differ from English; en-US holds all), decoded the first time the language is used.
   - `L10nPluralRules`: the CLDR plural rules of the 39 languages compiled to Kotlin.
3. Runtime: `L10n` (shared `l10n/L10n.kt`) holds the current language as a `StateFlow` (`L10n.language`, `setLanguage(id)`, `isRtl`) and resolves each message once per language: a missing key, a translation that does not parse, or one that uses a placeholder English lacks shows English for that key; a translation may leave placeholders out (react-intl formats it, so ja-JP's monthly posts message without `{suffix}` shows as in RN), and one that fails while formatting (an argument of the wrong type) shows English too. Formatting never throws. `setLanguage` matches ids like RN's mapToRegisteredLocale (`ru`, `ru_RU`, `pt-BR` to pt-PT, `zh-Hant-TW` to zh-TW; unknown is English). `L10n.byKey("wallet.$type")` covers RN's run time key names.

Adding copy (owner decisions 10-22): add the key to `src/config/locales/en-US.json` under the fitting group, even for native only copy (one Crowdin source; it moves under `native/` when RN retires). When native's text equals an RN en-US value and means the same thing in the same context, reuse that RN key (its translations come along); the same English with another meaning (a load-more "More" against RN's emoji "More", a poll's "Vote" button against a wallet activity) gets its own key. When native reworded RN copy, add a new key with native's wording; never change RN's English under an existing key. Plurals are ICU plural messages (`{count, plural, one {# vote} other {# votes}}`), not `{suffix}` tricks. Then use `L.<group>.<key>`.

Adding a string (both apps; one source file, decision D2):
1. Add the English text to `src/config/locales/en-US.json` only. Never edit the other 38 files by hand: Crowdin writes them and the next pull overwrites local edits (the one exception is a seed made by a script and uploaded to Crowdin, as `tools/l10n/seed-native-plurals.mjs` below). RN keys go under the fitting RN group; native only copy goes under `native.<area>`.
2. Write it as ICU MessageFormat: `{name}` placeholders, a choice as `{kind, select, a {..} other {..}}`. No `selectordinal`, number styles or dates (the native build refuses them); an apostrophe before `{`, `}` or `#` starts a quote (`''` for a literal one there).
3. Counts: in a native key, an ICU plural (`{count, plural, one {# day} other {# days}}`; English needs `one` and `other`, translators add the forms their language needs). Not in an RN key: RN runs on Hermes, which has no `Intl.PluralRules`, so react-intl shows a plural message as its raw pattern (RN's `post.a11y_*` plurals already do). RN keeps its English hacks until it is retired.
4. Native: use the generated `L.group.key(count = n)` (Kotlin) or `L.Group.key(count: n)` (Swift); a plural argument is `Int`, so passing a string does not compile. A reused RN key goes into `shared/l10n/rn-reuse.json` with its English (`RnReuseTest`); never reuse an RN key whose English fakes a plural, add a native plural key instead.
5. Never change RN's English under an existing key for native's sake (D3); a typo fix keeps its translations as unapproved (`update_option` in `crowdin.yml`).
6. After merge to `development`, `crowdin-upload.yml` sends the new source to Crowdin; translations come back through `crowdin-download.yml` (the `sync-translations` label or a manual run), one PR. Until then the new key shows English in every language.

Shared copy (`l10n-copy-shared`):
- Key choice: a native text that is word for word an RN en-US message with the same meaning (same text and same meaning) reuses that RN key, so its translations come along (about 1050 keys, placeholders renamed to RN's). A text native reworded, or native only copy, gets a key under the top level `native` group of `en-US.json`, one subgroup per copy area (`native.wallet`, `native.chat_thread`, `native.app_error`, ...): one clear place for Crowdin, no clash with RN keys, and RN ignores it (about 470 keys). RN's English is never changed.
- `shared/l10n/rn-reuse.json` records each reused key with the English native showed. build-logic `RnReuseTest` fails when RN's English under a recorded key changes (a reuse must not reword native) and when Kotlin code (shared and Android) reads an RN key that is not recorded; record a new reuse there after checking the texts match. Keys read at run time (`L10n.byKey("notification.$key")`, `post_dropdown.$id`) are recorded by hand.
- Copy is read when shown: copy objects are getters (`val X: String get() = L...`), lists and maps of labels are getters or map to lambdas, enum labels are lambdas behind a `label`/`title` getter, so a live switch reaches everything. Swift sees the same properties. `CopyCatalogTest` reads every copy getter, function and enum label in English, switches to `en-XA` and fails on any English left.
- Messages are whole: fragments built by concatenation became one message with placeholders; counts use ICU plurals (`{count, plural, one {# view} other {# views}}`); lists of role names join through `CopyLists.and`. RN's own fake plurals (`{suffix}`, `attempt(s)`, counts before a fixed plural noun) are not reused: native reads its own plural keys under `native.plurals` (`l10n-crowdin`, below).
- Copy is presentation only: code never decides by comparing catalog text (the language can change between producing and checking). Unknown chat authors stay null (`ChatQuote.username`, `ChatMessageItem.authorName`) and show "Unknown user" through a getter; an undecided send is `AppError.undecided`; a refused profile read is `ProfileWrites.ReadRefused`; a swap's end is `TradeOutcome`. Key roles show through `CopyLists.role` while `KeyRole.value` stays the chain word. `CopyIdentityTest` and `ChatModelsTest.aReplySendsTheAuthorsNameWhateverTheLanguage` switch language between the two steps. build-logic `CachedCopyTest` scans commonMain for cached reads (object level `val x = L...`, `by lazy`, enum constructor arguments, `MutableStateFlow` initial values) without a list of copy; `RnReuseTest` also derives the run time key families (`NotificationFormat.COPY_KEYS`, `PostMenuItem` ids) from the sources.
- Text that leaves the app stays RN's fixed English: the `message` of the answers to an app that opened an ecency:// link (`CallbackText`, RN useLinkProcessor word for word), the dApp page answers, the tip memo and other chain text. Deliberate difference from RN: a cloned draft's title "Copy of {title}" is in the app language (user content the user sees, not a protocol field; RN writes English).
- Left as literals on purpose: chain and protocol strings (amounts such as `0.000 HBD`, tickers, operation names, HTTP verbs, dApp bridge answers to the page, including `BridgeGuards` refusals, HiveAuth app metadata), server error matchers, exception and log messages that never reach the UI unmapped, brand and dApp names, language autonyms, debug `toString`s, the editor's markdown templates and upload placeholder.

Screen copy (`l10n-ui-android`):
- Text both apps' screens show word for word lives in shared copy objects, read when shown: `UiCopy` (generic words: Cancel, Delete, Retry...), `EditorUiCopy`, `ReadingUiCopy`, `ChatUiCopy`, `ProfileUiCopy`, `WalletUiCopy`, `AuthUiCopy`, `ShellUiCopy` (`ScreenCopy.kt`), and the sign-in screens' `LoginCopy`, `HiveAuthCopy`, `AccountCopy`, `WelcomeCopy`, `BrowserCopy` (`AccountCopy.kt`, moved from Android). Keys follow the shared copy rule (RN key when the English is the same, recorded in `rn-reuse.json`; else `native.ui.*` per area). Swift reads them as `EditorUiCopy.shared.ADD_CHOICE`. Text only Android shows (biometric prompt, Play Billing, image saver errors) is `AndroidCopy` (`native.android.*`). `ScreenCopyTest` reads every one in English and in `en-XA`.
- Messages are whole (`ChatUiCopy.editedAt(time)`, `ReadingUiCopy.tabSelected(title)`, `StakeCopy.alreadyDelegatedTo(recipient, hp)`), counts shown on screen go through `LocaleFormat.integer` or an ICU plural (`ProfileUiCopy.cardStats`, `EditorUiCopy.wordsCount`).
- Not localized: test tags (a tag once built from a label now uses a fixed id, such as `draftsTab:Drafts`; Android's tabs, pills, option sheets, stats and key rows take enum names: `searchTab:PEOPLE`, `tab:CREATED`, `option:MARK_READ`, `stat:views`, `key:POSTING`), brand names, tickers, chain and file formats, outbound text (above), debug screens.
- Windows: `ui/Windows.kt` defines `AlertDialog`, `ModalBottomSheet`, `DropdownMenu`, `Dialog` and `Popup` with the Material and platform names and the parameters the app uses, each slot inside `FollowAppLanguage`, so an open window switches with the language. Screens in `com.ecency.app.ui` get them by the plain name (a same package declaration wins over star imports). `AppLanguageTest.theAppsWindowsFollowASwitchWithoutAnyCodeOfTheirOwn` covers them.
- Literal check: build-logic `UiLiteralLintTest` (runs in `:build-logic:test`) scans `androidApp/src/main` for string literals that read like copy (words with a space, a capitalised label, and any text in a text slot: `Text(..)`, `Text(text = ..)`, `label`, `placeholder`, `supportingText`, `title`, `message`, `text` and accessibility labels) outside test tags, logs, annotations, comparisons and animation labels, and fails on any that is not in `androidApp/l10n-literals-allowed.txt`. Allowlist rule: add a line `File.kt<TAB>literal as in the source<TAB>why` only for text that is not copy (a brand, a ticker, an id or format, an exception that never reaches the screen, a debug screen); copy goes to the catalog. A stale line fails too. Known gap: a single lower case word passed outside a text slot (a custom composable's `label: String = "or"`) and text built at run time are not seen. The same test fails on any import of a Material or platform window (aliased, or the `ui.window.*` star import) or a fully qualified call to one outside `ui/Windows.kt`.

Formatter (`IcuMessage`): `{x}`, `{x, number}` (numbers in `#`, `{x, number}` and a number in `{x}` are written in the app language by `LocaleFormatter`, grouped, at most 3 decimals, as ICU and formatjs; the plural form follows the message's own language, so an English fallback picks English forms with German digits: "1.234 votes"), `{x, plural, =N {..} one {..} other {..}}` with `#` (in the plural's own sub-messages, not in a nested select, as ICU4J and formatjs) and `offset:N`, `{x, select, ..}`, nesting, apostrophes as ICU's DOUBLE_OPTIONAL mode (react-intl's: `''` is `'`, a quote starts only before `{`, `}` or `#` in a plural, so `l'ami` stays literal). `selectordinal`, number styles and dates are refused: the build fails on them in en-US, and a translation using them shows English. In an RTL language every substituted text value is wrapped in FSI..PDI (`Bidi.isolate`), so `@user` or `1.000 HIVE` inside Arabic keep their own direction; RTL is ar, fa and he (the RTL languages among the 39); ku-TR is Latin script and stays LTR.

Plural rules source: `shared/l10n/cldr-plural-rules.json`, ICU4J 75.1 (CLDR 45) `PluralRules.forLocale(id).getRules(keyword)` per RN locale id (pt-PT has its own rule, unlike pt). To refresh: print the same with a newer ICU4J and bump the version in `libs.versions.toml` (`icu4j`, test only); `IcuOracleTest` fails until file and library agree.

Why generated constants rather than bundled JSON resources: they need no resource plumbing (the iOS framework is static and has no bundle; Android and both test targets read them as plain code), no JSON parsing at run time, and only the current language is decoded. Measured: decoding one language table takes about 0.1 ms on the JVM host and 0.2 to 0.3 ms on the iOS x64 simulator (debug), all 39 together 3.5 ms and 12 ms; nothing is decoded at app start until the first message is read. Size: the 39 tables hold 1.6 M characters (2.1 MB UTF-8). The arm64 release framework (static) grows by 2.7 MB of string data and 0.06 MB of code (Kotlin `L` is not exported; Swift `L` compiles into the app), 2.9 MB in all or 0.65 MB gzip; the Android debug APK grows by 0.8 MB (2.2 MB of dex; R8 drops unused accessors in release builds).

Checks: rejected translations (a placeholder English lacks, or malformed ICU) are reported by `L10nCatalogTest` (printed as `l10n rejected:`, not pinned, so a Crowdin sync cannot break the build) and show English. Today: el-GR `post_poll.interpretation_token` (translated placeholder name; fix in Crowdin to `• Ερμηνεία ψήφου με βάση το {token}`) and pt-PT `transfer.confirm_summary` (malformed `@{delegator{delegatee}}`; fix to `Delegar {hp} HP ({vests} VESTS) A @{delegator} de @{delegatee} `). For Crowdin: bg-BG `profile.joined` drops `{time}` and shows without the date (as in RN; fix to `Се присъедини преди {time}`). Seeded translations that keep a plain `{count}` where English has a plural are valid and show as before; translators add the plural forms in Crowdin. Pseudo locales (`PseudoLocales`): `en-XA` accents and pads every message by 40% in brackets, `ar-XB` forces RTL text (RLM then RLO, as Android's own ar-XB) and turns on RTL. Tests install them directly; debug builds take them by a launch argument (`--es pseudoLocale ar-XB` on Android, `-pseudoLocale ar-XB` on iOS, `L10n.usePseudo`), over the saved language and without touching the OS per-app language. Release builds never call it.

App language (`l10n-locale-state`, owner decisions 10-22):
- One source of truth: `SettingsModel`'s language (`settings.language`) sets `L10n` at start (before the first frame), on a picker change, after the RN settings import and when a system setting is adopted. The apps turn this on with `AppEnvironment.drivesLanguageCatalog`; tests leave it off unless they test it, since the catalog is one per process.
- First start with nothing saved: the device language through `LocaleMatch`, saved once (`settings.localeDetected`), English when the app lacks it; RN's own choice wins over that detection at the import (RN `autoDetectLocale`).
- Compose: `AppLanguageRoot` (around the theme in `EcencyApp`) provides `LocalAppLanguage` and `LocalLayoutDirection`. The local is static, so a change recomposes everything below the root while remembered state stays; the activity handles `locale|layoutDirection` itself, so nothing restarts. A separate window (Dialog, sheet, popup, menu) composes on its own: the app's windows come from `ui/Windows.kt` (screen copy, below), which puts their content inside `FollowAppLanguage`.
- SwiftUI: `LanguageRoot` (in `EcencyApp.swift`) sets `\.appLanguage`, `\.locale` and `\.layoutDirection`, and rebuilds the navigation root with `.id(language)`: SwiftUI does not track catalog reads, so screens are built again (the path, sheets and models in `AppContainer` stay; screen local state does not). The first frame of the screens waits until the language is final (`SettingsState.languageSettled`: at once, or on the first native start after the RN import has applied its language, at most 1.5 s), so the import never rebuilds a screen the user already opened.
- Direction: right to left for ar, fa and he, whatever the device's direction; ku-TR is Latin script and stays left to right.
- OS per-app language, both ways (`SystemLanguage`, rule `LanguageSync`): Android 13+ `LocaleManager.applicationLocales` with the generated `res/xml/locales_config.xml` (`generateL10nResources`, build-logic `SystemLocales`, from the 39 locale files, not committed; manifest `android:localeConfig`), synced at start and on every resume. iOS: the app's own `AppleLanguages` default, which Settings > Ecency > Language writes; the language appears there because the app declares the 39 languages (`CFBundleLocalizations` in `project.yml` and an empty `Localizations/<tag>.lproj` each, `AppLanguageTests` checks both against the catalog); synced at start (iOS ends the app when that setting changes). The picker writes it too: iOS applies it to the bundle (system texts) at the next launch, the app's own copy switches at once. Debug demo and fake-data runs leave the OS setting alone.
- OS tags (`L10n.systemTag`): the bare language ("de"), except zh-Hans, zh-Hant, sr-Latn and pt-PT; each maps back through `LocaleMatch`. What the app writes (`LanguageSync.tagToWrite`) is the device's own tag when it is the same language and extends that tag (en-GB, fr-CA, zh-Hant-TW), so the platform's dates, times and numbers keep the device's region; else the system tag (a pt-BR device gets pt-PT). Both read back as the same language, so a sync never writes twice.
- The sync rule. A device language change only changes the device's languages; the per-app setting keeps what the app wrote, so the saved choice stands (RN detects once, D5). A per-app change is told apart by `settings.languageSynced`, the app language last written to or adopted from the OS: a per-app language that maps to one of the 39 and is neither that nor the saved one was set by the user in the system settings (or before the first start, which Android allows) and is adopted; a per-app setting cleared after the app had set one ("System default") puts the app in "following the system" (`settings.languageSynced` = `system`, unlike absent = never synced): the device language, mapped (English when unknown), becomes the language, nothing is written back so the system keeps showing "System default", and later device language changes are followed; a language picked in the app or in the system settings ends it; otherwise the saved language is written when the OS shows another (first sync, picker, RN import, an unknown tag). Comparison is by mapped id, so "iw" or "zh-Hant-TW" read back count as the same language.

RTL (`l10n-rtl-pass`):
- What mirrors: everything laid out with start and end (rows, paddings, alignment, `offset(x:)`), tab strips and horizontal scrollers, the image pager, swipe directions, progress bars and value sliders (vote weight, power down percent), chat bubbles (own messages on the trailing side) and icons marked `AppIcon.isDirectional` (Android `autoMirrored`, iOS symbols that flip).
- What stays left to right in every language (Android `LeftToRight`, `ltrFieldStyle`): the balance chart (time runs left to right, y axis pinned left, opened at the newest end), the PIN keypad (1 2 3 from the left, as the system pads), media transport and scrubbers on Android (Material: playback is not mirrored; the Speak voice player and the Listen player), code blocks, and fields for usernames, keys, amounts, numbers, URLs and emails (the "@" before a username field stays on its left).
- Text direction: Android text takes the direction of its first strong letter (`TextDirection.Content` in the theme's typography and `LocalTextStyle`): Compose otherwise forces right to left on every text without a direction in an RTL layout, so "@alice" showed "alice@" and "1.000 HIVE" showed "HIVE 1.000". A `TextStyle(...)` built from scratch does not inherit that, and `BasicTextField` ignores `LocalTextStyle`, so every Compose `TextStyle(...)` in the app names its `textDirection` (build-logic `TextDirectionLintTest` fails otherwise): `Content` for text of any language (editor title, tags and body, chat composer, search bars), `Ltr` for machine text. RTL catalog messages start with one RLM (`L10n.format`, also the iOS widget formatter), so a translation that opens with a Latin word ("HIVE ...", "PIN ...") still lays out right to left; an English fallback does not get it, and the OS-read resources do not need it.
- Post bodies follow their content, not the UI (`List<Block>.isRtlContent()`, HTML `dir="auto"`: the first strong letter, skipping mentions, tags, digits of any script and combining marks; RTL scripts outside the BMP such as Adlam and Hanifi Rohingya count): an English post reads left to right in Arabic, an Arabic post right to left in English; the quote bar and list markers sit on the body's start. HTML `left` and `right` (`text-left`, `data-align`) stay absolute (`BlockStyle.Align.Left`, `End` = right).
- Mixed direction: placeholders in catalog messages are isolated by the formatter; values the UI puts next to catalog text outside a message ("From: @alice", "Balance 1.000 HIVE", a transaction id) go through `Bidi.inUi`, which isolates them while the language is right to left.
- Checks: shared `BlocksTest.bodyDirectionFollowsItsFirstStrongLetter`, `BlockBuilderTest.styleClasses`, `L10nCatalogTest.aDebugPseudoLocaleHoldsAgainstTheSettingsLanguageUntilEnded`, `LanguageSyncTest` (a device tag in another script is not written: ku-Arab-IQ choosing ku-TR writes `ku`); Android `RtlLayoutTest` (each of the above under an RTL root: body direction both ways, quote side, chart axis, keypad order, voice player, field and theme text direction, isolation, draft dates).
- iOS: the direction is set twice, in the SwiftUI environment and as the window scene's direction override (`UIKitDirection` in `AppLanguage.swift`), which reaches sheets, covers, alerts and the lock and auth windows live (SwiftUI does not pass the environment into them; reading an override never set throws, so it is read only once set). Machine fields use `leftToRightField()`, values beside labels `bidiValue()`. Sliders, progress bars and media scrubbers mirror, as the HIG asks and the system player does; the play triangle does not. SwiftUI Text takes its own first strong direction, so the Compose issue above does not exist there. Tests: `RtlLayoutTests`, `PostRenderTests`, `AppLanguageTests` (pseudo RTL root), `CopyLanguageTests` (draft dates), UI `RtlUITests` (mirrored tab bar with `-AppleLanguages (ar)` and with `-pseudoLocale ar-XB`, launch arguments only).
- Screenshot runs: the main screens in `ar-XB` and `en-XA` at the largest font scale (Android 2.0, iOS AccessibilityXXXL). Fixed from them: Latin text forced right to left (above), the editor header squeezing the submit button away at large text, now Save and Preview turn into their icons when their label does not fit (`LabelOrIcon`, Android); unmirrored sheets and windows, a translated RTL body aligned to the wrong side, and rows that broke words or clipped at the largest sizes, which now fall back through `ViewThatFits` (reply as an icon, stacked follower stats, the wallet actions as a 2 by 2 grid, the balance under the asset name) (iOS).

Formatting (`l10n-format`, owner decision 10-22: ages, dates and numbers in the app language; chain amounts POSIX):
- `LocaleFormat` (current language, read on every call) and `LocaleFormatter.of(id)` (one language; tests, Swift `LocaleFormatter.companion.of(languageId:)`), shared `l10n/LocaleFormat.kt`: `decimal`, `fixed`, `toFixed`, `integer`, `posix` (an already rounded POSIX text), `percent`, `fiat`, `token`, `compact`, `assetText` / `amountsIn` (chain asset text shown), dates by pattern (`dateShort` "3/5/2024", `date` "Mar 5, 2024", `dateLong` "March 5, 2024", `time`, `dateTime`, `weekdayDateTime`, `MONTH_DAY`; device time zone through `localOffsetMillis`, expect/actual), `relative` ("vor 3 Stunden"), `now`, `duration` ("3 Jahre"), `age` ("3 Std."). Display only.
- Pure Kotlin over vendored CLDR data, not platform formatters: both platforms and every test target give the same text (Android's `java.text` and iOS's Foundation differ in CLDR version and details, and Robolectric host tests would run the JDK's), and it is checked. Data: `shared/l10n/cldr-formats.json` (ICU4J 75.1, CLDR 45: digits, symbols, grouping, percent affixes, short compact patterns, the 7 date skeletons resolved to patterns, Gregorian month, weekday and day period names, relative, wide and narrow unit patterns; plural forms equal to `other` dropped). 106 KB, compiled by the generator (`FormatDataGenerator`, fails the build on a date field the runtime does not format, such as `B`, or incomplete data) into one minified JSON text per language, decoded on first use: 0.4 to 0.6 ms per language on the JVM host and the iOS x64 simulator (debug), 16 ms for all 39. To refresh: bump `icu4j` and copy `shared/build/cldr-formats.json`, which `CldrFormatsOracleTest` writes when the vendored file differs.
- Choices: digits are the language's CLDR default numbering system, as the platforms and RN's Intl use for that locale: Arabic-Indic for ar-SA, Persian for fa-IR, Bengali for bn-BD, ASCII for the others (hi-IN's CLDR default is Latin). Grouping as CLDR, minimum grouping digits included (bg, es, et, hu, pl, pt-PT write 1234 but 12 345; ICU4J's older MessageFormat and RelativeDateTimeFormatter ignore it, Intl honours it). Rounding half away from zero on the exact binary value (JS toFixed, Intl). Gregorian calendar in every language (CLDR defaults ar-SA to Islamic, fa-IR to Persian, th-TH to Buddhist). 12 or 24 hours as the language's CLDR default, not the device setting. sr-CS formats as sr-Latn (the RN file is Latin script).
- RN parity: where RN uses `Intl` (wallet `formatAmount`, balance analytics) numbers are grouped; where RN prints `toFixed` (payouts, vote value, voters, trade rate, HP figures) they are `toFixed` in the app language (separator and digits, no grouping), so en-US text stays RN's byte for byte.
