Fiat keeps RN's symbol in front in every language. en-US keeps moment's English for `FromNow` ("a few seconds ago", "an hour ago"); other languages write the same buckets with CLDR relative times ("now" for the few seconds). Card ages (`TimeAgo`) are CLDR narrow units in every language (en is unchanged: "now", "5m", "3h", "2d", "4w", "1y"). Compact numbers keep RN's magnitudes and rounding where the language abbreviates that magnitude with one digit, else CLDR's (ja "12.3万", de has no thousands and writes the whole number); en's thousands suffix is CLDR's "K" (the balance chart wrote "k"). The chain asset text in wallet history rows shows localized (`CoinActivity.displayValue`) while `value` stays POSIX (a repeated transfer reads it); voters likewise (`rewardText`, `percentText`).
- Chain bound text is POSIX by construction: `Num`, `Amounts`, `Asset` and the operation builders never read the language (`ChainPathsStayPosixTest` scans `api/broadcast`, `hive`, `auth`, `secure` and the dApp bridge for any use of the formatter); `Asset.parse`, the dApp bridge amounts and the custom_json builders (`WalletOps.pointTransfer`, `engine`) accept ASCII digits with a '.' only, so a display string ("1.000,5", "١٫٥") throws instead of being signed. Confirmation texts that quote what is signed ("Swapping 4.000 HIVE for 1.000 HBD") stay POSIX too. `ChainAmountsLocaleTest` reruns the wallet broadcast suites, the dhive vectors and the hive-uri tests in ar-SA, de-DE, hi-IN, fa-IR and bn-BD with unchanged expected JSON.
- Typed amounts: every amount field (transfer, recurrent executions, swap, tip, stake) reads the keyboard of the app language through `Amounts.typedText`: any Unicode decimal digit becomes ASCII, and the Arabic decimal separator "٫" and a comma (RN, every language) become '.'. A typed number has no grouping, so the one separator is the decimal point; a text with two kinds of separator ("1.000,5", "1,000.5") or the Arabic thousands separator "٬" is ambiguous and refused (the field keeps its text). `Asset`, the dApp bridge and the operation builders still take ASCII only. Field text (`StakeMath.hpText`, max, use balance) stays POSIX; its display twin (`hpDisplay`) localizes. `ChainPathsStayPosixTest` also checks that the amount composers (`TransferModel`, `WalletActionModels`, `StakeModels`) reach the display formatter only from their listed display members. Time pickers use the language's clock (`LocaleFormat.uses24Hour`, as the shown times).
- `Num.fixed` rounds half away from zero on the exact binary value at any number of decimals (JS toFixed; checked against BigDecimal HALF_UP over 3000 values and 0 to 10 decimals); before, more than 3 decimals rounded the double product half to even, and values below about 0.001 at 1 to 3 decimals came out as zero. Chain callers (the dApp bridge's HP to VESTS at 6 decimals) now match RN's toFixed; the wallet golden vectors are unchanged.
- Display texts of list rows (`VoterRow.rewardText`, `percentText`, `CoinActivity.displayValue`) are computed when the row is built.
- Platform sites: Android `java.text.DateFormat`, `SimpleDateFormat` display, `DateUtils` and `String.format` numbers, iOS `DateFormatter` display, `.formatted`, `RelativeDateTimeFormatter` and `String(format:)` numbers go through `LocaleFormat` (parsing and API formats keep `en_US_POSIX` / `Locale.US`). Text built in a model keeps the language it was built in until the model rebuilds it (most screens rebuild on the switch; see app language).

OS-read strings (`l10n-system-strings`): what the system shows outside the app's screens, generated at build time from the same locale files (not committed) and listed in `shared/l10n/system-strings.json` (which key each shows; keys under `native.widget`, `native.widgets`, `native.system`, `native.permission`, or reused RN keys such as `notification.notification` and `post.listen`). Each language shows its translation under the catalog's rule (build-logic `SystemStrings.resolve`: set, parses, no placeholder English lacks), else English, so every file holds every string.
- Android, `:androidApp:generateL10nResources` (build-logic `SystemStrings.android`, with `locales_config.xml`): `strings.xml` in `values` (English) and one folder per language from its OS tag (`values-de`, `values-pt-rPT`, `values-b+zh+Hans`, `values-b+sr+Latn`, `values-b+fil`, legacy `values-iw` and `values-in`; bare `values-pt`, `values-sr` and `values-zh` hold what `LocaleMatch` picks for a device no region or script folder matches, such as pt-BR, Cyrillic sr-RS or zh-SG), for the widget picker labels and descriptions the manifest and provider infos name. Escaped for aapt (apostrophes, quotes, a leading `@` or `?`, XML entities, `formatted="false"` with `%`, quoted white space). `app_name` stays "Ecency" in every language (brand, `translatable="false"`). Text the app sets at run time reads the catalog in the app language instead of resources (that follows the in-app language below Android 13 too): the notification channel names and descriptions (`SystemChannels`), the dictation and "Listen paused" notifications and Listen's skip buttons, and the Glance widgets. `SystemLanguageSurfaces` renames the existing channels (never creates one) and redraws every widget when the language changes; a widget drawn in a process the app has not started in reads the saved language (`WidgetLanguage`), else the device's. Listen's skip button names are set when its service starts.
- iOS, `:shared:generateAppleL10n` (`SystemStrings.apple`, into `shared/build/generated/l10n/apple`): `<tag>.lproj/InfoPlist.strings` per language (the permission prompts: Face ID, camera, photo library, microphone), which the app's "Localized system strings" phase converts to binary and copies into the bundle's lproj folders before signing; `project.yml` keeps the English base (a build-logic test holds it equal to en-US). iOS shows them in the app's language from Settings > Ecency > Language, which the picker writes (applied at the next launch). `CFBundleDisplayName` stays "Ecency". The widget extension runs in its own process and does not link the shared framework: its messages, the plural rules and the digits and grouping of every language come as Swift data (`WidgetL10nData.generated.swift`, copied by the widget target's "Widget copy" phase, gitignored), formatted by `Widgets/WidgetL10n.swift` as the catalog does (own message or English with English plural forms, language digits, bidi isolates in ar, fa, he). The app hands its language over through the App Group (`Widgets/language` beside the snapshot, `IosWidgetStore.handOver`, then the timelines reload); before the app ran, the extension matches the device language as `LocaleMatch`. The gallery name and description follow when WidgetKit asks the extension again. Decided against `.xcstrings` for the extension: a bundle lookup follows the device's language, not the app's, and the plural rules would be Foundation's rather than the catalog's. The share and notification service extensions show no text of their own (only the brand as display name), so they get none.
- Tests: build-logic `SystemStringsTest` (folder mapping, Android and Apple escaping, the translation rule, all 39 languages give every file and string, the project's English base equals en-US, the manifest names only generated strings); Android `SystemStringsFlowsTest` (each of the 39 languages reads its generated widget labels through the system's resource lookup, equal to the catalog; widget text and digits follow the app language; the cold-process widget language; channels renamed on a language change; the dictation notification); iOS `WidgetL10nTests` (the widget table equals the shared catalog for every message, count and language, digits, RTL isolation, parser quoting, device language matching equals `LocaleMatch`, the App Group hand-off read on the extension's code path, InfoPlist.strings of every language in the app bundle).

iOS screens (`l10n-ui-ios`): every text a SwiftUI screen shows or VoiceOver speaks comes from the catalog.
- Sources: the copy the two apps share is a shared copy object (`UiCopy.shared.CANCEL`, `EditorUiCopy.shared.cost(cost:)`, `LoginCopy.shared.TITLE`; the app's own `LoginCopy`, `AccountCopy` and `BrowserCopy` enums are gone, they shadowed these). iOS only copy reads the generated Swift `L`: an RN key when native's English is RN's word for word (`L.Post.a11yVotes(count:)`, `L.Schedules.pending`, recorded in `rn-reuse.json`), else a key under `native.ios.<area>` with native's wording (`L.Native.Ios.Vote.failed`). Shared copy never names a member after an Objective-C macro (`YES`, `NO`): the framework header would not compile, so the editor's are `YES_BUTTON` and `NO_BUTTON`.
- Catalog strings are Swift `String`s, so `Text(copy)`, `Button(copy)`, `.navigationTitle(copy)` take SwiftUI's verbatim (`StringProtocol`) initializers and nothing is looked up again as a `LocalizedStringKey`.
- Whole messages: what was built from fragments is one message (`L.Native.Ios.Card.payoutDeclined(payout:)`, `SignInAction` gives one message per action instead of "Sign in to " + a verb); counts are plurals, so English now says "1 reply" and "1 reblog" where native said "1 replies". Code never decides by comparing copy: the biometric offer names Face ID or Touch ID through `native.ios.lock.offer_title` instead of replacing "biometrics" in the English, a drafts confirmation knows it moves by a flag. Label rows keep RN's `label: value` layout, a colon between two catalog texts.
- Live switch: the root rebuilds with `.id(language)`, so nothing may hold copy across it: lists of labels are computed (`CommentSortLabel.all`, the tag feed's sorts, the welcome lines), never a `static let`.
- Numbers and dates: counts through `Fmt.int` and whole percents through `Fmt.percent` (`App/Fmt.swift`, over `LocaleFormat`; `Text("\(n)")` would format with the device locale), dates through `LocaleFormat` (drafts and schedules show their time with `dateTime` in the device's time zone, where both apps showed the raw UTC ISO text; Android does the same, `shownDraftDate`, and both read the server's zone-less times as UTC). System pickers (`DatePicker`) follow `\.locale`, which `LanguageRoot` sets.
- Left as literals, listed with a reason in `iosApp/l10n-literals-allowed.txt` (tab separated, as Android's list): brand and mascot names (Ecency, Esie, GIF), queue labels, URL prefixes, crash report text, chain tickers and RN's already delegated fragment, debug and fake data screens. Never localized: `accessibilityIdentifier`s, test ids, outbound text (link callbacks, device names, analytics), Apple's names Face ID and Touch ID.
- Checks: build-logic `SwiftCopyLintTest` scans `iosApp` (app and extensions) for a literal with a letter as the shown argument of `Text`, `Label`, `Button`, `Toggle`, `Picker`, `TextField`, `Section`, `Menu`, `Stepper` and the like (`verbatim:` included), of `navigationTitle`, `alert`, `confirmationDialog`, `accessibilityLabel`, `accessibilityHint`, `accessibilityValue`, and the value of copy arguments (`title:`, `message:`, `label:`, `placeholder:`, `prompt:`, `action:`, ...), fails on an allowlist entry that matches nothing, and on a `static let` or file level `let` that reads the catalog; `RnReuseTest` also maps the Swift `L` accessors back to keys (from the generated L.swift's doc lines) and fails on an RN key the Swift code reads unrecorded. iOS `CopyLanguageTests` (labels read again after a switch, `Fmt` and the card label in de-DE and ar-SA, the iOS only plurals). Existing UI tests run in en-US and keep their English.

Crowdin (`l10n-crowdin`, owner decisions 10-22 and 10-26: ICU aware, native only plural keys, RN unchanged):
- `crowdin.yml` (repo root): the one source `en-US.json` (plain key-value JSON, auto detected; Crowdin reads the ICU inside values by itself, there is no per-file ICU switch), `preserve_hierarchy`, and `update_option: update_as_unapproved` (a changed English string keeps its translations, marked for review; without it Crowdin deletes them). Docs: https://support.crowdin.com/developer/configuration-file/ (Changed Strings Update), https://support.crowdin.com/icu-message-syntax/.
- Native plural keys: the 23 RN messages with fake plurals that native showed are `native.plurals.<RN key path>` in en-US (`native.plurals.notification.monthly_posts` for `notification.monthly_posts`, Kotlin `L.native.plurals.notification.monthlyPosts(count = n)`), with real ICU plurals and the RN placeholder names; they are not in `rn-reuse.json`. RN's keys and texts are untouched (RN cannot format plurals, above). Native only copy that counted (`native.ui.auth.pin_digits`, `native.editor_extras.day_streak`, `native.chat_sheets.new_group_hint`, `native.broadcast_errors.too_large`) became plurals in place. When RN is retired, delete RN's fake plural keys (the RN paths listed in `tools/l10n/seed-native-plurals.mjs`) together with the other RN only keys.
- Seeded translations: `node native/tools/l10n/seed-native-plurals.mjs` (from the repo root; `--check` to only report) fills each language's missing `native.plurals.*` key from its translation of the RN key: `{count} word{suffix}` becomes a plural with that word in every CLDR form of the language, `failed attempt(s)` style text becomes `{count, plural, .. {# <text>}}` (RN showed the count before it), anything else is copied as it is (a plain `{n}` is valid ICU and shows as before). Skipped and so English: untranslated keys (equal to RN's English), ru-RU `checkin` (stem before `{suffix}`), uk-UA `checkin` and `monthly_posts` (the suffix was the noun itself). A key Crowdin already translated is never overwritten, so the script is safe to rerun.
- Owner checklist (Crowdin project 340925; nothing here is automated):
  1. Settings > QA checks: turn on "ICU syntax" (marks broken plural or placeholder syntax in the editor).
  2. Make the repo equal Crowdin first: run `crowdin-download.yml` (or label a PR `sync-translations`) and merge its PR.
  3. Rebase this branch on that fresh `development`, then reset the 38 translation files to development's versions (`git checkout development -- src/config/locales/ ':!src/config/locales/en-US.json'`) so no stale seed survives (the script never overwrites an existing `native.plurals` key), rerun `node native/tools/l10n/seed-native-plurals.mjs` and commit. The seed then starts from Crowdin's current translations.
  4. Merge. The push runs `crowdin-upload.yml`: the new `native.*` keys arrive as new source strings; RN's strings are unchanged.
  5. Upload the seeded translations once, from a checkout of the merge commit: `crowdin upload translations --branch development --dryrun` first, then without `--dryrun`, ideally one language at a time (`-l ru`, the Crowdin language id). Do this right after the merge, before translators work on the new strings: identical translations are skipped only while the repo still equals Crowdin, so only the new native keys gain text.
  6. Fix the three broken translations in the editor (Checks above: el-GR `post_poll.interpretation_token`, pt-PT `transfer.confirm_summary`, bg-BG `profile.joined`).
  7. Pull again and run the native `L10nCatalogTest` report (`l10n rejected:` lines); ask translators to add plural forms (Editor > Advanced filter > String type ICU; Copy Source fills the forms of each language).

Tests: generator `./gradlew :build-logic:test` (also part of `:shared:check`): `L10nGeneratorTest` (typed accessors, naming, tables, determinism, the real files), `MessageArgsTest`, `PluralRuleCompilerTest`; shared `IcuMessageTest`, `PluralRulesTest` (golden categories per language family), `L10nCatalogTest` (switching, RN style id matching, fallback for missing, malformed, mistyped and extra placeholder messages, dropped placeholders allowed, every message of every locale formats, RTL isolation, pseudo locales, decode cost); Android host `IcuOracleTest` (plural rules and formatter against ICU4J for all 39 locales); formatting: Android host `CldrFormatsOracleTest` (vendored data is ICU4J's; numbers, percent, compact, every date pattern in three time zones, relative times, durations, ages and message numbers against ICU4J for all 39 languages) and `ChainPathsStayPosixTest`, build-logic `FormatDataGeneratorTest`, shared `LocaleFormatGoldenTest` (en, de, ru, ar, ja, hi goldens on every target, plural interplay, the facade, edge cases), `DisplayFormatsLocaleTest` (each converted shared site in other languages), `ChainAmountsLocaleTest`, `BalanceAnalyticsTest.labelsAndNumbersFollowTheAppLanguage`, iOS `WalletTests.testCopyAndFormatting` and `ProfileTests.testJoinDateFollowsTheLocale`; Android `SettingsFlowsTest.languageRowTitleComesFromTheCatalog`, iOS `SettingsTests.testLanguageRowTitleComesFromTheCatalog`. App language: build-logic `SystemLocalesTest` (tags, locales_config, the real files); shared `LanguageSyncTest` (every branch of the rule, tag round trip, RTL set) and `SettingsModelTest` (catalog and OS follow the setting, adoption, device change ignored, restart, "System default", RN import reaches the OS, tests without the switch stay English); Android `AppLanguageTest` (live switch recomposes a plain child, a lazy item and a dialog with state kept, RTL for ar, fa, he, LTR for ku-TR, `LocaleManager` round trip) and `SettingsFlowsTest.pickingALanguageSwitchesTheCopyLive`; iOS `AppLanguageTests` (live redraw, direction, bundle languages, app only `AppleLanguages`, picker writes it).

## Points and perks (wave 5a)

RN `screens/perks` (questsCard, spendOptions), `screens/spinGame` with `containers/spinGameContainer.ts` (ab9a588a82, 184d50d2c8, 764e5a24d9) and `components/spinGame`, `providers/queries/pointQueries.ts`, `utils/refreshQuests.ts`, `providers/ecency/ePoint.ts` and the adapter's `recordActivity`, over SDK 2.4.26 `modules/quests` and `modules/games`. Shared `api/ecency/PerksApi.kt`, `app/Perks.kt` (`QuestCatalog`, `QuestsData`, `PerksModel`, `StreakFreezeBuys`, `QuestRefreshes`, `SpendOptions`), `app/SpinGame.kt`, `app/PointActivities.kt`. Android `ui/PerksScreens.kt`; iOS `Screens/PerksViews.swift`. Inventory with status: points-inventory.md (wave notes).

- **Perks** (`AppScreen.Perks`, from the header gift, the editor quest chip, the Waves "check in" row and the `spin` push): quests from `POST /private-api/quests {username}` (public), asked on open, on every return to the foreground (RN opts this query into refetch on focus) and 70 s after an activity of the account (`QuestRefreshes`, one timer per account). Daily, Weekly and Monthly tabs from the SDK catalog without the spin; "progress/goal", the bar (green when done), the streak badge, "{n} in reserve" and "Protect streak (300 Points)". A failure without data shows RN's QueryErrorRetry texts; a failed refetch keeps the numbers. "Use your points": Daily spin, Boost Plus, Top up RC, Promote a post (the wallet wave's Redeem), Account boost (an in-app purchase).
- **Streak freeze** (`POST /private-api/streak-freeze/buy {code, idempotency_key}`): a confirmation first (RN buys on the tap), one key per confirmation made at the confirmation, kept app-wide per account while the outcome is unknown (a timeout, a thrown transport error, a 5xx or an unreadable 200), so the next confirmation replays it and never buys twice; a 4xx ends the key. 402 opens Get Points for the account, 409 is silent, anything else RN's "Could not buy a streak freeze. Please try again."; the quests are asked again after every outcome. The buy runs in the app's scope: a closed screen does not cut it off and a reopened one joins it. A key goes only with a definite outcome, not with a logout (the returning account's next confirmation replays it); the keys live in memory. After the outcome the owned count is asked again, queued behind a refresh already on its way.
- **Spin** (`AppScreen.SpinGame`, "Free Points"): `POST /private-api/get-game {game_type: spin, code}` always from the server; every status request is numbered app-wide (`SpinStatusRequests`), only the newest sets the counters and only its failure alerts. SPIN & WIN turns the rings for 8 s; one claim at a time (a press while one runs sends nothing, also after the rings stopped); a press asks a fresh status, drops it when a newer request started meanwhile, status 18 shows none left, no key says "Game key missing", else `POST /private-api/post-game {game_type, code, key}` in the app's scope. After the claim: 5 for a pack (status 3) else 0, the score bubble, the status again, unless the screen closed meanwhile (no status check from a claim that outlived its screen); no quest refresh (RN asks none). None left: the 5 SPINS pack (an in-app purchase) and "Next free spin in H:m:ss" (moment's `utc(diff).format('H:m:ss')`, pinned to node output, every 3 s).
- **Earning points** (`PointActivities`): `POST /private-api/usr-activity {ty, bl?, tx?, code}` after a vote (120), a new post (100), a new reply or wave (110; edits nothing), a reblog or cross post (130), each with the broadcast's transaction id (none: nothing sent), a key login (20), and the check-in (10) on the Waves tab and now when a post or comment is opened (15 min per account). Three tries (React Query `retry: 2`); still failing, it is parked per account in the device preferences (`points.pendingActivities.<user>`, at most 50, removed by a wipe) and replayed 3 s after the start, on every later return to the foreground and when the app lock stops hiding the app (a report is never sent while it hides the app), for the signed-in account: one replay per account at a time, each entry read again right before it is sent. A removed vote and an undone reblog report too, as the SDK's mutations do. An account without an Ecency session parks nothing. A report that went through schedules the quest refresh.

Deliberate differences
- The streak freeze asks for a confirmation and keeps its key after a lost answer (RN: a new key per tap, no confirmation).
- The status answer is untrusted: a non-2xx is an error ("An error occurred" or the server's JSON `message`), not a status; a key with white space is no key; a `next_date` without a zone is read as UTC (moment reads it in the device's zone).
- Spin rings: Material-style arcs drawn natively, not RN's SpinIndicator geometry; the ESTM tags and the logo are RN's images.
- The empty tier text has no dash ("No quests here yet, check back soon."); it cannot show with the 2.4.26 catalog.
- The check-in used to be sent once with no retry here; it now follows RN (three tries, parked).
- Not sent: the SDK's analytics `recordActivity("spin-rolled")` (in RN it posts an empty url and domain, which Plausible rejects; see Analytics).

Debug (fake data only, nothing is paid or claimed): Android `--es openScreen perks|spinGame`, `--ez demoStreak true`; iOS `-openScreen perks|spinGame`, `-demoStreak YES`. Demo spins hand out a synthetic key once per run, then none are left.

Tests: shared `PerksTest` (15), `SpinGameTest` (14), `PointActivitiesTest` (7), the check-in cases of `WavesGapsTest`; Android `PerksFlowsTest` (5); iOS `PerksTests` (4), `PerksUITests` (2). Mutation-checked (`points-mutate.py` in the session scratchpad): see the wave report.

## In-app purchases (wave 6b)

RN `providers/iap` (expo-iap 4.6: Play Billing 9 and StoreKit 2), `containers/inAppPurchaseContainer.tsx`, `screens/boost`, `screens/accountBoost`, the spin game's pack, `registerAccountModal`'s paid account, `parsePurchaseUrl`, `ecency.ts purchaseOrder`, checked against ePoints' `PurchaseOrderResource` and purchase worker. Shared: `app/Store.kt` (the store seam), `app/Purchases.kt` (`PurchaseService`, the money rules), `app/StoreScreens.kt` (`StoreScreenModel`), `app/DemoStore.kt`, `api/ecency/PurchaseApi.kt`. Android `iap/PlayStore.kt` (Billing 9.1.0), `ui/StoreScreens.kt`; iOS `App/StoreKitStore.swift`, `Screens/StoreViews.swift`, `EcencyApp/Store/Products.storekit`. Inventory: iap-inventory.md with the wave notes.

```kotlin
env.store = PlayStore(context) { activity }      // or StoreKitStore(), DemoStore(...) in fake-data runs; StorePlatform.None sells nothing
env.purchases.start(); env.purchases.onForeground()
env.storeScreenModel(StoreScreenKind.POINTS, username, productId)   // start(), buy(id), confirm(), dismissAlert()
```

- **Products** (RN's SKUs, all consumables): `099points 199points 499points 999points 4999points 9999points` (Get Points, highest price first, "N POINTS" from the store title, "BEST DEAL!" and "POPULAR!", then the FREE POINTS row that opens the spin game), `999boosts` (Get Account Boost, "Boost+  |  {HP of 553311 VESTS} HP"), `499spins` (the spin game's "5 SPINS" when none is left; a delivered pack reads the status again) and `999accounts` (register: "Buy Account - {price}", "Claim Paid Account" when one waits on the device; a purchase link hides the free card). Products the store does not price are not shown.
- **Order** (RN, unchanged on the wire): `POST /private-api/purchase-order {platform: play_store|app_store, product, receipt, user, meta?}` plus the signed-in account's `code` (RN's interceptor; ePoints ignores it); receipt = Play purchase token or StoreKit 2 `jwsRepresentation`; `user` = the screen's account (a link or the QR flow) or the signed-in one; a paid account sends `user` = signed-in account or "ecency" and `meta {username, email, referral}`. 60 s bound; 3 tries 1 s then 2 s apart; a timeout stops (outcome unknown). ePoints answers 201 when it queued the order (its worker verifies with the store, delivers, and acknowledges Play purchases itself) and 409 for a receipt it stored before or a failed insert.
- **Money rules** (`PurchaseService`): finish (Play consume, StoreKit finish) only after Ecency accepted; a failed finish is no failure (retried at the next recovery). A pending payment (Play PENDING, StoreKit `.pending`, Ask to Buy) is never posted and never shown as paid ("Your payment is pending..."); it is delivered when the store reports it paid. A 409 finishes the purchase only when this device saw Ecency accept that receipt or when Play reports it acknowledged (Ecency acknowledges after delivery). ePoints answers 409 for any failed insert too (its database down or locked, for the whole outage), so no number of 409s, however far apart, counts as a stored order: the purchase stays unfinished and is posted again silently (30 s, up to 3 times a run, then at each start and foreground) while the user sees "Your purchase is being processed...". On Play an order Ecency failed lapses into Google's automatic refund instead of being consumed with nothing delivered; on the App Store the transaction waits in `Transaction.unfinished` (a finished one is never refunded). One receipt is never posted twice at once. The target of every purchase is saved before the sheet opens (`purchases.intents`; a wipe removes them), so an interrupted purchase goes to the account it was bought for. An earlier purchase of the same product still on the device keeps its saved target: a paid one is delivered to that account instead of buying again, a pending one blocks a second purchase, and the user is told when that account is another one ("An earlier purchase of this product is being completed for @user first."). An App Store purchase Ecency keeps refusing (409 counted on 3 recoveries, that is a start, a return to the foreground or an account change, at least 6 h apart and never the automatic rechecks; or counted once and still unaccepted a day after the store's purchase date, never the age alone) stops blocking: it stays unfinished and is posted again, a new purchase opens, and the user is told once to contact support ("An earlier purchase of this product could not be completed..."); a paid account then offers "Buy Account" again so another name can be chosen. A saved target is bound to its purchase the first time that purchase is processed, so a later buy of the same product never changes whom it goes to. An Ask to Buy request (StoreKit's `.pending`, never listed in `Transaction.unfinished`) keeps blocking a second purchase across recovery until StoreKit reports its transaction, or for 48 h. On the App Store a purchase is known on the device by its transaction id (a re-signed receipt is not a new order; marks an earlier build kept by the receipt's hash still count) and one Ecency accepted is finished without being posted again, without touching any saved target, and never blocks a new purchase. A refunded purchase drops its saved target. A paid account's saved details survive a transient store error (network, store service) and go with a terminal one. Every unfinished paid purchase is posted again at start (StoreKit's transaction listener runs from launch), on foreground and after an account change; a revoked one is finished without an order. A paid account's name is checked against the chain rules before the sheet and before the order.
- **Errors** (RN providers/iap/errors.ts): a cancel is a breadcrumb only; each store code is its own Sentry issue (fingerprint `iap` + code, tags `iap.stage`, `iap.code`, `iap.product`, `iap.platform`), one failure delivered twice within 5 s is told once; Play's billing unavailable shows RN's "old version of Google Play"; Get Points toasts "Points purchase failed\n{message}", the other screens alert "Warning".
- **Links**: `https://ecency.com/purchase?type=boost|points|account&username&product_id&email&referral` (Android intent filter `/purchase`): Account boost, Get Points (asks "Confirm Purchase @user" / "Buying {points} for {user} at {currency} {price}" once; not restored after process death), or register selling only the paid account.
- **Local testing, never a real purchase**: Xcode runs use `Products.storekit` (scheme StoreKit configuration; synthetic products, nothing charged); the iOS unit tests drive StoreKit through `SKTestSession` (purchase, Ask to Buy, refund). The refund's push through `Transaction.updates` is not asserted (the test session does not always deliver it; the updates loop is covered by the Ask to Buy test): the refund test checks the recovery path, `Transaction.unfinished` read as REVOKED and finished without an order by a restarted service. Fake-data runs (`-fakeData YES`, Android `--ez demoAuth true`) use `DemoStore`: it completes, leaves pending or cancels a purchase offline and the order goes to the demo answers. Debug: Android `--es openScreen getPoints|accountBoost|getPoints:<user>:<product>`, iOS `-openScreen` with the same values.

Deliberate differences from RN
- Pending payments are not posted; a 409 is not taken at its word (above); recovery delivers to the saved target, not to whoever is signed in; iOS recovery reads `Transaction.unfinished` (RN's list missed consumables).
- A cancelled sheet shows nothing (RN's Get Points toasted "Points purchase failed" and register alerted "Fail!" on a cancel). Get Points shows its toast without RN's extra "Warning" alert on top; Account boost alerts a failed order (RN showed nothing).
- New copy where RN has none: the pending notice and "Your purchase is being processed..." for an order whose outcome is not known yet.
- The link's confirmation is an alert with the account's ribbon (RN's action sheet with the avatar); Account boost scrolls on small screens.
- Account binding (not RN): every purchase carries the account it is for as a token (`PurchaseService.accountToken`, a SHA-256 name-based UUID v8 of the lowercased name, no personal data): Play `obfuscatedAccountId`, StoreKit `appAccountToken`, back as `StorePurchase.accountToken`. Who an order credits (`creditedUser`): the target saved with that purchase on this device, else the account the token names among the accounts on the device (an interrupted purchase never goes to whoever is signed in when it names another of them), else the newest saved target of the product (only one of the token's account), else the signed-in account; a token naming no account here (and no saved target of its account) goes to the signed-in account with a breadcrumb. A saved target binds only to a purchase of its own account's token. A buy that finds an earlier unfinished purchase of the product credited to another account (by its saved target or token) tells the buyer ("being completed for @..."), saves no target of its own and shows no success for that delivery. Recovery at start waits for the accounts to load (iOS starts purchases before the session).

Owner actions (none done here). v4 ships as the update of the RN app (its app ids), so the store products, their prices and ePoints' verification of the package and bundle id already exist: nothing to create there (a separate preview app id would need all of that again, or store purchases off). ePoints:
- answer 409 only for the `receipt_hash` IntegrityError and 503 for any other database error (today every failed insert is a 409, so an App Store purchase made during an outage waits until this device sees a 201, and one refused for good stops blocking only after 3 refusals hours apart, or one refusal and a day);
- reopen a FAIL order on a duplicate receipt rather than answer 409 (it also blocks redelivery of a settled pending payment for RN builds);
- dedupe App Store orders by `transaction_id` (verified, before fulfilment): the unique key is `md5(receipt)` only, and StoreKit can hand out the same transaction with another signed form (`jwsRepresentation`), which today would be stored and delivered as a second order. This app keys its own marks on the transaction id and never posts an accepted transaction again, but RN builds and reinstalls do not have those marks.

Tests: shared `PurchasesTest` (21), `CrashAndAnalyticsTest` (8); Android `PurchaseFlowsTest` (9) and the purchase cases of `PerksFlowsTest`, `RegisterFlowsTest`; iOS `PurchaseTests` (6, StoreKitTest), `PurchaseUITests` (4).

## Ecency Pro (issue #3555)

Pro by in-app purchase, over the purchase service above (same order contract, money rules and recovery). Shared `app/Pro.kt` (`ProModel`, `ProCopy`), `ProMembersModel.activate` (ProfileSupport.kt); Android `ui/ProScreens.kt`; iOS `Screens/ProViews.swift`. Web wording from vision-next `pro.*` (`features/pro`).

- **Product** `1999pro` (`PurchaseSkus.PRO`, web `PRO_SKU`): $19.99 for 12 months, buying again while a member extends by 12 months (Ecency's Pro fulfilment). Play: a one-time in-app product, consumed after Ecency accepted the order like every SKU. App Store: a Non-Renewing Subscription in `Products.storekit`; `StoreKitStore.sells` takes it as non-renewing or consumable (both are finished after the order and wait in `Transaction.unfinished` until then), so either store setup works.
- **Perks card** (above "Use your points", signed in only): non-members "Go Pro for {store price} a year. ..." (no price: the plain text), members "You are a Pro member.". Membership is the badge's roster (`ProMembersModel`, `GET /private-api/pro-members`).
- **Sheet**: title, web's subtitle and the four benefits (verified badge, free blog at yourname.blogs.ecency.com, supports Ecency, early access); the buy button with the store's localized price ("Get Pro for $19.99 a year", members "Extend by 12 months for $19.99" with a note); none when the store does not sell it ("not available from the store right now"). No link-out and no copy pointing to buying elsewhere.
- **Purchase**: for the signed-in account only (`PurchaseTarget(user = signed-in)`, its account token in the transaction, no gifting); refused while any Pro buy is under way (another account's included). Accepted order: "Payment received. Activating your membership." until the account is on the roster, then "Welcome to Ecency Pro" (an extension: "Payment received. Your Pro membership is being extended by 12 months.", since the roster already lists the account and cannot show the new expiry). The roster is read past the API's 5 minute cache at once and every 30 s, each waiting account for at most 12 reads, several accounts together (Ecency delivers from a queue and edge caches the list), for every delivered `1999pro` including recovered ones, by the account it was credited to. What happened to an account's latest Pro purchase (pending, processing, delivered) is kept app-wide (`ProPurchases`), so a payment that settles later, a recovered purchase or an outcome that landed while Perks was closed shows when the sheet opens; it is cleared once the success was seen. Failures and cancels as the other purchase screens (a cancel shows nothing and orders nothing); another account's outcome never shows on the signed-in account's sheet. An interrupted purchase is delivered at the next start to the account it was bought for (saved target, as Points).
- **Pro prices of perks**: neither web nor the SDK has a Pro price on the client. AI costs and free allowances are the server's answers (`ai-assist-price`, `ai-generate-price`, `ai-transcribe-price`), shown as answered, so a Pro allowance shows once the server applies it; the streak freeze label is the SDK's fixed `STREAK_FREEZE_PRICE` (300, as web) while the server prices the buy. A Pro freeze price needs the SDK and server first (open item).
- **Debug**: fake-data runs sell `1999pro` from `DemoStore`; a demo Pro order puts its account on the demo roster. `-demoNoPro YES` (iOS) / `--ez demoNoPro true` (Android) empties the demo roster (spike-alice is Pro by default).
- **Money risk until the backend lands**: ePoints answers 201 to a purchase order without checking the product (`purchase_order` POST); its worker verifies later against `APP_STORE_PRODUCT_MAP` / `PLAY_STORE_PRODUCT_MAP`, which lack `1999pro`. The app finishes (Play consume, StoreKit finish) on that 201, so a `1999pro` bought before the backend change is consumed and its order then FAILS: paid, nothing delivered. The client cannot see the worker's failure; the guard is the order of the owner items below (no store product, nothing to buy).
- **Owner items, in this order** (not done here):
  1. ePoints: add `1999pro` to `APP_STORE_PRODUCT_MAP` and `PLAY_STORE_PRODUCT_MAP` (`fulfillment.py` already routes products ending in `pro` to the Pro fulfilment, months from `STRIPE_PRODUCT_MAP`); ideally also validate the product at the purchase-order POST and answer 400 for an unknown one (the app then keeps the purchase unfinished instead of consuming it). Deploy and verify with a sandbox / license-tester order.
  2. Only then create `1999pro` in Play Console (one-time, $19.99) and App Store Connect (Non-Renewing Subscription, review notes and screenshot).
  3. Refunds: App Store Server Notifications `REFUND` and Play Real-time developer notifications (voided purchases) into the same revoke path as Stripe (ePoints revokes only Stripe today), so a refunded store purchase ends the membership.
- Follow-ups: claiming the free blog and the expiry date (the roster has none).

Tests: shared `ProTest` (26: stale pending, token binding, earlier purchase of another account at buy, account switch, settled pending, kept outcomes, multi-account activation); Android `ProFlowsTest` (4) and the token case of `PurchaseFlowsTest`; iOS `ProTests` (3, StoreKitTest), `ProUITests` (2).

## Crash reporting

RN `App.tsx` and `utils/sentryUtils.ts`. Android `crash/SentryReporting.kt` (sentry-android-core and -ndk 8.20.0, started in `EcencyApplication`, auto-init off in the manifest); iOS `App/CrashReporting.swift` (sentry-cocoa 8.58.4 over SPM, started in the App's init); shared `app/CrashReports.kt` (`CrashReporter`, `CrashScrubber`, `IapErrors`).

- DSN: RN's project (the DSN in RN `App.tsx`, shared `CrashDsn.DEFAULT`; a DSN only allows sending events, so it is public like RN's). Build setting `ecency.sentryDsn` in `native/local.properties` (Android also `ECENCY_SENTRY_DSN`), which CI writes from the optional secret `NATIVE_SENTRY_DSN`: empty is RN's project in release builds and nothing in debug builds, `off` turns reporting off, any other value must be an https DSN or nothing is sent.
- As RN: `sendDefaultPii` false, no tracing, no session replay, errors at Sentry's default sample rate (all), release `<app id>@<version>+<build>` with the build number as dist, environment `production` or `debug`. Failed HTTP answers are not turned into events (RN does not).
- Scrubbing (every event and breadcrumb, `beforeSend` / `beforeBreadcrumb`): RN's WIF and Bearer/Basic patterns plus JWT-shaped tokens, long hex (keys, signatures), query and JSON fields named code, token, key, memo, pin, password, signature, receipt, email and similar, e-mail addresses; breadcrumb data, extras and named contexts recursively (depth 6), values under secret keys dropped whole, request headers and cookies removed, no user. The scrubber is total: a failure returns a placeholder instead of the text, and `beforeSend` never throws. RN's benign errors are dropped (cancellations, wrong PIN, invalid credentials, a cancelled signing).
- Reported from shared code: store errors and order failures (IapErrors), a 409 taken as recorded (warning), the error boundary's catches. Fake-data runs report nothing.
- Symbols (docs/release-builds.md, "Crash symbols"): the store workflows upload the Android R8 mapping and the iOS dSYMs to the same project with `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN`, as RN did. Android events carry the mapping id CI generated (`BuildConfig.PROGUARD_UUID`, Sentry `proguardUuid`); iOS dSYMs match by their own image ids.

## Analytics

RN `providers/plausible` and `usePlausibleTracker`: one Plausible `pageview` per opened post (`POST {host}/api/event {name: "pageview", url: "app://<android|ios>.ecency.com/<post url>", domain: "ecency.com", force: true}`, RN's user agent `Ecency/<version> (<OS> <version>; <model>) Version/4.0 Chrome/<version> Mobile`), fire and forget, nothing while the lock hides content. No key exists for this endpoint. RN has no opt-out, so neither has this app. Host: `ecency.plausibleHost` (Android also `ECENCY_PLAUSIBLE_HOST`): empty is RN's `https://pl.ecency.com` in release builds and off in debug builds, `off` turns it off, anything but a bare https host turns it off. The SDK's `spin-rolled` activity is not sent (RN posts it with an empty url and domain).

## Chats

RN `screens/chats`, `providers/chat` (mattermost.ts, mattermostWebSocket.ts), `config/chatApi.ts`, `hooks/useMattermostWebSocket.ts`, the chat sheets (channel and message options, new group, rename, who reacted, emoji picker), the settings DM privacy row and the profile and quick profile "Message", including RN's group conversations and who-reacted work (4e8ea6078e, 03c324070b, 144e088763, bce22f3c39). Shared: `api/chat/ChatApi.kt`, `ChatModels.kt`; `app/ChatService.kt` (sessions, badge, privacy), `ChatsModel.kt`, `ChatThreadModel.kt`, `ChatSheets.kt`, `ChatSocket.kt`, `ChatFormat.kt` (RN's pure helpers), `DemoChat.kt` (offline fake). UI: Android `ui/ChatScreens.kt`; iOS `Screens/ChatViews.swift`. Inventory row by row: chats-inventory.md with the wave notes.

```kotlin
env.chat.start(); env.chat.onForeground()           // the chat badge (apps call these next to notifications)
env.chat.chatsModel()                               // the Chats tab: show(), refresh(), setQuery(), toggleSort(), perform(row, action), join(), startDirect(), groupCreated(id)
env.chat.threadModel(channelId, type)               // a conversation: start(), setActive(), loadMore(), setText(), send(), replyTo(), startEdit(), react(), remove(), setPinned(), attach(file)
env.chat.newGroupModel(); renameModel(id, name); privacyModel(); openDirect(username)
```

- **Transport**: the chat API is Ecency's relay on RN's `ECENCY_BACKEND_API` (ecency.com) under `/api/mattermost/...`: `POST bootstrap {accessToken, refreshToken, username}` gives a chat token and the viewer's Mattermost user; every other call carries the token in `X-MM-Token`. The websocket is `wss://<that host>/api/mattermost/websocket?token=...` (RN derives it from `ECENCY_BACKEND_API`; `ACTIVITY_WEBSOCKET_URL` is the notification socket, not the chat one). Build setting: `ecency.chatBackendUrl` in `native/local.properties` (Android also `ECENCY_CHAT_BACKEND_URL`), empty for ecency.com; anything but a bare first-party `https://host` (no port, not even 443, no path, query or fragment; lower-cased) is ignored (`AppEnvironment.setChatHost`, `ChatApi.canonicalBase`) and `ChatApi` sends nothing to an untrusted base, so the access token and the chat token only ever reach Ecency.
- **Session** (RN bootstrapMattermostSession): one bootstrap per account at a time, the answer reused for 30 s, tokens in memory only and passed per call (no shared header that one account could overwrite for another). Logout, logout-all and the wipe (`accountsLeft`) void every bootstrap on the way (also for a caller that joined one already on its way), so a chat token never outlives its account; the channel hints, renames and closed DM notices (kept by channel id) that a departing account saw go too, while what only the accounts that stay saw is kept. A refused access token is renewed once (`SessionRenewals`); a call refused with 401 bootstraps again once.
- **Badge** (RN `_refreshUnreadChats`): read at account load and unlock, on foreground, after a channel was read or muted, when a read conversation closes; never while signed out, offline or locked. RN's unread rules (`unread_eligible`, muted, never-viewed non-DMs with 0 or null) are pinned by goldens from mattermost.ts.
- **List**: RN's four sections and sort orders (and sort by name), DM partners resolved through `users/ids`, group titles and two avatars, community avatars from `hive-NNN`, relative times, unread counts, the status pill; options (mark read, favorite, mute, leave channel or conversation, rename group for the owner); search (350 ms, 2 characters, local plus server channels, people with a Hive name first) with Join and message buttons; New group (2 to 7 others, the server's refusal text shown); renames newer than a list fetch are kept.
- **Conversation**: pages back with `before`, a non-member joins and reads again, fewer than 15 rows asks for more; RN's join notices grouped ("N people joined"), rename notices; the "New messages" marker at the oldest unread row, scrolled to once, the channel marked read; messages with RN's parsing (images out of the text, `@name` to profile links, `:emoji:` from RN's map, links including `hive://`, mentions bold, inline markdown as RN's inlineMarkdown.ts), quotes, link cards, reactions (tap toggles, long press: who reacted), "Edited"; composer with mentions, reply, edit, Hive post link card, image attachment through the app's image upload, send with `pending_post_id`, the posting ban notice (counts down, lifts itself) or RN's toast; message options (quick reactions and the emoji picker, Reply, Copy, Share, Translate, Edit, Pin/Unpin, Delete with confirmation); pinned, members and group sheets; the DM safety notice; moderators of the channel's community may pin and delete.
- **Live**: the websocket follows the screen (on screen, app in front, not locked): authentication, ping every 30 s with a 10 s pong bound, backoff 1 s to 30 s for at most an hour; only a connection that got the server's `hello` counts as up (open-then-close before it is a failed attempt, so the backoff grows and the hour runs). It ends for good on a 15 s connect timeout (Android's OkHttp connect bound is the same 15 s), a handshake refused with 401, 403, 404 or 426, a close with one of those codes or 1002 or 1003 (RN doNotReconnectCodes; the code comes from `HasSocket.closeCode`) and a FAIL answer to the authentication challenge. The handshake status is read from the engine's own wording (`HasSocketRefused`: Ktor's "expected status code 101 but was N", 401 on both engines; OkHttp's "Expected HTTP 101 response but was 'N ...'"); Darwin reports other refused statuses as NSURLErrorBadServerResponse without the code, so on iOS a 403, 404 or 426 is retried with backoff until the hour runs out; posts, edits, deletions, reactions, typing ("a is typing...") and group renames arrive in place; the echo of one's own send is recognised by its pending id, and whichever of the echo and the send's answer lands first scrolls the thread to the sent message (the thread may sit at the unread marker; RN scrolls only when the answer lands first).
- **Lock and accounts**: no chat request while the lock hides content (Android and iOS compose nothing then); the websocket closes behind the lock; an account change empties the list and stops any open conversation (its models answer nothing for the next account).
- **Entry points**: the Chats tab, profile and quick profile "Message" (RN's "User has not joined chats" toast). RN has no push type and no link that opens a chat, so neither does this app; a restored conversation (saved navigation keeps only the channel id and type) reads its name and type from the channel list.

### Official accounts and Ask Esie (web parity, issues #3602 and #3603)
- Shared `app/OfficialAccounts.kt` ports the web's `features/chat/official-accounts.ts`: the fixed list (`esie`, `ecency`), `isOfficial` (exact, ignoring one leading `@` and case, so `esie1`, `ecency-official`, `esie` with a Cyrillic letter or in fullwidth letters never match) and `impersonates` / `safeName` (a chosen name that reads as an official account: lookalike letters, digits for letters, spaced or dotted letters, invisible characters, NFKD with marks dropped, one unreadable letter allowed; `UnicodeText` gives NFKD and the categories per platform). One source of truth for both apps.
- The badge keys only on the chat user's `username` (never `nickname`, `props.hive_username` or a post's `override_username`); posts from webhooks or bots (`relayed`) and system notices never get it. It shows on the chat list row (`ChatRow.official`), the conversation header (`ChatThreadState.official`), message author names (`ChatMessageItem.authorOfficial`) and the profile header. A text label "Official" (white on the blue fill, AA in both themes, in sp / Dynamic Type), read as "Official Ecency account" (`native.chat.official_badge*`).
- Names other people chose (`nickname`, `name`, `hive_username`, group names) that pose as an official account are replaced by the username (or a group's members) wherever chat shows them (`ChatUser.safeNickname`, `safeName`, `hiveName`, `ChatGroups.safeGroupName`).
- "Ask Esie" (`SideMenuItem.ASK_ESIE`, `native.side_menu.ask_esie`) sits after Docs and opens the direct message with `esie` like profile "Message" (`MenuAction.DirectMessage`); signed out it is not listed, as on the web. Differences from web: no pinned Esie row at the top of the chat list and no Esie avatar in the menu row (the menu uses the AI sparkles icon like its other rows).
- Tests: shared `OfficialAccountsTest` (web cases plus lookalike usernames), `OfficialChatTest`, `ShellTest`; Android `ChatFlowsTest.askEsieOpensHerConversationMarkedOfficial`; iOS `ChatTests` / `ChatUITests` official cases. Fake data: `DemoChat` answers a direct message with `esie` with one made-up greeting.

### Deliberate differences from RN
- Chat tokens are passed per call instead of one shared axios header; a 401 re-bootstraps once and an expired access token is renewed once (RN shows the error).
- "Mark as read" also zeroes `message_count` locally (RN zeroes the other counters only, so its row could keep a badge until the next load). A failed badge read keeps the last count (RN sets 0).
- The composer's link preview is made only for Hive posts: the app never fetches arbitrary third-party pages while someone types (RN reads any page's Open Graph tags with a desktop user agent). Cards that arrive with a message (`link_*` props) show as RN shows them.
- Message images and link card images go through the image proxy (RN loads them from their hosts).
- E-mail addresses in messages stay text (RN's linkify makes mailto links); link detection otherwise matches linkify-it on the golden cases.
- A send error is a toast (RN sets an error that is only visible in an empty thread). The typing event is sent at most every 2 s (RN sends one per keystroke).
- The DM safety notice, once closed, stays closed for the run of the app (RN per screen). The unread marker sits on the right row when join notices are grouped (RN counts posts, not rows).
- A URL that is not a valid pattern no longer crashes the message (RN builds a RegExp from it).
- Inline markdown (#3468) reads as web's `simpleMarkdownToHTML` reads it, not as RN's own `parseInlineMarkdown`: `render/markdown/ChatInline.kt` runs the remarkable port with only its text, escape, code span, strikethrough and emphasis rules (`InlineParser.chat()`). So `\*` escapes, a code span takes a run of backticks (`` `` a`b `` ``), `a*b*c` italicises, `snake_case` and `__init__.py` stay, and remarkable's own oddities (`**_*x*_**`) are web's too. Block constructs (lists, headings, quotes), HTML and entities stay as typed (`!ban <user> [30d]` keeps its placeholder; web's sanitizer drops it), and marks never pair across a blank line. Links and mentions are single units for the markdown (a placeholder character during the parse): marks run through them as on web (`**hi @bob**` is bold through the mention, a link inside `_..._` is italic), markers inside them never count, and a code span shows a link or mention as raw text. A link ending in markers leaves only the part that closes a mark opened before it (`**see https://x.com**`; `**see https://x.com/a_**` and `a _b_ c https://x.com/a_` keep `a_`), as web's linkify-after-emphasis does; `_` right after a link or mention is inside a word (`hi @bob_x_` stays plain, as on web). Messages over 8,000 characters (`ChatText.MAX_MARKDOWN`; Mattermost allows 16,383) show their marks as typed, since rebuilds run on the main thread; links and mentions still work. Code spans get a 12 % black shade on both apps. Spans are cached per message id and text (`ChatThreadModel.spansFor`), so a rebuild for a reaction or a new message parses only new or edited messages. Goldens: `node native/tools/render/generate-chat-markdown-fixtures.mjs` (`ChatInlineFixtures`, 387 cases, 239 of them checked against `simpleMarkdownToHTML` itself inside the generator); RN's `markdown` goldens in `ChatGoldens` are no longer asserted.
- As in RN, a reply's quote is the parent text the sender's client put in the post (`parent_message` props) when present, shown as sent and not checked against the quoted message.
- Name sorting follows ICU's root order for the characters names use (`Collate`), not a full collation.

### Owner action
Chats need a signed-in account with an Ecency access token. Nothing here was run against the production chat API: all writes and reads go through `DemoChat` in tests and fake-data runs. An unauthenticated read of `/api/mattermost/channels` answers 401 (reachable, not IP-locked); whether `bootstrap` accepts this app's user agent should be checked once with a real account before release.

Debug (fake data only): Android `--ez demoAuth true --ez seedAccounts true --es openTab CHATS`, `--es openRoute ChatThreadScreen --es arg ch-ecency` (also `ch-dm-alice`, `ch-group`); iOS `-fakeData YES -seedAccounts YES -startTab CHATS`, `-openScreen chat:ch-ecency`. Goldens: `node --experimental-strip-types --no-warnings native/tools/golden/generate-chats.mjs <ChatGoldens.kt> <ChatEmojiData.kt>` from the RN root (RN's own modules, synthetic inputs; it also writes the emoji map).

Tests: shared `ChatFormatTest` (goldens), `ChatSessionTest`, `ChatsListTest`, `ChatThreadTest`, `ChatSocketTest`; Android `ChatFlowsTest`; iOS `ChatTests`, `ChatUITests`.

## Platform features (wave 6a): share in, QR scanner, dApp browser and bridge, proposal vote

RN `screens/dappBrowser` (screen, bridges, `useHiveBridgeHandler`, `signConfirmSheet`, `domainBlacklist`), `components/qrModal`, `components/shareIntentSheet`, `ios/eshare`, the Android SEND intent filters, `components/proposalVoteRequest` with `providers/queries/proposalQueries`. Shared: `app/DappBrowserModel.kt` (tabs, URL bar, `BrowserPolicy`, the bridge), `DappBridgeOps.kt` (`BridgeOps`, `BridgeGuards`, `DappBlacklist`, `BridgeLabels`), `DappBridgeScript.kt` (the page side), `QrScan.kt`, `ShareIntake.kt`, `ProposalVote.kt`, `DemoDapp.kt`, `hive/GovernanceOperations.kt`. Android `ui/PlatformScreens.kt`, `ui/QrCamera.kt`, `ShareIntents.kt`; iOS `Screens/PlatformViews.swift`, `App/SharePlatform.swift`, the `EcencyShare` extension (`ShareExtension/`). Inventory row by row: platform-inventory.md with the wave notes.

- **Share into the app**: Android `ACTION_SEND` (text, an image) and `ACTION_SEND_MULTIPLE` (images); iOS share extension (images, one web URL, text). "Share to..." (Blog Post / Wave; closing it is Blog Post, as RN) shows only once the app is unlocked and the accounts were read; signed out the login opens and the share waits for that sign-in (RN opened the editor signed out). Blog Post: a new post, the text inserted as a snippet at the caret, the images uploaded through the editor's upload (prepared like picked images: re-encoded, metadata such as EXIF and GPS stripped). Wave: the composer with the text and the first image. A share belongs to the account that picked its target only; an account change, a logout or a wipe drops it; unclaimed it expires after 10 minutes. At most 5 images (the app's pick limit; RN takes up to 100) and 60,000 characters.
  - Android takes only `content://` images from another app's provider: `file://` URIs and the app's own providers are refused, so a share can never upload the app's private files (`ShareIntents`).
  - iOS: the extension (RN's bundle `app.esteem.mobile.ios.eshare`, shown as RN's "eshare" share entry) writes `Share/<uuid>/` (files plus `share.json`) into RN's App Group `group.com.ecency.eshare` and opens `app.esteem.mobile.ios.share://inbox/<uuid>` (a scheme of its own; RN's eshare used `ShareMedia://` with another format, so leftovers of RN shares in the group are never read). It reads nothing of the app: no Keychain group, no settings. The app reads a folder only for a UUID id, plain file names, files inside that folder (symlinks out are refused), deletes each file once prepared, and removes folders older than a day at launch and all of them on a wipe.
- **QR scanner** (RN QR_SCAN, `AppScreen.QrScan`): header and side menu. Android CameraX preview with zxing decoding (`QrDecoder`), iOS AVFoundation metadata output; the permission is asked on open and a refusal shows RN's "Unable to get Camera permission" with Close / Allow (system settings). One code per open. A code goes through the app's link handling (`QrScanModel`): `hive://`, `ecency://` and ecency.com /auth links to `AuthLinkModel` (which still confirms anything it would sign), ecency.com content links into the navigation, other web pages to the in-app browser, anything else "Unsupported URL!". The transfer screen's scan button reads the recipient (`TransferRecipientParser`). Declaring `CAMERA` on Android makes the system refuse the photo and video capture intents to an app that does not hold it, so "Capture a photo" and "Record Video" now ask for it first (`rememberCameraGate`, `MainActivity`'s video capture).
- **dApp browser** (`AppScreen.Explore` home, `AppScreen.DappBrowser(url)` a page; saved as the home: a URL is history and may carry a page's token): RN's tabs, header (home or close, back, forward, URL bar with the lock, clear, reload), progress, error with Retry, the directory on a home tab. Only http(s) loads in a page's main frame; `about:`, `data:` and `blob:` only in frames; `javascript:`, `file:`, `content:`, `intent:` and the rest are blocked; a `hive://` or `ecency://` link opens the app's link handling only when the user tapped it (`BrowserPolicy`). No file or content access, no mixed content, no popups (new windows load in the tab), camera, microphone and location refused. Explore has its own web profile (Android `ProfileStore` "explore" where supported, iOS a persistent `WKWebsiteDataStore(forIdentifier:)`), apart from HiveSigner's pages, the players and the signup challenge; it is cleared when no account is left and on a wipe. Android back goes back in the page's history first (RN closes the screen).
- **The bridge** (`window.hive_keychain` with Keeper's `window.hive.providers` entry, `window.peakvault`; RN's request APIs kept): the script runs in the top frame of https pages only and never on hivesigner.com; messages travel through an origin-bound channel (Android `WebViewCompat.addWebMessageListener` with `addDocumentStartJavaScript`, answering through the frame's reply proxy; iOS a `WKScriptMessageHandler` in the page world, answering with a JSON string literal to the script's receiver). Without those Android features (WebView before 83) the bridge is off, never a JavaScript interface. Rules (`DappBrowserModel`, all beyond RN unless noted):
  - A request counts only from the active tab's main frame, over https, from the origin the tab shows; the confirmation names the host the web view reports, never the page's `domain`/`sourceUrl`. Nothing is asked behind the app lock; one request at a time (others answer "busy"); a tab that navigated to another origin or closed drops its request unanswered; closing the browser rejects a waiting one.
  - RN's sheet (label, host, phishing warning from RN's list, Account, Authority, details; Confirm / Cancel, dismissing rejects). Text the page wrote (Peak Vault's `displayMessage` title, a Keychain custom request's `display_msg`) is never the sheet's title: it shows quoted under "Message from <host>" (RN used Peak Vault's title as the sheet title).
  - A `hive://` or `ecency://` link tapped in a page opens the app's sign prompt with "Requested by the page <host>" when the link names no requesting app.
  - Parity note: Peak Vault's `connect` answers the signed-in account name without a prompt, and an account mismatch error names it, as RN does (Keeper and Peak Vault behave the same); the page learns which account is signed in here. The operations shown are the ones that will be signed, built here (`BridgeOps`, pinned to RN's own builder by `PlatformGoldenVectors`), and their authority is what they need (`OperationAuthority.required`), not what the page claims. An operation must be `[name, {body}]` with the name as JSON text and a name `OperationAuthority` knows: hived also takes an operation's number (10 is account_update) or a `<name>_operation` spelling, which would show as something else, so both are refused before any prompt for every kind of account. The operations the SDK's authority map leaves out (limit_order_create2, witness_update, witness_set_properties, feed_publish, the escrow operations, account creation) are active, decline_voting_rights owner (refused), instead of the SDK's posting default.
  - Refused before any prompt: owner operations, account_update, account_update2 with key fields, recovery and account creation (`BridgeGuards`), more than 10 operations, operations a key account cannot serialize yet; RN's unsupported types (signTx, encode, decode, signedCall, addAccount, authority changes, createClaimedAccount, swap); Peak Vault sign-only, contact picker, encode/decode and memo broadcasts (RN). Amounts must be exact text or numbers with at most the asset's decimals (RN rounds `parseFloat(x).toFixed(3)`).
  - A confirmed broadcast is one action with its own id through `WalletSends` (auth upgrade, HiveSigner, HiveAuth as everywhere; a send that may have landed is followed up, never signed twice). signBuffer signs sha256 of the UTF-8 message with the posting or active key (lone surrogates refused, memo refused) after the confirmation and, when the app lock is on, the PIN (as the sign-in proof for another app); HiveSigner and HiveAuth accounts have no key to sign with (RN). The account must be the same when the request came and when it is confirmed.
- **Proposal vote** (RN ProposalVoteRequest): the card above the main feed's first tab, signed in, for the newest non-expired @ecency proposal (`database_api.list_proposals`) the account has not voted (`list_proposal_votes`), not dismissed "Forever" and not "Remind Later" within 3 days (device preferences per proposal and account, removed by a wipe). "Cast My Vote" signs `update_proposal_votes` (new serializer, with `account_witness_vote` and `account_witness_proxy`, golden vectors from dhive and the SDK) with the active authority as one action; "Thank You!", then "We are grateful!" for the rest of the run; failure "Fail!: <message>".

Golden vectors: `GOLDEN_MODULES=<the wallet scratch install> node native/tools/golden/generate-platform.mjs native/shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/ecency/core/hive/PlatformGoldenVectors.kt` (governance transactions, 28 bridge requests through RN's builder, signBuffer).

Deliberate differences from RN: everything marked "beyond RN" above; the wave composer takes one shared image; the share waits for the login; the transfer scanner is a full-screen cover (RN's sheet); witness votes and proxy links are now signable by key accounts.

Owner actions: none for ids or profiles (RN's App Group and eshare App ID and profile, CI secret `EXTEN_PROVISIONING_PROFILE`, named per target through `$(ECENCY_SHARE_PROFILE)`); App Store review note: the share extension only hands content to the app; Play: the camera permission is for QR scanning, photo and video capture.

Debug (fake data only): Android `--es openScreen explore|qr|dapp`, `--es demoShare <text>`; iOS `-openScreen explore|qr|dapp`, `-qrScan <value>` (the simulator has no camera), `-demoShare <text>`. `dapp` loads `DemoDapp` as if from https://dapp.example/, which asks for a vote on load.

Kotlin/Native note: in this file's iosX64 build (2.3.10) a leading `if (!isMainFrame) return null` in `BrowserPolicy.bridgeOrigin` behaved as if it were not there (frames passed; the iosX64 run of `DappBrowserModelTest` caught it). This was observed for that file's compile only, not as a general rule (exact copies elsewhere behave). The check is now the function's last expression with its own assertion, and both apps also drop frame messages before the model sees them (Android `DappWeb.mainFrameMessage`, iOS `DappWebViews.mainFrameBody`). Android WebView 113 refuses the origin rule `https://*`: the listener and the script use `*`, and the native origin check decides.

Tests: shared `PlatformGoldenVectorTest`, `DappBrowserModelTest`, `PlatformModelsTest`; Android `PlatformFlowsTest` (and the updated Explore case in `CommunityFlowsTest`); iOS `PlatformTests`, `PlatformUITests`.

## Parity gaps (gaps2)

RN parity gaps over the existing screens, both apps. Shared: `app/Recap.kt`, `BalanceAnalytics.kt`, `ScrollTop.kt`, `DigestEntries.kt`, `PayoutDetails.kt`, `PostStats.kt`, `LinkSheet.kt`, `CommentThreads.kt`, `ProfileWallet.kt`, `ImageReveals` and `PostBadges` (in `FeedFilters.kt`, `PostCard.kt`), copy in `UiCopy` and the `*Copy` objects next to them (RN en-US keys in comments). UI: Android `ui/GapsUi.kt`, `ui/WalletChartsUi.kt`, `ImageSaver.kt`; iOS `Screens/Gaps2Views.swift`, `App/PhotoSaver.swift`.

- AI recap in the reading bar (RN postReadingMetadata): Words | Reading | AI "Recap" for a signed-in reader of 50 words or more; `ai-assist` with `summarize` and the speech text (10000 characters), paid as the editor's assist (one stored key per run, scoped to the post, "Check again" for a run without an answer, nothing sent without a price and a balance that covers it). The summary box translates through translate.ecency.com with a language picker. Difference: the cost shows under "Recap" (RN shows none), and a short balance is refused before sending (RN learns it from the 402).
- Balance analytics (RN balanceAnalyticsSheet; the chart-line button on HIVE, HBD and HP details): `balance-api` `balance-history` (200 a page, newest page first, older pages when the chart is panned to its left edge) and `aggregated-history` (yearly, monthly, daily). Android draws the chart on a Canvas with RN's pinned y labels; iOS uses Swift Charts. RN's CoinGecko price chart is dead code in RN and not built.
- "Show Images" off holds back feed card images behind RN's "Tap to load image" placeholder, one image per tap for the session (`ImageReveals`); the NSFW placeholder is never gated.
- Scroll-to-top popup (`ScrollTopRule`): post lists (feed, tag, community, profile) show the scroll to top (drawn as the FAB's twin, see Feed "Owner notes 10-29") when scrolling up more than 5000 dp and hide it on tap (blocked for a second); iOS Waves also hides it on scroll down; Android Waves keeps its own threshold (15 rows down while scrolling back up).
- Welcome mascot: RN's `love_mascot.png` (Android drawable-nodpi from @3x, iOS asset catalog 1x/2x/3x), right aligned, 45% wide, 227 high, 0.7 opacity.
- Feed tab: RN's waves "Getting started" card above the feed for signed-in readers (the shared `WavesOnboardingModel`); "Post your first Wave" opens the wave composer there.
- Digest entry points: another profile's email button and the community "Newsletter" chip open the digest sheet (creator / community); the own profile shows the sender row (`GET /api/newsletter/sender`: weekly and monthly subscribers, copy subscribe link, Manage); the end of a post offers RN's subscribe card (RN pickPostDigestTarget; shown only after a successful subscriptions lookup without that list; the dismissal is per viewer and list, `digest.postPrompt.*`, forgotten on wipe).
- Payout popover (RN payoutDetailsContent): tapping a payout on cards, the post bar, comments and waves; Promoted, Potential, Author, Curation, Max Accepted, Breakdown (median feed and `hbd_print_rate`), Beneficiaries, Payout (moment `fromNow`), the $0.02 warning. Values in dollars as the cards (RN converts to the chosen currency).
- Views and stats (RN postDisplayView, postStatsModal): `POST /api/stats` (a Plausible read; nothing is recorded) for the path without its category over the post's lifetime; visits as the count, Visitors / Views / Minutes, the device breakdown, PROMOTE POST to `Redeem("promote", "author/permlink")`. Difference: RN shows `visit_duration / 1000` under "Minutes" (seconds read as milliseconds); here seconds become minutes.
- Badges: RN's Ecency source badge (the Ecency logo mark, 12 dp/pt, soft gray `#d1d5db` light / `#374151` dark as RN and web, labelled "Published with Ecency", no visible text) and RN's AI disclosure (`json_metadata.ai_tools.media_generation || writing_edit`) after the date on cards and post headers (RN's startsWith rule), and the post footer "Posted by {author} via {App} {age}" with both badges (RN's looser footer rule).
- Image viewer: pinch (1x to 5x) and double tap (1.5x) zoom, "Preview (i/n)", share, copy link, save to the "Ecency" album (Android MediaStore Pictures/Ecency, WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE only up to Android 9; iOS Photos with library access for the album, add-only access saves without it; permission strings in `project.yml`). Difference: RN's "copy" button shares; here share and copy are both offered.
- Link sheet: external links in post, comment and wave bodies ask first (RN "Link": Copy, Open in Explore, Open in System browser, Cancel). "Open in Explore" (with RN's warning, its dash written as a full stop) shows only once the dApp browser opens URLs (`LinkSheet.exploreScreen`), hidden in this build.
- Reply threads: replies under a top-level comment start collapsed (RN), the chevron pill opens them; a thread holding a reply sent from this device opens by itself; a comment whose replies are not loaded opens its own thread.
- Another account's Wallet tab (RN walletView without dropdowns): unclaimed rewards as text and Wallet Details (HIVE, staked HIVE with delegated / received / net, HBD, savings HIVE and HBD, next power down), read only. Difference: the tab keeps its "Wallet" label (RN shows the estimated value there).
- Tests: shared `RecapModelTest`, `BalanceAnalyticsTest`, `DigestEntriesTest`, `PostStatsTest`, `Gaps2Test` (image reveals, scroll rule, payout rows, badges, link sheet, comment threads, profile wallet); Android `Gaps2FlowsTest` plus cases in `WalletFlowsTest`, `GapsFlowsTest`, `ProfileFlowsTest`, `LoginFlowsTest`; iOS `Gaps2Tests`, `Gaps2UITests`.
## RN parity gaps (gaps3)

- **Scaling tests** (`LinearScaling`): every "stays linear" check bills the measuring thread's CPU time (JVM `ThreadMXBean`, Kotlin/Native `CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID`), keeps the fastest run of each size across up to five attempts, and only uses wall time as a hang guard. A quadratic still fails (an ancestor walk in `Dom.insertNode`: exponent 2.37 to 2.47).
- **Out of RC** (`RcOfferModel`, `env.rcOffer`): any broadcast refused for lack of resource credits (vote, reblog, cross post, follow, mute, comment, wave, post) raises one app-wide offer instead of its toast or alert, as RN `ui.rcOffer`: "Top up RC with Points" (REDEEM `rc_topup`), "Boost account", Cancel. Matched with RN's `RC_SHORTFALL` over the whole error chain. Android draws it in `ShellPrompts`, iOS in the `AuthOverlay` window (above every sheet, below the lock).
- **Feature spotlight** (`SpotlightModel`): `/private-api/spotlights` (cached an hour), mobile and audience filter, per-account dismissal (`spotlight.dismissed.<id>_<account>`, wiped with the account marks), highest weight then earliest start, texts in the app language. Heads the first home feed tab. The button dismisses and follows the announcement's safe link rules (https or an ecency.com path).
- **Walkthrough dot** (`WalkthroughModel`): RN's red dot on the feed filter bar's "+" while signed in, until first used; device-wide, imported from RN `walkthrough.walkthroughMap`.
- **Body links**: `hive://` and `ecency://` links in a post go to `AuthLinkModel.handle` (`Route.AppLink`), so a sign link asks first, as from another app.
- **`hiveuri` pushes**: the payload's `hiveUri` (hive or ecency scheme only) goes to the same link model; never for a push addressed to another signed-in account.
- **App links**: bare `/trending`, `/hot` (the global ranked feed), `/waves`, `/waves/*` (Android manifest; routing in `ContentLinks`).
- **Profile edit images**: gallery, camera and (Android) files; the avatar is the centred square at 512 px (`AvatarCrop`), uploaded through the editor's upload and saved at once without closing the screen (RN `goBack: false`). The image link entry stays.
- **Wave composer**: dictation and AI assist over the composer (`AiTextTarget`; a wave offers improve, grammar and summary), and the "Image in clipboard · Paste image" chip.
- **Reply editor account tile**: opens the accounts sheet; the reply opens again for the account switched to (`ReplyAccountHandoff`), starting from the text that was there unless that account has its own draft of it.
- **Uploads gallery "+"**: images join the gallery without going into the body.

Deliberate differences: no interactive avatar crop (centre square; RN shows a crop UI); a dictated wave transcript goes at the end of the text (the composer does not report its caret); the reply tile reopens the editor for the new account instead of keeping one editor across accounts; the spotlight button follows only https or ecency.com links.

## Polish (owner list)

Deliberate improvements over RN, both apps unless noted.

- **Toasts** (`UiMessages`, iOS `MessagesOverlay`, Android `ToastView`): every toast is centred at the bottom, one line or several (RN `toastNotification`: 100 pt up, 22 pt corners, at least 1/1.9 of the width). The iOS host sat at the left edge: `GeometryReader` places its content top leading and the toast only filled the height, so the stack was as narrow as the toast unless a banner widened it. Every toast goes through the shared channel; Android's `AppHost.toast` and "No browser available" no longer use a system `Toast` (only the debug renderer demo, which runs without the app, still does). Toasts live in a window of their own, so a toast posted while a sheet is open shows on top: iOS `ToastOverlay`, a passthrough window at `.alert + 0.5` (above sheets and signing prompts, below the lock window) that takes only touches on the pill; Android `ToastWindow`, a non-focusable, non-touchable app window added per toast through the activity's window manager (it stacks above sheets and dialogs opened before it), lifted by the keyboard height the app window reports when the toast appears, composed only where the app content is (never over the lock). Android toasts are not tappable, as in RN (`applicationScreen.tsx` gives the toast no `onPress`); iOS keeps tap to dismiss (a deliberate difference). Android announces each toast to TalkBack from the app's view (TalkBack does not reach a window that takes no touches), and falls back to drawing the toast in the content if the system refuses the window. The iOS toast window binds to the first scene once (the app has one scene). Debug: iOS `-demoToast <text>`, Android `--es demoToast <text>`.
- **Composer tags** (`EditorTags.input`, `EditorModel.setTagInput`): a typed tag leaves the field with the keystroke that made it a chip. iOS root cause: a SwiftUI `TextField` bound to `tagInput` never showed the model clearing the text it had just set inside the field's own setter, so "a " stayed and the next key sent "a b", parsing "a" again ("Tag exists already"). The field is now a `UITextField`: UIKit applies every edit (composed characters and emoji included), `editingChanged` hands the result to the model, and the model's text replaces it with the caret kept the same distance from the end. Shared guard for any field that lags a frame (Compose can too): the text that already became chips (`TagInputResult.consumed`) is dropped from the next value instead of being parsed again; it is forgotten once the field shows the model's text (`tagFieldShown`: Android after each composition, iOS after setting the field), and on a removed tag or a cleared editor, so a duplicate typed or pasted into the cleared field is always RN's toast. A real duplicate is one RN toast, nothing added, the field cleared; more than 10 tags keep RN's warning. iOS also adds a tag on losing focus (RN `onEndEditing`, as Android already did).
- **Comment action row**: no "Reply" text button on a comment; its comments icon with the reply count is the reply button (Android `ActionsRow(commentsReply = true)`, a button labelled "Reply" with the count as its state; iOS `reply-comment`, "Reply to author" with the count as its value), so the replies caret stays on the line (a 360 dp phone at default text). At the largest text sizes the row wraps (Android `FlowRow`, iOS a third `ViewThatFits` step that keeps count and caret together); the post's bottom bar and the comments header keep their "Reply" buttons. Android's comment sort tabs wrap too (the last one was cut at font scale 2 on a 320 dp phone).
- **Reputation**: no badge on avatars (cards, post header, comments) and no "(rep)" next to names (profile, home header, search rows, waves). The quick profile sheet keeps it.
- **PIN with biometrics** (`AppLockModel.startEnable(biometricAvailable)`): with a strong biometric enrolled (iOS `LAContext` biometrics, Android `BIOMETRIC_STRONG`), turning the lock on offers biometrics first (`biometricOffer`, "Use PIN instead" leads to the plain setup). After the platform prompt succeeds the user sets a backing PIN ("Set a PIN to back up biometric unlock"); once it is confirmed, biometric unlock is bound to the biometrics enrolled then through the existing `BiometricBinder` (Android Keystore key invalidated by enrollment, iOS domain state), so the lock screen starts with biometrics. Nothing is bound before the PIN; the three-wrong-PINs wipe is unchanged; nothing enrolled at bind time leaves the PIN lock on with RN's "could not be turned on" notice. No biometrics: straight to the PIN setup. The keys screen's "Set PIN" goes through the same offer.
