A binder that throws after the PIN is set leaves the lock on with the notice; a pad that replaces a pending PIN confirmation answers its caller false. Debug: iOS `-demoBiometrics YES`, Android `--ez demoBiometrics true` (fake data / demo auth only).
- **Side menu**: a 16 pt gap replaces RN's divider. The footer group (QR, Refer, Docs, Settings, Logout, version) is pinned to the bottom; the main group starts at the top and scrolls on its own. `SideMenu.pinsFooter` keeps one row (56 pt) for the main group; below that (a landscape phone, the largest text on a small phone) the whole menu scrolls as one so nothing is cut off. The footer is measured in the same layout pass that makes this choice (a custom `Layout` on both platforms), so it never flips after the first frame.
- **Waves quick submit**: the text fills the sheet down to the short-content hint and the toolbar, and the toolbar sits on the keyboard (or on the sheet's bottom edge without one, iOS hardware keyboard included). iOS: the editor had a 180 pt cap and a `Spacer` under the toolbar; the sheet background now covers the keyboard area and PUBLISH stays on one line. Android: the sheet takes the full height and the text field takes the rest (`weight`).

- **Image source chooser** (`MediaSourceMenus`, iOS `MediaSourceViews.swift`, Android `MediaSourceChooser.kt`): where an image comes from is a compact sheet sized to its content instead of a plain dialog: one row per route with an icon tile, the surface's own title and a short line under it (`MediaSource.subtitle`), then Cancel; a pick closes the sheet, then opens the picker (iOS waits for the sheet to go). The routes per surface are unchanged: profile avatar and cover (gallery, camera where there is one, files, image link), chat attachment and video thumbnail (library, files). The editor's uploads sheet shows the same options as tiles (gallery, files, camera, paste when the clipboard has an image); the waves composer keeps its toolbar buttons. Accessibility text sizes (Android from 1.5x) drop the icons and use the rows; VoiceOver reads the title with the line as a hint (TalkBack: the tile's click label). The library stays the system photo picker (PHPicker, no library permission; no recent photos strip, which would need one).
- **Spin wheel** (iOS `SpinStage`): the wheel sits on the screen's centre line. iOS cause: the screen's column sat in a `GeometryReader`, which places content top leading, and the column was only as wide as its widest part, so while the rings turned (no tags) the wheel sat at the left. The rings shrink to their part where 230 pt do not fit (short phones, large text; Android scaled them as ovals). Portrait only, as the app.
- **Side menu header**: the picture is cut to the drawer. iOS cause: `clipped()` on a `scaledToFill` image clips to the image's own frame, so the 2.3 : 1 picture ran past the drawer's edge over the scrim (`DrawerHeaderBackground` clips a view with the header's frame). Android: the header grows with the account lines at large fonts (it was a fixed 164 dp), and the drawer widens with the font scale as on iOS (at most 85% of the screen, 92% from 1.5x, where the rows drop their icons).

Tests: shared `EditorTagsTest`, cases in `EditorModelTest`, `AppLockModelTest`, `ShellTest`; Android `ShellFlowsTest` (toast centring, pinned footer on a 320 x 640 dp screen, one scroll on a landscape screen), `EditorFlowsTest`, `LockFlowsTest`, `ProfileFlowsTest`, `WavesFlowsTest`; iOS `ShellUITests` (toast frame midX within 3 pt of the screen's, side menu at XXXL text), `EditorUITests`, `LockUITests`, `ProfileUITests`, `NotificationUITests`, `PolishUITests` (wave composer layout; screenshots with `TEST_RUNNER_POLISH_SHOT_DIR`); chooser, spin and side menu: shared `MediaPickingTest`, Android `MediaSourceChooserTest`, `PerksFlowsTest` (wheel centre and roundness, 320 x 480 dp at 2x), `ShellFlowsTest` (header inside the drawer, Arabic at 2x), iOS `LayoutPolishTests` (drawn with ImageRenderer: wheel centre at four phone sizes and three text sizes, rings inside their part, header no wider than the drawer in both directions), `LayoutPolishUITests` (screenshots with `TEST_RUNNER_POLISH3_SHOT_DIR`).

## Esie (the mascot)

Esie is drawn natively from vectors on both platforms (owner decision 10-22: native vectors, no Rive, no WebView, no
animation library).

- Art: `tools/esie/art/esie.svg` is the approved master (named parts in z-order, generated by `build.py` from the
  traced `paths.json`), `motion.css` the approved motion, `reference/*.png` the approved renders.
- Shared data: `tools/esie/esie_to_kotlin.py` converts the master into `shared/.../esie/EsieArtData.kt` (one layer per
  shape: absolute M/L/C/Q/Z path, chain of named parts, solid or gradient fill with its bounding box matrix, stroke,
  clips, opacity; a translucent group of like shapes, the head shade, is one layer so its opacity applies once; the
  master's soft blur is left out, about 1 dp at these sizes and costly). After an art change:
  `python3 native/tools/esie/esie_to_kotlin.py native/tools/esie/art/esie.svg native/shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/ecency/core/esie/EsieArtData.kt`
- Shared rig: `EsieRig.fill(spec, elapsedMs, buffer)` writes, without allocating, each layer's opacity and the matrices
  before and after its clip into the view's `EsieFrameBuffer` (iOS copies it out in one call); `EsieRig.frame` is the
  readable version for tests. `EsieMotion` holds the durations and keyframes; `EsieRig.viewport(mood)` the framing,
  sampled from the rig over every cycle so the hop, jolt and wave never clip, and baked into `EsieViewports`
  (`EsieViewportsTest` resamples and prints new values after an art or motion change). Moods: rest, thumbs up, wave, happy
  (waving, closed eyes, one hop), sad (brows, lids, tear, sigh), thinking and surprised (for Esie AI), plus a talking
  mouth.
- Cost: at most 30 frames a second (`EsieRig.FRAME_MS`), and after 30 s with the same spec (`SETTLE_MS`) each cycle
  runs on to its next start and holds there (no jump; the tear has run off, the thought dots are even), then from
  `SETTLED_MS` she only blinks: `nextFrameDelayMs` sleeps until the next blink and gives a short burst of frames through it.
  Any spec change starts over. Reduce motion is the shared motion level (Android `LocalMotion`, iOS `\.motion`, so the
  `-reduceMotion YES` debug flag reaches her): a still pose with no frames at all; a talking mouth still swaps on its
  140 ms beat.
- Views: Android `EsieView` (Compose Canvas on the framework canvas; frames only while composed and resumed), iOS
  `EsieView` (SwiftUI Canvas in a TimelineView driven by the rig's schedule, paused off screen and in the background,
  keeping its time; a new spec resets it). Decorative (hidden from TalkBack and VoiceOver) unless given a label; test tag and
  accessibility identifier `esie`, mood in the `EsieMoodKey` semantics (Android) or the accessibility value (iOS,
  exposed with `-fakeData` for the UI tests).
- Placements (`EsiePlacement`, same mood and size on both): empty lists rest (feed, profile posts and waves,
  bookmarks, notifications, followers, communities, search, drafts and schedules, chats, voters, reblogs), failed
  loads and the error screen are sad (`ErrorBlock`/`EsieMessageBlock` on Android, `ErrorStateView` on iOS; offline
  failures arrive as network errors there), success moments are happy (a won spin, a finished quest tier, and on
  Android the "Post published" dialog; the iOS one is a system alert; on the spin screen she is 64 so the pack and
  the countdown stay on short phones), welcome (at most 40 % of the width) and login wave three times, then rest.
  The account boost screen shows her thumbs up (`PROMO`, 160). The welcome screen's old `love_mascot` image and the boost screen's old coin mascot (`boost_logo`, RN `esteemcoin_boost.png`) are gone; no old mascot picture is left in either app. Inline footers ("No more posts", load-more errors) and
  sheets stay without Esie (iOS `ErrorStateView(esie: nil)` for such rows).
- Debug gallery of all moods: Android `--ez esieGallery true`, iOS `-esieGallery YES`.
- Tests: `EsieTest` (shared: settle rule, frame buffer, viewport over whole cycles), `EsieFlowsTest` (reduce motion,
  narrow welcome), `PerksFlowsTest` (short phone spin), `LoginFlowsTest` (Android), `EsieTests` (motion level,
  schedule), `EsieUITests` (`-reduceMotion YES`) (iOS).

## Motion

One motion system, its numbers in the shared core (`shared/.../app/Motion.kt`: `MotionTokens`, `MotionPolicy`), mapped once per platform: Android `ui/Motion.kt` and `ui/Skeleton.kt` (Compose specs, `LocalMotion`), iOS `App/Motion.swift` (SwiftUI animations, `\.motion`). Screens never hold a literal duration or curve.

Tokens: `PRESS_MS` 90, `SHORT_MS` 150 (icon swaps, colours, counters), `MEDIUM_MS` 250 (enabled states, content replacing a skeleton), `PAGE_MS` 300 (screens), `PAGE_FADE_MS` 150 (the reduce motion crossfade), `SHIMMER_MS` 1200, `SHAKE_MS` 400 (`SHAKE_DP` 12, 3 cycles), `DONE_HOLD_MS` 900; curves Material 3 `STANDARD`, `EMPHASIZED_DECELERATE`, `EMPHASIZED_ACCELERATE`; springs `POP` (damping 0.45, stiffness 500) and `SETTLE` (1.0, 700), iOS uses `responseSeconds` (2 pi / sqrt(k)); `POP_SCALE` 1.25, `PRESS_SCALE` 0.98, `PRESS_DIM` 0.06, `SHARED_AXIS_DP` 30, `FADE_THROUGH_SCALE` 0.92.

Reduce motion (`MotionPolicy.level`): iOS `accessibilityReduceMotion`; Android has no switch of its own, so "Remove animations" (animator duration scale 0, followed live) turns motion off. `FULL` moves, pops and shimmers; `REDUCED` keeps fades only (page moves become crossfades, no pops, shakes, bounces, shimmer or press growth); `NONE` lands every change at once. Debug launch arguments on iOS: `-reduceMotion YES`, `-disableAnimations YES`.

Where it is used:
- Screens: Android pushes and pops with Material shared axis X and switches tabs with fade through (`AnimatedNavHost`, `tabTransform`); back that pops a screen is predictive (`PredictiveBackHandler` seeks the pop, cancelling springs back; `enableOnBackInvokedCallback` in the manifest), other backs (menu, Feed tab) keep the plain handler. A popped screen's models close when its exit transition ends (`Navigator.settle`). iOS keeps NavigationStack's push and edge swipe back, now also on screens with their own header (`SwipeBackEnabler`: chat thread, dApp browser, QR scanner; not where back is hidden on purpose). On iOS 18 a feed card's image zooms into its post (`heroSource` / `heroDestination`, `.navigationTransition(.zoom)`); iOS 17 and reduce motion keep the system push.
- Vote: spinner while in flight, then the icon pops back in filled (Android `AnimatedSwap` and `popOnChange`; iOS one SF Symbol with `.symbolEffect(.replace)` and a `.bounce` when the vote lands), the vote, comment and reblog counts roll (Android `AnimatedCounter`, iOS `.numericText`). Haptic on vote.
- Toggles: follow labels pop (profile, quick profile), favourite pops and recolours, bookmark and reblog from the post menu tap a light haptic; copy link plays the success haptic.
- Tabs and filters: the selected tab icon fades its colour and pops (Android) or bounces (iOS); feed filter pills fade their fill.
- Create button: sinks while pressed and springs back (Android `pressScale`, iOS `PressScaleStyle`), ripple kept.
- Sending: reply, wave PUBLISH and chat send turn into their own progress; chat shows a tick for `DONE_HOLD_MS` while the next message can be typed; success haptic (Android `rememberSendPhase` / `SendContent`, iOS `SendContent`).
- PIN pad: dots fill with a fade and a pop; a refused PIN shakes them with the error haptic (`MotionPolicy.pinRejected`).
- Pull to refresh: the platform indicators; Android adds the light haptic (UIRefreshControl has its own).
- Skeletons (one shimmer per platform, `Skeleton` and `Bone`): feed cards, the post body, the profile header, wallet rows, notifications and the chat list's first load. Spinners stay for unknown sizes and blocking work (load more, searches, sheets). The shimmer runs only at full motion and while its container is on screen (lazy rows that leave are disposed; iOS `TimelineView` pauses on disappear; Android checks its window bounds); Android feed cards fade in as they replace it (opacity only, no placement animation).
- Tap feedback: Android keeps Material ripples from the touch point; icon-only controls (header icons, back arrow, card options) use an unbounded ripple (`iconClickable`). iOS cards use `PressableStyle`: a soft tint grows from the touch point (`TouchPoint`, a window recognizer that only observes) and the card sinks slightly; reduce motion keeps a plain dim.

Haptics are light and few (`HapticEvent`: vote, toggles, success, wrong PIN, refresh), never on every tap, and follow the system's own setting, not the motion level.

Deliberate choices: Android has no shared element from card image to post: the post screen has no hero image (the body starts with text or its first image at a different size), so shared axis is the honest transition; iOS's zoom does not need a matching element, so it is used there. iOS avatars open the quick profile sheet (RN behaviour), so there is no avatar to profile zoom on cards. The header account pill keeps its click order (clipping it first broke its tap in tests), so its ripple is rectangular.

Tests: shared `MotionTest` (levels, policy, tokens, spring response, shake keyframes, PIN rule, haptics); Android `MotionFlowsTest` (haptic constants per SDK, popped models held until the transition settles, push and pop at every motion level, predictive back seek, cancel and commit, vote state landing at every level, shimmer only at full motion and on screen, send phases with the success haptic, system duration scale); iOS `MotionTests` (levels and flags, fades and pops per level, springs, send phases, shake, swipe back rule, vote symbols, shimmer phase, menu toggles, haptics) and `MotionUITests` (swipe back from the chat thread, open a post and swipe back at full and reduced motion).

## Launch screen

RN uses react-native-bootsplash: its logo on the page colour (white, dark `#1E2835`), faded out when the app is ready (`RNBootSplash.hide({ fade: true })`). Native does the same with no animation library:
- Android: androidx core-splashscreen (1.0.1, Kotlin 1.x). `MainActivity` starts on `Theme.Ecency.Starting` (`res/values/themes.xml`): `windowSplashScreenBackground` `@color/splash_background` (`values-night` for dark), `windowSplashScreenAnimatedIcon` `@drawable/splash_logo` (the Ecency mark, RN EcencySourceBadge's path, 100 dp in a 288 dp icon as RN's bootsplash logo, brand blue `#004BD8` in both modes as RN), then `Theme.Material.NoActionBar`. `installSplashScreen()` runs before `super.onCreate`; the exit listener fades it out (`LaunchSplash.fadeOut`).
- iOS: `UILaunchScreen` in `project.yml` (`LaunchBackground` colour set, light and dark; `LaunchLogo`, RN's BootSplashLogo PNGs, 100 pt). `LaunchSplashCover` draws the same over the first frame (in the system appearance, as the launch screen was) and fades it out; it takes no touches and VoiceOver skips it.
- The fade is `MotionTokens.SPLASH_FADE_MS` (220 ms, RN bootsplash's), kept under reduce motion (a fade), none when motion is off.
- Tests: shared `MotionTest`; Android `LaunchSplashTest` (theme, dark colour, fade, animations off); iOS `LaunchSplashTests` (Info.plist names existing assets, both colours, fade per motion level).

## Accessibility

### Android

TalkBack:
- Every `clickable` names its role (`Role.Button`, `Tab`, `Switch`, `DropdownList`, `RadioButton`); icon-only buttons carry a localized label (the `Glyph` description or the container's `contentDescription`). Tabs (bottom bar, feed pills, profile, community, waves, wallet charts, comment sort, quest tiers, browser tabs) are `Role.Tab` with `selected`.
- List rows are one focus stop (`ui/A11y.kt`): a feed card reads a summary (reblogger, title, author, community, age, badges, summary, votes, comments, payout), a double tap opens the post, and its taps (upvote, comments, author, community, voters, reblogs, tip, payout details, options, reveal) are custom actions. Comment and wave headers do the same for their row while the body after them keeps its links and images; notification and chat rows offer the avatar and options as actions. The small taps inside a merged row stay for touch and hide from TalkBack (`LocalMergedRow`, `hideFromAccessibility`); badges inside a merged row drop their own description (the summary says it once). The action lists are remembered per row, state and language; summaries are built only when TalkBack asks. A chat bubble's double tap opens its options (a plain tap does nothing, as RN).
- Screen titles (`TopBar`), sheet and dialog titles, settings cards and wallet sections are headings. Toasts, banners, `MessageBlock` / `ErrorBlock` and inline form errors are polite live regions; the PIN pad announces each digit.
- Text fields read their placeholder as the hint (placeholder inside the `decorationBox`). Settings dropdowns are one stop: "Language, English, drop down list", expand and collapse actions, the chosen item `selected`.
- Touch targets: Compose widens the hit area of a tap target smaller than 48 dp (`ViewConfiguration.minimumTouchTargetSize`) without changing layout, where no neighbour or clip intervenes. So tiny glyph buttons get padding inside their click area (sideways only in single-line rows such as the chat editing banner, the browser tab chip and a split route, so the row keeps its height; on all sides where the row is already taller, about 30 dp of their own; the pinned-message unpin X keeps all-side padding: its row is the parent's tap and the vertical padding keeps the touch area off the text) and a gap to the next target or text (icon clusters 12 dp apart, a row's trailing X 12 dp from its amount, which a test checks), and `minimumInteractiveComponentSize()` is not used because it grows rows (a search bar jumping when its clear button appears). Search bars sit in a stable 48 dp host.

Font size and display size:
- Text is `sp`; containers holding text use `heightIn(min = ...)`, never a fixed height (top bar, settings dropdown, chat search). Long labels wrap (settings values, search placeholders, the top bar title up to two lines with an ellipsis) or ellipsize on purpose. The editor header's Save and Preview switch to their icons when the label does not fit and back when room returns (rotation, split screen, a smaller font): `LabelOrIcon` measures the label against the width it gets on every layout.
- Checked at 360 dp wide and font scale 2.0 (Android 14 nonlinear scaling) in Robolectric with real font metrics; on the emulator with `font_scale` 1.3 and 2.0 and a larger display size (`wm density`).

Motion: `LocalMotion` follows "Remove animations" (README "Motion"); the side drawer crossfades or appears at once instead of sliding, the spin game's rings stand still.

Contrast (WCAG AA): RN's tokens fall short on white (`#788187` 3.97:1, `#357CE6` 4.06:1, `#E63535` 4.26:1, icon `#C1C5C7` 1.74:1). Light now uses `darkGray` / `darkText` `#67717A`, `blue` `#2E6BD4`, `red` `#D32F2F`, `icon` `#868F95`; dark keeps fills that white labels read on (`blue` `#2E6BD4`, `red` `#D32F2F`) and adds `blueText` `#7FB0F7` and `redText` `#FF8F8F` for text, links and small icons (dark Material `primary` is `blueText`), `icon` `#8C959B`. Post body links and danger text follow. Every `buttonColors(...)` names its `contentColor` (white on the blue and red fills); the dark scheme's own `onPrimary` is navy on its light blue `primary`.

Checks: build-logic `A11yLintTest` (every clickable, toggleable and selectable names a role; an icon-only or avatar-only tappable container or a clickable icon has a label; any `blue` / `red` fill token used outside a fill (a background, gradient, button container, track or the light scheme's primary; an `animateColorAsState` line ending in `// fill` marks an animated fill; borders are not fills, a 1 dp outline needs 3:1) fails, so text, icons, wrappers taking a colour, `when` branches and locals use `blueText` / `redText`; every `buttonColors` names its content color), `TextDirectionLintTest` (also `TextStyle.Default` and `BasicTextField` without a `textStyle`). Android `A11yFlowsTest` (native graphics, 360 dp): no action TalkBack reaches is unlabelled or roleless on the feed, post, settings, login, waves, notifications, wallet and chats; the merged card and its custom actions; tab roles; headings; the dropdown; `LabelOrIcon` switching back; the search bar keeping its height when the clear button shows; comment headers not inflated; no cut text at font scale 2.0 (a line wider or taller than its box, unless the last line is a deliberate ellipsis). The scans (`A11yScan.kt`) also run with real rows in `NotificationFlowsTest`, `ChatFlowsTest` (list and thread), `WavesFlowsTest` and `WalletFlowsTest` at font scale 2.0 and 360 dp. `ContrastTest` holds the tokens to 4.5:1 (text) and 3:1 (icons) in both themes.

### iOS

Screen reading (VoiceOver, Switch Control), Dynamic Type up to AX5, Display Zoom and small phones, Reduce Motion, Bold Text, Increase Contrast and Smart Invert, on every screen.

- Helpers (`App/Accessibility.swift`): `AdaptiveStack` (an HStack that stacks at accessibility sizes, `DynamicTypeSize.stacksRows`), `.largeContent(title, appIcon:)` (the large content viewer for controls that keep their size: tab bar, header and toolbar icons, the editor's bottom toolbar), `.tapTarget()` (44 by 44 points), `.keepsColorsInverted()` (Smart Invert; every `LoadedImageView` and avatar does it), `.onEscape` (VoiceOver's two finger Z on custom overlays: drawer, banners, toast, signing overlay, lock prompts, scanner; system sheets have it already), `.announces(text, attempt:, debounce:, sticky:)` (`attempt: ObjectAttempt(error)` holds the failure, so its identity is never reused; sticky entries are kept per site and failure, so several rows at one site each speak once, and lapse after two minutes unseen) and `Announce.post` (errors and statuses shown in place: each time the view appears with the text and not again while it stays; a new failure with the same text announces when its identity changes; `sticky` for lazy list rows, once per call site until the text goes away; validation that follows typing waits until it stays for a second; toasts and banners announce themselves), `\.assistiveTechnology` (VoiceOver or Switch Control on).
- VoiceOver: icon-only controls are labelled by their action, screen and section titles are headers, selected tabs, pills, segments and chips say so, toggles read as switches with their value. A feed card, wave, comment, notification, chat message, search result and list row is one stop with a summary, and its secondary actions (comments, voters, reblogs, tip, author, community, options, reply, react, copy, remove, each link inside a chat message, the leaderboard's earn tip) are rotor actions (`accessibilityAction(named:)`); the separate buttons and their identifiers stay for touch and UI tests. Decorative images are hidden.
- The Feed tab stays mounted under the other tabs so its lists keep their place, but with VoiceOver or Switch Control on it is unmounted: a hidden SwiftUI tree still exposed its explicit containers, scroll views and images. Debug `-assistiveTechnology YES` forces that state (the accessibility UI tests use it). Known cost: those users find the feed at its top again after visiting another tab (the scroll position is not restored).
- Dynamic Type: text uses text styles or `.scaledFont` (RN's point sizes scaled relative to a text style); readable text never uses a literal `.font(.system(size:))` (lint below). Rows stack at accessibility sizes, segment rows keep equal widths while labels fit and scroll sideways otherwise (`FittingSegments`), chips wrap (`ChipFlow`, a chip wider than the row wraps its text), sheets scroll and fixed detents grow (vote weight, share chooser), the drawer widens with the text, the editor's tag hint is a wrapping Text (a UITextField placeholder is one line). Controls that cannot grow (tab bar, toolbars, badges) cap their size and show the large content viewer.
- Motion: programmatic scrolls (`motion.scroll`), the drawer, gallery zoom and the spin wheel follow Reduce Motion (README "Motion").
- Contrast: every text token meets WCAG AA (4.5:1) in light and dark. The changed colors are in one file, `App/ContrastPalette.swift`, with RN's originals, the same values as Android (approved by the owner), plus two iOS only colors that follow the same AA rule: RC text 0x0B7D59 (also `successText` in light, for gains and done states) and the unread notification row 0xF3F7FD: light gray 0x67717A, blue 0x2E6BD4, red 0xD32F2F, icon 0x868F95; dark blue and red fills 0x2E6BD4 and 0xD32F2F under white text, and `accentText` 0x7FB0F7 and `dangerText` 0xFF8F8F wherever blue or red draws text on the dark background. The root tint (`AppTheme.rootTint`) is the text blue, since it colors text buttons, links and navigation items; filled buttons use `.buttonStyle(.prominentFill)` (white on `accent`, 5.04:1). Increase Contrast picks `AppTheme.highContrast`. A debug `-forceDarkTheme YES` runs the app dark whatever the setting (the dark audit). The widget extension compiles the same palette file.
- Simulator checks without touching device settings: launch with `-UIPreferredContentSizeCategoryName UICTContentSizeCategoryAccessibilityXXXL` (or `...XXXL`, `...L`).
- Tests: `AccessibilityTests` (ScaledFont grows at AX5, AdaptiveStack stacks only at accessibility sizes, ChipFlow keeps a long chip inside the row, 44 pt targets, the WCAG ratio helper, text tokens AA in both themes, the announcement log, and a source lint that fails on a literal point size in any spelling (`.font(.system(size:))`, `Font.system(size:)`, `UIFont.systemFont(ofSize:)`, `.custom(_, size:)` without `relativeTo:`) unless the line draws only an `Image`, a widget symbol or an emoji glyph, or says `fixed size:` and why); `AccessibilityUITests` (Xcode's `performAccessibilityAudit` on feed, post, waves, wallet, notifications, chats, profile, communities, search, settings, login and editor, at the default size and at AX5, and six of them in dark mode; tab labels, traits and size at AX5; the feed card one stop inside the screen at AX5; the German tag hint wraps at AX5; the hidden Feed tab out of the tree). Mutation checked: putting the Feed tab back under other tabs fails the hidden tab test and the audits. The audit reads pixels at frames it took earlier, so it runs only on a still screen (two screenshots 0.5 s apart within 1000 changed pixels, Esie and the status bar left out) and runs again when the screen after it differs from the one before (an image that loaded mid audit moved a card's title); contrast readings on text scrolled out of its scroll view (the AX5 profile summary, capped at 70 % of the screen) are not counted.
- Audit exclusions (`AuditExclusions`, each with its reason, by named identifier only): the status bar and keyboard; "partially unsupported" Dynamic Type (the heuristic flags `@ScaledMetric` sizes and deliberately capped controls; "unsupported" still fails); contrast on disabled controls (WCAG exempts them) and on a listed set of identifiers whose colors compute at or above 4.5:1 but read lower on anti-aliased glyphs; the card summary's five line preview; a paragraph whose link makes it a short hit region; the empty chats search field's text container; on SwiftUI nodes the audit cannot resolve to an element, up to 3 contrast readings per screen (their colors are unit tested), and, for clipping and Dynamic Type issues there, a bounded allowance per screen and description (`unresolvedAllowance`, the counts measured on 2026-10-27: feed 2 clipped and 3 fixed size, feed AX5 1, profile AX5 3, communities AX5 2, settings AX5 3; none elsewhere, the dark run included). It is not identification: the audit names no node and every such issue shares one generic description; the likely sources are unattributable system items (tab bar icons, the navigation bar at AX5) and the feed cards' deliberate five line summaries. They are printed every run and one more than allowed fails.
- A new combined element (one VoiceOver stop over several texts) hides its texts from the audit's contrast check: add its color pairs to `AccessibilityTests.testTextOnOtherBackgroundsMeetsAA`.
- Card and wave footers pick one row or wrapping lines by the text size and width (`AccessibleFooter.wraps`, through `AnyLayout`, so the vote button keeps its state), not by ViewThatFits.
- Not done: long chat channel names can still break mid-word beside the favourite star at AX5; no announcement for messages arriving in an open chat (it would be noisy).

## Icons

One icon list, native pictures per platform. Shared `app/AppIcon.kt`: `AppIcon` names every icon by meaning (`CLOSE`, `VOTE_UP`, `WALLET`), with `isDirectional` for icons that mirror in right to left layouts; models carry `AppIcon`, never a platform or RN icon name (`AppTab.icon`, `SideMenuItem.icon`, `CommunityManageAction.icon`, quest and spend rows, wallet history rows). `native/scripts/icons/icons.tsv` lists every entry with its Material Symbol and SF Symbol names (outlined at rest and the filled form): the Android drawables are generated from it and both maps are tested against it.

- Android: Material Symbols Outlined, weight 400, as vector drawables `res/drawable/ic_<icon>.xml` and `ic_<icon>_fill.xml`, converted from the official SVGs (google/material-design-icons, Apache 2.0, `scripts/icons/NOTICE`) by `scripts/icons/material_symbols.py`; directional ones carry `android:autoMirrored`. `ui/AppIconMap.kt` maps entries to drawables; draw with `Glyph(AppIcon.X, tint, size, filled = active)`.
- iOS: SF Symbols available on iOS 17, mapped in `Screens/AppIconMap.swift` (`icon.symbol(filled:)`); draw with `Image(appIcon: .x, filled:)`, `Label(title, appIcon:)` or `UIImage(appIcon:)`, sized with `.scaledFont(...)` or a text style so they follow Dynamic Type. Directional icons use the backward / forward symbols, which mirror on their own. The widget extension does not link the shared core: `Widgets/WidgetSymbols.swift` holds its four names.
- Outlined at rest, filled for an active, selected or toggled state (the selected tab, a favourite, a done quest, a Pro badge, media controls). Never mix Material Rounded or Sharp in.
- Custom: the Waves mark (`WavesMark`, one shared path drawn by both platforms), the Ecency logo mark (`EcencyLogoMark`: RN's EcencySourceBadge path and view box unchanged, read into cubics in shared code; Android `EcencyLogoMarkVector`, iOS `EcencyLogoMarkShape`), `UpvoteGlyph` and its SwiftUI twin, the PNG brand images. No text or emoji character stands in for an icon.
- Add an icon: add the entry to `AppIcon` and a row to `icons.tsv` (pick the Material Symbol on fonts.google.com/icons, Outlined; the SF Symbol in the SF Symbols app, available in iOS 17), run `python3 scripts/icons/material_symbols.py` from `native/`, add the drawable lines to `ui/AppIconMap.kt` and the symbol lines to `AppIconMap.swift`. An icon-only control takes its accessibility label from its action, not the icon name.
- Tests: shared `AppIconTest`, `AppIconListTest` (the list is exactly the enum); Android `AppIconTest` (every entry resolves to a 24 dp vector, filled where the list says, mirroring as declared) and `AppIconSourceTest` (no icon drawn outside the maps on either platform, no text or emoji icons, widget names listed, every SF Symbol exists on iOS 17 per the Mac's symbol catalog); iOS `AppIconTests` (every symbol draws, outlined and filled).
- Debug gallery of every icon: Android `--ez iconGallery true`, iOS `-iconGallery YES`.

## Never commit

Signing keys, keystores, provisioning profiles, `google-services.json`, `GoogleService-Info.plist`, `.env` files, API tokens, DSNs or any real account keys. `native/.gitignore` blocks the common ones; review every diff anyway, this repo is public.

## Media picking (images and videos)

One layer for the only two upload kinds, used at every surface (editor uploads and its gallery, waves composer, chat attachment, video upload and its thumbnail, profile avatar and cover). A deliberate improvement over RN, whose picker options were a compromise (RN #3564, #3561).
- Shared (`app/MediaPicking.kt`): `MediaSurface` (kind and how many at once per surface) and `MediaTypes`, the one allow-list: JPEG, PNG, GIF, WebP, HEIC/HEIF images; MP4 and MOV videos. Anything else is refused with a message naming what is accepted. Images keep the existing preparation (upright, at most 1920 px, every metadata block dropped, GPS included; HEIC and WebP become JPEG; an animated GIF goes as it is and must fit the 30 MB upload limit). Videos have no size limit of their own (tus to 3Speak, as before).
- Android (`MediaPicker.kt`): "Choose from Library" is the system photo picker where the platform has it (SDK 33+, or 30 to 32 with R extension 2), else `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT` with `EXTRA_MIME_TYPES`; "Choose from Files" is always `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT`. Not `PickVisualMedia.isPhotoPickerAvailable`: it also counts the Play services backport, which fails on Android 10 and older and on Go devices. No storage permission. Images are read at once into memory by the preparation; a picked video is copied into the app's cache before upload, so a withdrawn grant cannot break a running upload.
- iOS (`App/MediaPicking.swift`): PHPicker (no library permission) asks the item provider for an allowed type only and reads the current representation (our pipeline converts HEIC); an iCloud-only item shows its download with a cancel (`MediaLoadingOverlay`) and a failure message. "Choose from Files" is `fileImporter` limited to the same types, one importer per screen. Camera capture stays its own action.
- Tests: shared `MediaPickingTest`; Android `MediaPickerTest` (picker choice, document picker intent, filtering, video copy); iOS `MediaPickingTests` (types, refusals, HEIC to JPEG without location) plus the existing metadata stripping tests.

## Known RN bugs fixed in native

- #3535 signing: an `account_update` or `account_update2` that sets `owner` is an owner operation (never signed, refused on links and in the dApp bridge); a `custom_json` with both authority lists is refused as unsupported (one key signs); the HiveAuth key type follows the operation's fields.
- #3600 key entry: a pasted public key and a private key of another role get their own messages.
- #3599: "Reported" only when the report went through; AI Recap is copy only; numeric avatar sizes (128, 256, 512); bare `/created` app link; "Communities" search tab; the Manage Authorities button reads "Open".
- #3460: the translate `/detect` call only for a signed-in reader.
- #3586: an API-flagged wave under "Always warn" stays listed with readable text and covered media, revealed per wave; "Always hide" leaves it out. Its thread screen keeps the cover when it was opened from a list this session. Known gap: a wave opened by a link was never listed and the post API carries no flag, so it opens uncovered, as every post screen does (NSFW covers live on list cards).
- #3527: publishing waits for a draft save in flight.
- #1019: avatars that are buttons on their own have screen reader labels.
- #3579: link callbacks pass an allow-list (`https://host` or an app's own `scheme://`), system and browser schemes refused. Deliberate losses: `http://localhost` development callbacks and authority-less app callbacks (`app:///path`) are refused too.
- #3564, #3561: the media picking layer above.
- #2627: a cold start link under the welcome screen opens after the terms are accepted (iOS queued it; Android kept it).
- #2672: the server picker marks the saved server (iOS had no mark; Android compared exact strings).
